- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-0-contracts.md`
  summary: Phase 1 CI must include the Phase 0 freeze gate — isolation compile of pweb.rpc.intf.pas with zero unit paths, compile of all three contract units, the banned-identifier grep sweep, and the blob-interface-absence check.
  evidence: Verification-gap review confirmed the freeze invariants (RTL-only isolation, frozen wire tables, dependency bans) are currently verified only by one-shot manual runs; nothing committed re-verifies them. CI is ratified to start at Phase 1 (phase-plan.md), so the committed re-runnable gate lands there rather than adding Phase 0 tooling outside the frozen layout.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-1-cap1-webview-binding.md`
  summary: Upstream's cmake fetches the WebView2 SDK nuget by version with no URL_HASH, leaving an unverified network input to the DLL build; evaluate vendoring or hash-pinning at the next pin review.
  evidence: cmake/webview.cmake:3 at pinned commit cbbdee44 pins version 1.0.1150.38 but relies solely on nuget version immutability for integrity.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-4-cap4-asset-system.md`
  summary: TZipAssetStore case-collision rejection folds ASCII only; Unicode case pairs (e-acute vs E-acute) are not detected as colliding archive entries.
  evidence: NTFS treats non-ASCII case pairs as equal per its $UpCase table, so such an archive is ambiguous against a folder store, but a faithful fold needs a ratified Unicode-folding decision (ties into CAP-6 bundler validation) rather than an ad-hoc table.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-5-cap5-frontend-sdks.md`
  summary: CI downloads the pinned pas2js archive from getpas2js.freepascal.org on every run with no cache; an upstream outage fails the pipeline even though the pin is sha256-verified.
  evidence: Review of tools/get-pas2js.ps1 + ci.yml — availability (not integrity) depends on the upstream host per run; an actions/cache keyed on the lock sha256 would remove the dependency without weakening the pin.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6-cap6-release-bundle.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the CAP-4 Unicode case-fold finding above (spec-phase-4, TZipAssetStore ASCII-only fold) is closed by the CAP-6 D1 ratification (2026-08-12): collision rejection now uses the pinned mORMot Unicode 10.0 UpperCaseReference fold, enforced at bundle construction and at TZipAssetStore construction.
  evidence: src/assets/pweb.assets.zip.pas construction fold upgraded to UpperCaseReference; src/assets/pweb.assets.bundle.pas enforces the same fold pre-write; mandated D1 tests in test/assets/pweb.test.bundle.pas (fold-equal reject, non-fold-equal accept, machine-independent verdicts, crafted-ZIP bypass rejected by the store).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6-cap6-release-bundle.md`
  summary: The pwebbundle CLI directory walk uses unprefixed FindFirstFileW (MAX_PATH bound) and reads file contents through the Ansi TFileName layer, so dist trees deeper than MAX_PATH or names not representable in the process ACP fail the build loudly; revisit with proper wide/long-path I/O at CAP-7 cross-platform.
  evidence: Review of tools/bundler/pwebbundle.pas — enumeration is wide but unprefixed, content reads go through Utf8ToString+StringFromFile; failures are loud (build error), never silent corruption, so v1 Windows-first scope tolerates it.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b0-webview2-runtime-foundation.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the signer-pin finding above is closed by the CAP-6b1 ratification (2026-08-13): the lock schema gained the optional `authenticode-subject` key (populated on the first real pin, evergreen-bootstrapper), tools/get-webview2-runtime.ps1 enforces it at fetch time AFTER the sha256 pass, and the setup helper re-verifies the extracted payload natively via WinVerifyTrust + exact leaf-subject match before execution.
  evidence: webview2-runtime.lock `authenticode-subject` entry; tools/get-webview2-runtime.ps1 fetch-verify authenticode leg; src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.provision.pas PWebWv2AuthenticodeCheck; fixture cases in test/cap6b/check_wv2lock.ps1 (subject parse, unsigned refusal, wrong-subject refusal on a really-signed binary).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b1-normal-evergreen-installer.md`
  summary: PWebWv2FileSha256 hashes the whole payload in memory via StringFromFile, which is fine for the ~1.7 MB bootstrapper but should become a streamed digest before CAP-6b2 verifies the ~150 MB Standalone Evergreen Installer through the same primitive.
  evidence: Adversarial review of src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.provision.pas — the unit is the natural verify path for the next shard's much larger artifact; an in-memory read of 150 MB is wasteful and failure-prone on constrained machines.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b1-normal-evergreen-installer.md`
  summary: Every Microsoft rotation of the Evergreen Bootstrapper behind the mutable fwlink turns CI red until a human re-ratifies the lock (by ratified design); consider caching the verified artifact keyed on its lock sha256, and document the expected breakage cadence, when CI availability becomes a pain point.
  evidence: Adversarial review of test/cap6b1/build_normal_setup.ps1 and the CI CAP-6b1 block — the build-fails-on-drift rule is the ratified CAP-6b1 pin semantics, but the repo already records the analogous no-cache availability concern for pas2js fetches.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b1-normal-evergreen-installer.md`

  evidence: Adversarial review of tools/setup/normal.iss — an explicit MinVersion would fail earlier with a clearer message, but the supported-OS floor for PWeb apps has never been ratified anywhere in the spec set.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b2-offline-evergreen-installer.md`

  evidence: src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.provision.pas streamed PWebWv2FileSha256; test/platform/pweb.test.wv2provision.pas StreamingDigest (multi-chunk fixture vs one-shot digest, FIPS vector, empty/missing-file fail-closed); test/cap6b2/run_offline_setup_gates.ps1 S1/V1 (streamed accept of the real standalone, digest == Get-FileHash == lock pin).

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: OUTSTANDING — the CAP-6b3 fixed-runtime clean-machine VM gate (test/cap6b3/run_fixed_clean_machine_gate.ps1) has no passing transcript. It refuses to run under CI by design and is the profile's ONLY proof of matrix rows F2 (no Evergreen present: the fixed app still runs) and the runtime-absent half of F12 (a broken bundled tree fails closed with no Evergreen to fall back to). Every automated CAP-6b3 gate runs on a host that HAS a usable Evergreen runtime, which makes the no-fallback legs meaningful but can never demonstrate operation without one.
  evidence: git grep finds CAP6B3_CLEAN_MACHINE_PASS only inside test/cap6b3/run_fixed_clean_machine_gate.ps1; the CI block deliberately omits it (the script throws under $env:CI/$env:GITHUB_ACTIONS).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: All three install profiles (normal, offline, fixed) share one AppId `{7C3E9A1B-5D24-4F68-A0C9-2B8E6D4F1A57}` and one `{localappdata}\Programs\PWebRelease` directory by ratified anti-fork design, so installing the normal or offline profile OVER a fixed install overwrites the release triple with an Evergreen-mode binary while orphaning the ~690 MB `runtime\webview2` tree — and nothing detects the mixed state: the Evergreen-mode app never looks at the tree, and the uninstaller of the newer install removes only what it recorded. Profile switching needs an explicit ratified story (detect-and-clean at install time, or per-profile AppIds, which would cost the shared identity).
  evidence: tools/setup/pwebappsetup.issi authors one AppId for all profiles; test/cap6b3/run_fixed_setup_gates.ps1 gate 4 asserts the exact installed set only for a clean fixed install; no gate installs one profile over another.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: All three profiles share the same WebView2 user-data folder (`%APPDATA%\releaseapp.exe`, the default derived from the executable name), so a profile folder written by a NEWER Evergreen runtime can make `webview_create` fail against the older pinned fixed runtime, and no uninstaller ever removes it. A fixed-runtime deployment that wants true isolation needs its own user-data folder (an explicit CreateCoreWebView2EnvironmentWithOptions userDataFolder, which is CAP-4W patch territory) plus uninstall-time cleanup.
  evidence: The CAP-4W probe runner (build/cap6b3/probe/run_probe.ps1) deletes `$env:APPDATA\fxprobe.exe` between modes precisely because a stale profile perturbs the result; nothing in tools/setup/*.iss removes the folder at uninstall.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.fixed.pas composes every path with plain concatenation and no `\\?\` long-path prefix, so a deep install location plus the deepest members of the runtime subtree (`…\runtime\webview2\Microsoft.WebView2.FixedVersionRuntime.<version>.x64\EBWebView\x64\…`) can exceed MAX_PATH and fail loudly at validation or during the manifest walk. Same class as the recorded pwebbundle limitation; revisit with proper long-path I/O at CAP-7 cross-platform.
  evidence: The unit's PWebWv2FixedPathShapeOk deliberately REFUSES the `\\?\` form (it is indistinguishable from a device path at the shape level), and the manifest walk uses unprefixed FindFirstFileW — mirroring the existing tools/bundler/pwebbundle.pas entry above.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: PWebWv2FixedAclVerify now REFUSES any ACE whose type is neither ACCESS_ALLOWED nor ACCESS_DENIED (adversarial-review finding: a callback/object ACE carries no SID at the offset this walk reads, so its grant would be invisible and skipping it would be a hole). That is the correct fail-closed choice, but it means a managed image that adds conditional/callback ACEs (ACCESS_ALLOWED_CALLBACK_ACE and friends, as some AppLocker/central-access-policy deployments do) to `%LOCALAPPDATA%` would make every fixed-profile startup refuse. Parsing those ACE forms properly, or narrowing the refusal to callback ACEs that actually grant write, needs a ratified decision about which access-control shapes the profile supports.
  evidence: src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.fixed.pas PWebWv2FixedAclVerify unhandled-ace-type branch; no test fixture creates a callback ACE because SDDL conditional ACEs would need a supported-shape decision first.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: `OutputBaseFilename=setup` in the shared identity include makes every profile's `setup.exe` subject to the Windows application-compatibility shim, which loads `version.dll` (and other DLLs) unsafely from the setup binary's own directory — a DLL-planting surface on any machine where the user runs setup.exe from a writable/shared folder. Inno Setup 6.7.3 emits this warning on every compile of all three profiles. Renaming the output (e.g. `pwebsetup`) is a one-line ratified change but alters the artifact name every gate and doc references.
  evidence: ISCC compile listing for all three profiles: "Warning: Setting the [Setup] section directive \"OutputBaseFileName\" to \"setup\" is not recommended: all executables named \"setup.exe\" are shimmed by Windows application compatibility to load additional DLLs, such as version.dll."
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md`
  summary: WAIVED — CAP-6b3 real runtime Gate B (a disposable Windows instance with NO Evergreen runtime) and Gate C (a Windows 10 instance proving the AppContainer requirement Microsoft documents for Fixed Version >= 120 on unpackaged Win32 hosts) are both waived by explicit user decision on 2026-08-14, superseding the OUTSTANDING record above for this shard. This closes CAP-6b3 acceptance item 20; item 11 keeps its implemented-and-proven half (grant applied and re-read BY SID, non-elevated, on Windows 11) and rests on this waiver only for the Windows 10 environment itself.
  evidence: No CAP6B3_CLEAN_MACHINE_PASS transcript exists — git grep finds the marker only inside test/cap6b3/run_fixed_clean_machine_gate.ps1, which refuses to run under CI by design, and no Windows 10 host was available. What therefore stays unproven on real hardware is exactly two things: the runtime-absent case (matrix row F2, and the runtime-absent half of F12), and Windows-10-specific AppContainer enforcement. Everything else those gates would have covered is proven on this host with a usable Evergreen 151.0.4129.78 installed: gate 7 (bundled runtime selected, OBSERVED pin, browser image inside the bundled tree, 42), gate 8 (broken tree fails closed twice, no fallback) and gate 10 (failed gate leaves no app, no tree, no uninstall key, setup exit 5).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the shared-AppId profile-switch finding above (three profiles, one AppId, one install dir, nothing recording WHICH profile is installed and an orphaned ~690 MB `runtime\webview2` tree after an Evergreen install over a fixed one) is closed by CAP-6b4. One product identity is ratified unchanged (same AppId, same `{localappdata}\Programs\PWebRelease`, `PrivilegesRequired=lowest`), profiles become mutually exclusive MODES, and the mode is recorded by a deterministic HKCU marker — `HKCU\Software\PWeb\PWebRelease` value `Profile` ∈ `normal | offline | fixed-runtime` — written by Inno's own `[Registry]` phase, which runs after every `[Files]` entry and is therefore the COMMIT POINT of a switch. `PWEB_PROFILE` became required and is validated case-sensitively at preprocess time (no silent default). The orphan is reclaimed by Inno's own `[InstallDelete]` in `pwebprovgate.issi`, which runs strictly after the frozen `PrepareToInstall` Evergreen gate, so a refused `fixed → Evergreen` switch touches zero files. No `DelTree`, no `RegDeleteKey`, no hand-rolled cleanup was added anywhere.
  evidence: tools/setup/pwebappsetup.issi (`PWEB_PROFILE` require+`Pos()` validate, `PWEB_RUNTIME_SUBDIR`, the `[Registry]` marker with `uninsdeletevalue`/`uninsdeletekeyifempty`); tools/setup/pwebprovgate.issi `[InstallDelete] Type: filesandordirs; Name: "{app}\{#PWEB_RUNTIME_SUBDIR}"`; test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1 contracts (a)/(c); test/cap6b4/run_profile_matrix.ps1 rows I1–I3, S1–S6, F1–F4, U1–U3.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the `OutputBaseFilename=setup` appcompat finding above is closed by CAP-6b4. `PWEB_SETUP_BASENAME` is required by the shared identity include with no default, authored once per profile manifest, and parsed by each build script instead of being passed with `/F`, so the artifacts are now `PWebRelease-Normal-Setup.exe`, `PWebRelease-Offline-Setup.exe` and `PWebRelease-FixedRuntime-Setup.exe`. `_bmad-output/specs/spec-pweb/deployment.md` was verified NOT to freeze the old basename (its only `setup.exe` occurrence is illustrative, inside an ASCII flow diagram). A contract sweep forbids any committed reference to a shipped `dist/windows/*/setup.exe` and any `/F` override of a shipped artifact (only the TEST-ONLY `/Fabortprobe` remains permitted).
  evidence: tools/setup/{normal,offline,fixed}.iss `PWEB_SETUP_BASENAME` defines; tools/setup/pwebappsetup.issi `OutputBaseFilename={#PWEB_SETUP_BASENAME}`; test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1 contract (b); ISCC 6.7.3 compile listings for all three profiles no longer emit the "shimmed by Windows application compatibility" warning.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: RATIFIED USER-DATA POLICY — the shared WebView2 user-data folder finding above (`%APPDATA%\releaseapp.exe`, the default derived from the executable name, shared by all three modes) is answered for CAP-6b4 by an explicit policy rather than by a redesign: user data is SHARED across profiles and PRESERVED by uninstall. No setup manifest authors an `[UninstallDelete]` section, the `[InstallDelete]` reclaim targets only `{app}\runtime\webview2` and never `{app}` or any user path, and no uninstaller touches the folder. The underlying compatibility concern (a profile folder written by a NEWER Evergreen runtime can make `webview_create` fail against the older pinned fixed runtime) is NOT closed by this policy and remains open above — a per-profile `userDataFolder` is CAP-4W patch territory and was explicitly out of scope here.
  evidence: test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1 contract (f) (no `[UninstallDelete]`, no user-data target in any of the six setup manifests, no recursive user-data deletion in any gate script); test/cap6b4/run_profile_matrix.ps1 writes a sentinel into the folder before the chain and asserts it survives all three uninstalls (rows U1/U2/U3) byte-identical.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: MEASURED RESIDUAL, recorded rather than hidden — on a `fixed → normal/offline` switch the runtime tree is reclaimed by `[InstallDelete]` BEFORE the marker commits, so a failure inside the following three-file copy would leave a stale `fixed-runtime` marker on an Evergreen-mode install. It cannot produce the dangerous class (a fixed-mode binary with no runtime tree): the runtime proven usable in `PrepareToInstall` is Evergreen and the binary being installed is the Evergreen-mode one. Inno offers no post-commit deletion seam, and hand-rolled cleanup is exactly what CAP-6b3 measured to be harmful (an incidental EFileError from racing the installer). Revisit only if Inno gains a post-`[Registry]` failure hook.
  evidence: Inno's documented installation order (PrepareToInstall → `[InstallDelete]` → `[Files]` → `[Registry]`), re-measured with the pinned ISCC 6.7.3; the opposite direction has no such residual because the fixed profile's verdict gate precedes its shared-triple `[Files]` entries (test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1 contract (d), matrix row F3).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: OUTSTANDING (pre-existing, surfaced again by CAP-6b4 local verification) — Microsoft rotated the Evergreen Bootstrapper behind the mutable fwlink again, so `tools/get-webview2-runtime.ps1 -Artifact evergreen-bootstrapper` now fetches sha256 `4f29c9366bbcf7902a3fe9d87373b4b270fe176b3645e15f9bbf8048a5e07a7e` against the ratified pin `8c4a8054…`. `test/cap6b1/build_normal_setup.ps1` re-fetches unconditionally (unlike the CAP-6b2/6b3 builds, which reuse a local copy after re-verifying it), so the normal-profile build — and therefore the whole CAP-6b1 CI block — is red until a human re-ratifies the pin. This is the ratified drift behaviour, already recorded above; CAP-6b4 neither caused nor changed it, and deliberately did not touch the pin or the CAP-6b1 acquisition semantics.
  evidence: `pwsh -File test/cap6b1/build_normal_setup.ps1` on 2026-08-14 fails at the fetch-verify step with the digest above; the previously verified `deps/webview2-runtime/MicrosoftEdgeWebview2Setup.exe` still matches the ratified pin, so CAP-6b4's local verification of the normal profile was performed by compiling `tools/setup/normal.iss` directly against that lock-verified staged payload.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the Evergreen Bootstrapper upstream-drift finding recorded immediately above (OUTSTANDING, 2026-08-14) is closed by an explicit human re-ratification of the pin on the same day: `webview2-runtime.lock` artifact `evergreen-bootstrapper` now carries `size = 1708760` and `sha256 = 4f29c9366bbcf7902a3fe9d87373b4b270fe176b3645e15f9bbf8048a5e07a7e`, with a dated CAP-6b4 re-ratification comment. `tools/get-webview2-runtime.ps1` fetch-verify, `-Validate` and the CAP-6b0 lock fixture matrix all pass against it, so `test/cap6b1/build_normal_setup.ps1` runs end to end again and the CAP-6b1 CI block is no longer blocked. Nothing about the ratified drift SEMANTICS changed: a future rotation will fail the build exactly the same way, by design.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — fixed → fixed version-bump orphan. The CAP-6b4 stale-tree reclaim lives in `tools/setup/pwebprovgate.issi`, so it runs only when an EVERGREEN profile is installed over a fixed one; `tools/setup/fixed.iss` has no reclaim of its own. Installing a pin-bumped Fixed Runtime over an older fixed install would therefore leave the previous `Microsoft.WebView2.FixedVersionRuntime.<old>.x64` tree (~690 MB) stranded inside `{app}\runtime\webview2` beside the new one, and no gate would see it: the CAP-6b3 exact-set layout proof and the CAP-6b4 matrix both compare against the tree the CURRENT build staged. It is unreachable today — exactly one Fixed Runtime version is pinned, and the ratified matrix is profile-to-profile, never version-to-version — but a pin bump makes it real, and the fix (a same-shaped `[InstallDelete]` in the fixed profile, gated on the same ownership check) should land in the same change as the first bump.
  evidence: `tools/setup/fixed.iss` authors no `[InstallDelete]` section; the reclaim entries exist only in `tools/setup/pwebprovgate.issi`, which `fixed.iss` deliberately does not include (asserted by the CAP-6b3 no-provisioning-path sweep in `test/cap6b/check_wv2min.ps1`).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — the ratified CAP-6b1/6b2/6b3 specs still name `dist/windows/*/setup.exe` as the artifact each shard builds, which CAP-6b4 renamed. Those documents are FROZEN ratified artifacts, so they are deliberately left alone, and the CAP-6b4 no-`setup.exe` contract sweep is scoped to `test/`, `tools/` and `.github/` and deliberately does not scan `_bmad-output/`. The consequence is bounded and documented rather than hidden: a reader of a frozen spec will see the pre-rename artifact name, and the authoritative current name is the `PWEB_SETUP_BASENAME` define in each profile manifest.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b1-normal-evergreen-installer.md:77,107`, `spec-phase-6b2-offline-evergreen-installer.md:80`, `spec-phase-6b3-fixed-runtime-profile.md:17,85`; the sweep's directory scope is the `$shimHits` Get-ChildItem in `test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1` contract (b).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6b4-windows-profile-integration.md`
  summary: RESOLVED (structurally, superseding the same-day re-ratification record above) — the recurring Evergreen upstream-drift breakage is closed by pinning the resolved per-build delivery-CDN url instead of the `go.microsoft.com/fwlink` redirector. The redirector is a "latest" pointer by construction, so a byte-exact sha256 could never stay valid against it: Microsoft shipped THREE distinct bootstrappers in three days (8c4a8054 -> 4f29c936 -> be695eb3) and the standalone drifted too (f8d4ab07 -> 6ac57a21), turning hosted CI red within a day of each ratification. Both Evergreen entries now pin the `msedge.sf.dl.delivery.mp.microsoft.com/filestreamingservice/files/<GUID>/<file>` url the fwlink resolved to on 2026-08-15 — the SAME immutable-url shape the CAP-6b3 Fixed Runtime entry has always used, and that is the only artifact in this lock that never drifted once. The fwlink remains the documented discovery mechanism for finding the next build's url via `-Refresh`; it is simply no longer what a digest is pinned against. Verification semantics are untouched: sha256, then size, then Authenticode `Valid` with the exact leaf subject, delete on any miss.
  evidence: `Invoke-WebRequest -Method Head -MaximumRedirection 0` against both lock urls returns 301 to the pinned CDN targets; three consecutive dev-host fetches returned the same digest the hosted runner reported in run 31875019344, ruling out per-network CDN variance; after the re-pin `tools/get-webview2-runtime.ps1 -Validate` plus both `-Artifact` fetches verify, `test/cap6b/check_wv2lock.ps1` reports `CAP6B0_WV2LOCK_PASS cases=63`, and the rebuilt normal/offline profiles pass their own gate suites and the full CAP-6b4 matrix.

Asserting consistency between the three derived names and the header version, rather than the literals, keeps the protection while making a version bump legible. Left alone here because changing it on the macOS side only would break the parity with the Linux sibling that the CAP-7M0 review explicitly credited.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m0-macos-feasibility.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (pre-existing, surfaced by the CAP-7M0 human review) — the CAP-7L shell scripts open by deleting a directory tree with unguarded `rm -rf -- "${var}"`. Nothing validates the target first, so an empty variable turns `rm -rf -- "${work}/abi"` into `rm -rf -- /abi`. They are not wired to misfire today (`set -euo pipefail` plus `repo_root="$(cd -- … && pwd)"` aborts the assignment if the cd fails), but that is an argument that an accident is unlikely rather than impossible, in scripts CI executes as programs. CAP-7M0 replaced the pattern rather than merely guarding it, and the REPLACEMENT is what should be lifted here: the default path deletes NOTHING — a working directory that is absent is created, one that is empty is used, and one that is NON-EMPTY is a loud refusal naming the directory and telling the caller to pass `--clean` or remove it. `--clean` is opt-in per invocation and is the only route to a deletion, which then goes through `cap7m_rm_tree` (refuses empty target, `/`, a `..` path component, an unresolvable parent, an unusable basename, anything outside the allowed root, and the root itself; all comparisons on `pwd -P` output; literal unglobbed prefix strip; `${2-…}` so an explicitly empty allowed root dies instead of silently defaulting). The wipe bought nothing on CI, which checks out fresh; it only ever served repeated local runs, and it paid for that with a recursive delete on the default path.
  evidence: seven unguarded deletion sites — `tools/build-webview-so.sh:107` and `:163`; `test/cap7l/build_cap7l.sh:47`; `test/cap7l/check_abi.sh:60`; `test/cap7l/run_cap7l_gates.sh:72`; `test/cap7l/run_gui_matrix.sh:94`; `test/cap7l/run_release_layout.sh:52`. The pattern to lift is `cap7m_prepare_dir` + `cap7m_rm_tree` in `test/cap7m/cap7m_common.sh`, duplicated self-contained into `tools/build-webview-dylib.sh` so a build tool does not depend on the test tree.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m0-macos-feasibility.md`
  summary: RESOLVED 2026-08-16 (recorded because the failure mode generalises, not because work remains) — the `windows:` job was red for an entire session and no PWeb step ran, because `gcarreno/setup-lazarus` fetches a package list from `packages.lazarus-ide.org` even when ZERO packages are requested. That host went down: DNS resolved fine (37.97.187.115 / 2a01:7c8:aac1:2f0::1, via both the local resolver and 1.1.1.1) but TCP connect failed — curl exit 7, not 6 — for the entire site including `www`, for third parties as well as for us, and the runners got ECONNREFUSED on the same address. The installer itself was reachable on SourceForge throughout; the action was successfully downloading it. So the dependency that broke the gate was the package list, not the toolchain. Replaced with a pinned fetch+install (`tools/get-fpc-windows.ps1`, `fpc.lock` `windows-*` keys) mirroring the macOS path: sha256 AND size verified before use, MZ-shape check to reject a SourceForge interstitial, silent Inno Setup install, then assertions that `fpc.exe` exists, reports exactly the locked version, and targets win64/x86_64. Same Lazarus 3.4 bundle, same FPC 3.2.2, so `Assert FPC 3.2.2` and the other 77 steps are byte-untouched. THE GENERAL LESSON, which is why this is ledgered: an unpinned intermediary is an AVAILABILITY dependency as much as a supply-chain one, and every remaining third-party action in `ci.yml` (checkout, setup-node, upload-artifact, cache, msvc-dev-cmd) can hold a gate hostage the same way. They are pinned to commit SHAs, which fixes what they DO but not whether their hosts answer.
  evidence: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the replaced step was `Install FPC (Lazarus 3.4 ships FPC 3.2.2)` using `gcarreno/setup-lazarus@d633fa79b45e29794ab46ec31f7543e44e02efef`; the installer URL is the one the action's own log showed it fetching. Pin measured on 2026-08-16: sha256 `6ede48350b9890929088bd33aa441e1bf4cf813b21bc38149e3df3bbe673bbd3`, size `213944118`, equal to the server's advertised Content-Length.

They are in `src/platform/macos/pweb_cocoa_bridge.mm` because the task state machine and `task_info()` are Objective-C facts Pascal cannot express, and the alternative — proving the claim-once guards only in a GUI run — would make them provable solely by races that a synchronous main-thread handler makes structurally absent. The cost is real and is recorded rather than hidden: a few hundred bytes of unreachable-from-a-page surface ship in every macOS binary, and a reader auditing the bridge meets test scaffolding beside production code. A separate `pweb_cocoa_bridge_testing.mm` linked only by the gates would remove it, at the price of the state machine no longer being THE one the tests drive.

The state machine was built for that day; what does NOT yet exist is any gate that exercises a deferred delivery, and the runtime leg records `stop_arrivals=0` as a LIMITATION rather than pretending otherwise. Designing chunked delivery was explicitly out of scope for CAP-7M1.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`

It is NOT a claim that a hostile `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` cannot substitute a different `libwebview.0.12.dylib` at load time: on an ad-hoc-signed Mach-O the loader honours it. Closure is hardened runtime plus library validation, applied at signing time — and signing, notarization and `.app` packaging are all explicitly banned from this shard, so the mitigation cannot land here. Any future release-layout shard inherits this as a requirement, not as an option.
  evidence: `test/cap7m/check_release_layout.sh` strips the three hints in both the positive and the negative half and asserts only resolution and a named failure; nothing in the tree passes `-o runtime` or a `com.apple.security.cs.*` entitlement, because nothing in the tree signs.

CAP-7M0 measured 8 on x86_64 and 0 on arm64 and recorded them as `CAP7M_EXPORTS … other=N`; a patched upstream that added a C++ class with RTTI would add more `_ZTI`/`_ZTS` records and pass silently, because "matches the RTTI shape" says nothing about count. Pinning `other_count` per architecture in `webview.lock` would close it, and is a lock-schema addition CAP-7M1 deliberately did not make while its own new gates were unproven on a runner. The same gap does not exist for the bridge object, whose CAP-7M1 gate asserts the TEXT symbols exactly and records the data ones.
  evidence: `test/cap7m/check_webview_exports.sh` `other_count` is computed and recorded but never compared; `docs/wkwebview-macos-semantics.md` constraint 8 records `arm64 17 / x86_64 25` as a measurement rather than as a pin.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (a scope statement, not a bug) — a HARD LINK inside the asset root pointing at an inode outside it is served, on Linux and on Darwin alike, and no gate exercises it. The confinement model is "no SYMLINK on the resolved chain, and the opened descriptor's kernel path equals the expected path" — and a hard link satisfies both by construction: it is not a symlink, `lstat` reports a regular file, and `F_GETPATH` (like `/proc/self/fd`) returns the path it was opened by, which IS the in-root path. Neither `readlink` nor `F_GETPATH` closes this, so `F_GETPATH` must not be read as if it did. Closing it needs a different invariant (same-device + inode-set membership, or an `openat`-relative walk from a root descriptor), which is a change to CAP-7L's ratified algorithm rather than a macOS body for it. Stated in constraint 13 so a reader does not infer more from the re-proof than it claims.
  evidence: `src/assets/pweb.assets.folder.pas` `ResolveSegment` tests `fpS_ISLNK` and `fpS_ISREG` only; `ReadWholeFile` compares `FinalPathOfFd(fd)` against the walked path, which a hard link matches exactly; `test/cap7l/run_cap7l_gates.sh` and `test/cap7m/run_cap7m_gates.sh` both build symlink fixtures and neither builds a hard link.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — Darwin's `F_GETPATH` is measurably MORE PERMISSIVE than Linux's `/proc/self/fd` for an unlinked file, and no gate exercises the difference. Linux reads back `<path> (deleted)`, which fails the byte-exact re-proof and therefore fails closed; Darwin returns the last known path with no marker, so a file unlinked between the walk and the read still satisfies the re-proof and is served. It is not an escape — the descriptor was opened `O_NOFOLLOW` at a walked-and-`lstat`'d path inside the root, so the bytes served are the bytes that were confined — but it IS a behavioural divergence between two platforms the project claims parity for, and it is currently recorded in prose only. A fixture that unlinks an asset mid-read would turn it into a measured fact on both platforms; building one deterministically needs a seam the store does not have.
  evidence: `src/assets/pweb.assets.folder.pas` Darwin `PWebDarwinFinalPathOfFd` versus the `/proc/self/fd` branch and its `'<path> (deleted)'` comment; `docs/wkwebview-macos-semantics.md` constraint 13 records the divergence; no gate in `test/cap7l` or `test/cap7m` unlinks a file between the walk and the read.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — promote `TCocoaAssetHandler.Attach`'s per-view read-back from RECORDED to GATED once a hosted run has reported `readback=ours` on both architectures. `pweb_cocoa_handler_installed_on` is three-valued: `ours` is the per-view proof, `foreign` (another handler owning `pweb://` on this view) already refuses inside `Attach`, and `absent` is deliberately NOT a refusal because it cannot distinguish "nothing is installed" from "the configuration copy this `WKWebView` hands back does not carry the scheme-handler map". CAP-7M0 measured only that WRITING to that copy is silently ineffective (`postcreate_hits=0`), which is a statement about mutation; whether a READ sees a registration the original was constructed with has never been measured here. Gating `absent` on that unverified assumption would report a correct adapter as broken and cost a macOS run at 10x billing, so the first run MEASURES it via `CAP7M1_READBACK`. If both arches report `ours`, change the `case` in `assert_run` to refuse anything else and make `Attach` raise on `pcrAbsent`; if either reports `absent`, the per-view claim is not obtainable this way and the seam counter plus the served main document remain the whole proof.
  evidence: `src/platform/macos/pweb_cocoa_bridge.mm` `pweb_cocoa_handler_installed_on` returns `PWEB_COCOA_READBACK_OURS|ABSENT|FOREIGN`; `src/platform/macos/pweb.platform.cocoa.pas` `TCocoaAssetHandler.Attach` raises only on `pcrForeign` and stores the verdict in the `Readback` property; `test/cap7m/run_cap7m_runtime.sh` `assert_run` records `CAP7M1_READBACK store=… observed=…` and dies only on `foreign`. CAP-7M0 run `31912129480` recorded `CAP7M_M9 postcreate_install_accepted=1 postcreate_hits=0` with `configuration_is_same_object` observed as `1,1,0` across three cycles — pointer identity there is already known to be unreliable.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — add a bash-3.2 portability scan to the macOS gate suite. macOS ships bash 3.2 as `/bin/bash`, where `local a="$1" b="${a}x"` does NOT work: every word of the command is expanded BEFORE any assignment happens, so `${a}` is still unset and `set -u` turns it into `a: unbound variable`. Newer bash assigns left to right and hides the defect entirely, so it cannot be caught on a Linux or Windows dev host, and `bash -n` does not catch it either — it is a runtime expansion error, not a syntax error. One instance shipped (`run_cap7m_runtime.sh` `assert_uri_leak`) and was invisible until the runtime harness stopped crashing upstream of it, costing a hosted run to find. The repo already turns defect classes into gates (the no-inline-macOS-flag scan in `run_cap7m_gates.sh` is the model); a companion scan for self-referencing `local` lines belongs beside it. It was deliberately NOT added in the same change as the fix, because `run_cap7m_gates.sh` runs before every other macOS gate and a buggy scanner there blocks the whole job.
  evidence: `test/cap7m/run_cap7m_runtime.sh:261` before the fix — `local label="$1" log="$2" observed="${logs}/${label}-observed.tsv"`, which failed on both macOS jobs of run 31952514083 with `line 261: label: unbound variable`; `test/cap7m/cap7m_common.sh` already records the sibling bash 3.2 trap (`"${arr[@]}"` under `set -u` on an empty array) as the reason `CAP7M_FPC_ARCH_LINK_FLAGS` is a string rather than an array.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7m1-macos-platform-adapter.md`
  summary: RESOLVED — the `Attach` per-view read-back promotion recorded above is taken. The ledgered condition was "promote once a hosted run has reported `readback=ours` on both architectures"; run `31952514083` reported `CAP7M1_SEAM cycle=1..3 seam_ran=1 seam_delta=1 readback=ours` on `macos-x64` AND `macos-arm64`. So the configuration a `WKWebView` hands back DOES carry the scheme-handler registration the original was constructed with — a fact CAP-7M0 could not establish, because it measured only that WRITING to that copy is silently ineffective. `TCocoaAssetHandler.Attach` now raises on `pcrAbsent` as well as `pcrForeign`, and `run_cap7m_runtime.sh` requires `readback=ours` rather than merely refusing `foreign`. `Attach` therefore proves what its name claims — that THIS view routes `pweb://` to THIS handler — instead of proving that a process-global counter moved. The headless suite is unaffected: its refusal case relies on the seam-counter check, which still runs first and never reaches the native accessor.
  evidence: `src/platform/macos/pweb_cocoa_bridge.mm` `pweb_cocoa_handler_installed_on` returning `PWEB_COCOA_READBACK_OURS|ABSENT|FOREIGN`; `src/platform/macos/pweb.platform.cocoa.pas` `TCocoaAssetHandler.Attach` now refusing anything but `pcrOurs`; `test/cap7m/run_cap7m_runtime.sh` `assert_run` asserting `[ "${readback}" = 'ours' ]`; `test/platform/pweb.test.cocoa.pas:388` still exercising the seam-counter refusal with a non-webview handle.

The unit-path set is already proven on a stock FPC 3.2.2 elsewhere: `utils/pas2js/pas2js.pp` at the pinned revision compiles and links with exactly those `-Fu`/`-Fi` paths and the produced binary reports `-iV` = 3.0.1 (dev-host measurement; the Lazarus-bundled FPC install first tried carries a nonstandard `contnrs.ppu` without `PHashItemList` and is NOT stock 3.2.2 — the standalone 3.2.2 install is). - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-7-cap7f-final-integration.md`
  summary: DEFERRED — `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` allowlists per-file platform-conditional COUNTS, which is exact in both directions (growth and shrinkage both fail) but blind to a SWAP: replacing one conditional with another inside an allowlisted file — say `{$ifdef DARWIN}` becoming `{$ifdef ANDROID}` in `pweb.assets.folder.pas` — keeps the count and passes silently, and only the (unswept) content of the change would reveal it. The upgrade is a per-file FINGERPRINT: hash the ordered list of matched directive TEXTS (not line numbers, so pure line drift stays quiet) and pin that hash per allowlisted file, making any substitution a named failure. Not taken in CAP-7F because the allowlist grammar was brand-new and a fingerprint mispin would have blocked the closure aggregation the same day it first ran; land it the next time the sweep or the allowlist is touched.
  evidence: `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` `$allow` maps file -> integer count only; the sweep's own negative self-test (`check_cap7f_selftest.ps1`) proves the off-allowlist branch, not the swap-inside-allowlist branch.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8a-capability-policy-core.md`

Texts, not line numbers, so pure line drift stays quiet by design; the count check is kept for its more legible error.

Ratified semantics, not a defect — but a future host-hardening shard should consider an opt-in strict mode (or a host-side startup assertion that every context-template principal/window id is explicitly configured in the policy) so misconfiguration is a named startup failure rather than a silent widening.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8a-capability-policy-core.md`
  summary: RECORDED — the production release policy deliberately zero-cap registers TWO non-application methods beside the runtime pair: `example.report` (the page's machine-verdict channel, intercepted by the host-private TReportingBridge — the policy runs BEFORE the bridge, so an unregistered report method would be forbidden and no verdict could ever latch) and `No.SuchMethod` (the frozen CAP-5 SDK acceptance pages probe this unregistered method and require the bridge's typed `method_not_found`/404 for their `errmap` verdict). Registering the probe zero-cap is safe by construction: it is the ratified mapped-but-unregistered shape (spec I/O row "Not in catalog, mapped"; gate I7) — the policy allows it, the bridge catalog miss answers 404, and no service can ever be reached through it because no service of that name exists or may be registered without also appearing in the catalog. Every OTHER unknown method through the production host is now `forbidden` pre-bridge (ratified precedence, security-model.md:33-35), which the CAP-8A runtime deny probe (`Denied.Probe` -> `"denied":true`, anchored in the release args-gates and evidence emitters) proves on every run — an allow-all regression reports `"denied":false` (the probe 404s) and goes red at those anchors.
  evidence: `examples/08-release/releaseapp.pas` BuildReleasePolicy comments; `examples/04-react/frontend/src/App.tsx` Denied.Probe block; dev-host counterfactual 2026-08-18 — the pre-CAP-8A allow-all binary against the probe corpus reported `"denied":false` with exit 0, while the CAP-8A binary reported `"denied":true`; anchors in `test/cap7f/run_host_args_gate.ps1`/`.sh` and `test/cap7f/emit_evidence.ps1`/`.sh` (linux + macOS react branches).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8b-privileged-navigation.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION (ratified at Checkpoint 1, carried into the shipped design) — user activation cannot be reported honestly by any of the four engines, so a gesture is NOT a security boundary and the classifier never reads `UserActivated`. WebView2 `IsUserInitiated` and WebKitGTK `is_user_gesture` both report TRUE for a navigation issued in the continuation of a `webview_bind` promise (the ordinary shape of a PWeb page: `await invoke(...)` then follow a link), because upstream resolves that promise through the engine's script-execution API and the engine runs host-injected script WITH a gesture; WKWebView exposes no public gesture flag at all (`_isUserInitiated` is SPI, forbidden here). The consequence (ratification R-A): every raw external navigation inside the privileged WebView is ALWAYS cancelled (`https:`/`mailto:` included), no navigation callback ever reaches an OS opener, and handing a URI to the operating system became a capability-authorized runtime invocation (`pweb.openExternal` -> `external.open`) checked by the CAP-8A policy like any other method. `security-model.md` was NOT rewritten in this shard — the ratification records the intended new wording (a privileged WebView never navigates to external content; approved `https`/`mailto` URIs go to the OS only through an explicit capability-authorized invocation), and applying it to `_bmad-output/specs/spec-pweb/security-model.md` is a doc-only follow-up left for the spec owner.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap8b-audit-findings.md` W3/L3/M4 and "The definitive user-activation matrix (all four targets)"; ratification R-A in that same file; `src/security/pweb.navigation.policy.pas` header and `PWebClassifyNavigation` (two outcomes only, `UserActivated` deliberately unread); `test/security/pweb.test.navigation.pas` `ActivationIsNotAnInput` replays the whole corpus with the flag inverted and requires identical decisions.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8b-privileged-navigation.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION — "no subframe exists in the privileged WebView" is NOT literally achievable, and the acceptance wording was framed as "no untrusted content executes in any frame" instead. `frame-src 'none'` does not block an `about:blank` iframe on either engine measured (X1), and a trusted page can always create a same-origin empty frame; on Linux and macOS the raw `__webview__` message-handler transport reaches the native binding from EVERY frame, so an `about:blank` child is a full holder of the bridge — but it has no content of its own and only the already-privileged parent can script it, so nothing crosses a trust boundary. The real-window matrix therefore proves the property that IS deliverable: a tripwire method (`matrix.childExecuted`) that a SERVABLE trusted child (`/assets/child.html`) and a `data:` inline child each call over both transports if they ever execute, and the host requires that counter to stay 0 — `frame-src 'none'` and the classifier's subframe denial keep every child from loading at all.
  evidence: `cap8b-audit-findings.md` X1 and L1/M1; ratification R-B (case-insensitive authority, `connect-src 'self'`, no bootstrap exception); `test/cap8b/navmatrix.pas` `child_executed` ledger (required 0) + `test/cap8b/fixture/assets/child.html`+`child.js`; `test/cap8b/run_nav_matrix.{ps1,sh}`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8b-privileged-navigation.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION — on Windows/WebView2 a `javascript:` URL raises NO navigation event and EXECUTES in place, so the navigation classifier is NOT what stops it there; the native CSP is (`script-src 'self'` without `'unsafe-inline'`). Likewise `file:` and top-level `data:` raise no event (the engine refuses them itself). The classifier still carries those rows as defence-in-depth for an engine that might one day hand them over, but no test asserts the classifier as their Windows enforcement mechanism. This is why the CSP rides EVERY response unconditionally (not just `text/html`): an SVG served from `pweb://app` is a scriptable top-level document in the trusted origin, and gating the CSP on media type left it without `script-src`/`connect-src`/`frame-src` — a real defect corrected in `PWebNativeSecurityHeaders`.
  evidence: `cap8b-audit-findings.md` W2a/P1; `src/security/pweb.navigation.policy.pas` `PWebNativeSecurityHeaders` interface comment (unconditional CSP, the SVG rationale) and `test/security/pweb.test.navigation.pas` `NativeHeaderProfile`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8b-privileged-navigation.md`
  summary: RECORDED — the CAP-8B real-window matrix (`test/cap8b/navmatrix.pas`) substitutes a COUNTING invocation bridge for the mORMot SOA bridge on purpose, so every native arrival is attributed by method and bridge-isolation becomes an equality over the whole ledger rather than a pair of unread zeroes. The mORMot SOA path with the navsec block required is proven separately by the release smoke on all four targets (`examples/08-release/releaseapp.pas` `TReportingBridge` requires `navExternalBlocked`/`navAuthorityBlocked`/`navCspBlocked`/`navOpenExternal` before latching PASS). The two hosts are complementary: the release smoke proves the production bridge, the nav-matrix proves the guard/opener/CSP under a hostile corpus with per-method accounting. A future shard could merge them, but the split keeps each host's assertion legible.
  evidence: `test/cap8b/navmatrix.pas` `TCountingMatrixBridge` + the per-method `RequireCount` ledger; `examples/04-react/frontend/src/App.tsx` navsec block; `examples/08-release/releaseapp.pas` latch conditions.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8c-multi-principal-integration.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATIONS (ratified R-C3 at Checkpoint 1, recorded here as the canonical residual) — two engine facts the Phase-A topology probe measured through the frozen 17-export ABI are permanent constraints on every multi-window host, and CAP-8C deliberately builds NO platform-conditional workaround for either: (1) Windows/WebView2 `webview_terminate` posts a THREAD-GLOBAL quit (`PostQuitMessage`), so terminating a non-owner instance stops the shared loop NONDETERMINISTICALLY (both interleavings were recorded across runs of the same binary — the quit raced destroy's queue depletion); (2) macOS/WKWebView `webview_destroy` of a second instance while the loop runs KILLS THE PROCESS on both architectures (`crash_guard=destroy-second`, timeline truncated). The ratified shared lifecycle model simply never performs either operation: window close/reopen is SOURCE-level (frozen `IInvocationSource` Quiesce/Close + a fresh source on the long-lived instance), `webview_terminate` is called only on the run-owner at shutdown, and `webview_destroy` runs only after the owner's loop exits, reverse creation order. Any future shard that wants true mid-run window destruction needs an upstream conversation, not a workaround.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap8c-topology-findings.md` T2 + Ratification block (hosted run 32748017833, four `cap8c-topology-<target>` artifacts); `test/cap8c/multiprincipal.pas` R-C2/R-C3 teardown (no mid-loop destroy anywhere; terminate only via `RequestTerminate` on the run-owner; reverse-order post-loop destroys).

What does NOT exist is any host wiring, guard profile, or spec ratification for such a view — designing it belongs to the shard that needs it, against the R-C2 source-level lifecycle and the R-C3 limitations above.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8c-multi-principal-integration.md`
  summary: MEASUREMENT GAP (recorded, monitored by the production gate) — the R-C2 teardown shape on macOS (post-loop `webview_destroy` of TWO instances in reverse creation order) was RATIFIED but never measured by the Phase-A topology probe: on both macOS architectures the probe died earlier, inside the mid-loop `destroy(second)` experiment (`crash_guard=destroy-second`), so its post-loop destroy phase was never reached there. The CAP-8C multiprincipal harness performs exactly that ratified sequence in its GUI teardown on every hosted run, which makes the production gate itself the first and ongoing measurement of the shape; a macOS teardown crash there would surface as a red `CAP-8C multi-principal integration harness (WKWebView)` step, not as a silent assumption.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap8c-topology-findings.md` T2 ("Post-loop destroy order consequently went unmeasured there"); `test/cap8c/multiprincipal.pas` `RunGuiLeg` finally block (Login destroyed first, Main second, both strictly after `webview_run` returned).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8c-multi-principal-integration.md`
  summary: MEASURED CONSTRAINT + ESCALATION (frozen Cocoa adapter surface, surfaced by the two-window arming fix after hosted run 32770563751) — the macOS GUI leg cannot give its SECOND window a navigation guard through the frozen adapters: `TCocoaNavigationGuard.Create` arms a single process-wide slot (`pweb_cocoa_nav_arm` refuses a second guard with "a navigation guard is already armed in this process") and the one guard object offers a single-shot `Attach`. The ASSET side is solved platform-honestly without adapter changes (one process-wide armed handler created before either `webview_create`; the seam installs it into every configuration created while armed; the second view's service is asserted through its own page-loaded report) - and the same shape is NOT available for the guard, whose per-view delegate installation goes only through the frozen single-Attach class. Consequence: the hosted macOS multiprincipal gate will fail at a crisp pre-flight (the second guard Create) until a human decision: (a) ratify a Cocoa adapter surface extension (multi-arm slots and/or a per-view attach for additional windows - a frozen-surface change), or (b) ratify a different macOS guard topology for multi-window hosts. Not patched around here, per the CAP-8C review instruction; Windows (per-controller) and Linux (per-view signals, single context registration) both pass with two live windows - Linux proven locally under WSL Xvfb with the corpus digest byte-identical to Windows.
  evidence: `src/platform/macos/pweb.platform.cocoa.pas` `TCocoaNavigationGuard.Create` (nav_arm refusal) + `Attach` (single-shot `fAttached` refusal); `test/cap8c/multiprincipal.pas` GUI-leg topology note; hosted run 32770563751 (Linux/macOS asset pre-flight refusals, Windows green); local WSL run: linux-x86_64 MULTIPRINCIPAL PASS, security-corpus sha256 `c5fc378bc3c6eb6a...` equal to the Windows corpus.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-8-cap8c-multi-principal-integration.md`
  summary: RESOLVED by R-C4 (human-ratified, recorded in cap8c-topology-findings.md) - the MEASURED CONSTRAINT + ESCALATION entry above (the frozen Cocoa navigation guard held a single process-wide armed slot, leaving the macOS second window unguardable) is closed by the minimal PRIVATE-bridge extension the ratification authorized: `pweb_cocoa_nav_arm` now STAGES a handle and `pweb_cocoa_nav_attach` COMMITS it as the binding of the one WKWebView it is called with, into a small per-view slot table the delegate resolves each ARRIVING view against (handle-first/view-second atomic ordering; disown clears the staged slot and every binding of its handle; an unbound view fails closed and cancels). The delegate OBJECT remains the process-wide singleton, the C surface signatures are unchanged (no public webview ABI change, no new export, no upstream patch), the shared classifier / CSP block / decision paths are byte-untouched, and the bridge-global exactly-once identities (handlers_completed = action+response = allowed+cancelled) still hold as process-wide sums while per-guard Allowed/Cancelled counters stay per view through the Pascal registry slots. CAP-8B single-window behavior is PRESERVED verbatim: one Create (stage) + one Attach (bind) is observationally identical to the old arm, and the 'a navigation guard is already armed in this process' pre-flight still refuses a second unattached guard or a double-armed view. Files: src/platform/macos/pweb_cocoa_bridge.h (comment-only semantics update on nav_arm/nav_disown/nav_attach/nav_detach), pweb_cocoa_bridge.mm (the per-view binding table + staged arm), pweb.platform.cocoa.pas (comments; no code-path change).
  evidence: R-C4 in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap8c-topology-findings.md` (commit 7c4bf2c); the escalation entry above; `test/cap8c/multiprincipal.pas` GuiBuildWindow (per-window Create+Attach now serves both macOS windows); Windows navmatrix + multiprincipal re-run PASS locally after the change, Linux multiprincipal PASS under WSL Xvfb, corpus digest unchanged.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9a-quickjs-invocation-foundation.md`
  summary: CAP-9B SCOPE (deliberately not built here, per the frozen Never list) — script/package LOADING is entirely absent from CAP-9A: no plugin directory scanner, no plugin installation/discovery/update, no arbitrary filesystem module loading, no hot reload, and no ES module resolver (the foundation gate runs plain in-memory scripts only; `Evaluate` is called with `JS_EVAL_TYPE_GLOBAL` and nothing pumps a module loader). CAP-9B owns trusted plugin metadata, package/script loading, module resolution and the production lifecycle on top of the frozen engine/source architecture this shard proves.
  evidence: spec `<frozen-after-approval>` Never list; `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` (no filesystem access anywhere; scripts arrive only through the host-supplied mailbox); `test/cap9a/quickjsfoundation.pas` (all scripts are in-source constants).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9a-quickjs-invocation-foundation.md`
  summary: DEFERRED CANDIDATE — the Promise-returning `pweb.invoke` (native function returns a QuickJS Promise; completion posted to the plugin-thread mailbox; owning thread resolves/rejects and pumps `JS_ExecutePendingJob`) was measured at Checkpoint 1 and NOT chosen: it needs `JS_NewPromiseCapability` + `JS_Call` + a deterministic job pump + cross-enqueue JSValue rooting, a low-level surface the pinned high-level wrapper does not expose, while the ratified synchronous model (dedicated non-scheduler plugin thread, bounded wait, exactly-once completion guaranteeing wait termination) needs none of it. If a future shard wants intra-plugin invocation concurrency or async-first plugin scripts, the Promise model is the shape to ratify, against the same completion-thread-affinity rules (no worker ever touches JSContext/JSValue).
  evidence: spec Design Notes "Sync over Promise (measured)"; `deps/mormot2/src/lib/mormot.lib.quickjs.pas` `JS_NewPromiseCapability`/`JS_ExecutePendingJob` (present, unused); `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` `InvokeJson` (the synchronous envelope path).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9a-quickjs-invocation-foundation.md`
  summary: UPSTREAM BUG TO FILE (mORMot, worked around PWeb-side, pin untouched) — the pinned `mormot.lib.quickjs.pas` declares `procedure JS_SetMaxStackSize(ctx: JSContext; stack_size: PtrUInt)` while the pinned C implementation (`res/static/libquickjs/quickjs.h:464`) takes `JSRuntime*`: calling the mistyped binding writes the stack bound into JSContext memory and was MEASURED to corrupt the context (0xC0000005 under allocation pressure with a memory limit set, Windows x64, FPC 3.2.2). PWeb never calls the pinned binding: `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` re-declares the external with the correct `JSRuntime` first parameter (unit-local, shadowing the import) and all three resource limits then fail safe. An issue should be filed against synopse/mORMot2 describing the signature mismatch — strip any PWeb-proprietary context from the report before submitting.
  evidence: spec Design Notes "Pin defect (measured, Win x64)" (bisected: pinned-binding call crashes, runtime-typed call is safe across recursion + OOM + destroy); `deps/mormot2/src/lib/mormot.lib.quickjs.pas:950` vs `deps/mormot2/res/static/libquickjs/quickjs.h:464`; `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` (the private correctly-typed external and its warning comment).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9a-quickjs-invocation-foundation.md`
  summary: REVIEW DEFER (upstream companion to the JS_SetMaxStackSize report) — the pinned `mormot.lib.static.pas` declares `pas_malloc(size: cardinal)` / `pas_malloc_usable_size(...): integer` while the pinned QuickJS C sources call them with `size_t` (cutils.h): an allocation request above 4 GiB is silently truncated to its low 32 bits on the targets that link mormot.lib.static (Windows/Linux x64), returning an undersized buffer. In PWeb practice the per-plugin `JS_SetMemoryLimit` ceiling (64 MB default) makes such a request unreachable, and the aarch64-darwin exports PWeb ships itself were widened to `PtrUInt` with an overflow-guarded `pas_calloc` — but the pinned declaration should be reported upstream together with the stack-size signature mismatch. Strip any PWeb-proprietary context from the report before submitting.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9A diff (edge-case layer); `deps/mormot2/src/lib/mormot.lib.static.pas:257` (`size: cardinal`) vs `deps/mormot2/res/static/libquickjs/cutils.h:96` (`void *pas_malloc(size_t size)`); `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` aarch64-darwin export block (PtrUInt + overflow guard, with the divergence-from-pin comment).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: CAP-9B2 SCOPE (deliberately not built here, per the frozen Never list) — CAP-9B1 implements ATOMIC load only: one descriptor in, one Running plugin or nothing. There is no lifecycle state machine, no stop/start/reload, no lifecycle hooks, no plugin discovery or directory scanning, no directory watching, no installation/update path and no signed packages. `Unload` remains exactly the CAP-9A teardown (Quiesce → Close → join, engine freed on its owning thread) and is used only for the atomic-failure path and by the harness. CAP-9B2 owns the full lifecycle on top of the frozen `TPWebQuickJSPackageDescriptor` / module-loader layer this shard proves; CAP-9C owns release packaging and CAP-9 closure.
  evidence: spec `<frozen-after-approval>` Never list; `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` (`PWebLoadQuickJSPackage` is the only entry point and has no restart arm; no filesystem enumeration anywhere); `src/script/pweb.script.package.pas` (`IAssetStore` only, demand-driven, no enumeration added).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: DEFERRED CANDIDATE — dynamic `import()` is UNAVAILABLE rather than blocked, and that is a measured position, not an oversight. The pinned QuickJS enqueues a job for `import()` (`res/static/libquickjs/quickjs.c:28634`); PWeb pumps no job queue, so the promise never settles and the loader is never reached (corpus `dynamic_import probe=object|pending|1 loader_before=3 loader_after=3`). Force-pumping in the Checkpoint-1 probe proved `js_dynamic_import_job` → `JS_RunModule` → `js_host_resolve_imported_module` routes through the SAME scoped normalize/loader, so it can never bypass the resolver even if a future shard adds a pump; the loader additionally seals the graph once `FLoadState` leaves Loading. Enabling `import()` means ratifying a Promise/job pump first — the same decision CAP-9A deferred for the Promise-returning `pweb.invoke`.
  evidence: `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` (`NormalizeModule`/`LoadModule` refuse unless Loading); `test/cap9b1/quickjspackage.pas` `PAdversarial`; `build/cap9b1/quickjs-package-corpus.txt` `dynamic_import` row.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: PIN HAZARD TO AVOID (no PWeb code calls it; recorded so nobody adds one) — `JS_ExecutePendingJob(rt: JSRuntime; pctx: PJSContext)` in `deps/mormot2/src/lib/mormot.lib.quickjs.pas` carries a literal `// TODO: Check pctx if the type is right.` comment, and the pinned C writes through that pointer: calling it with `nil` is a null-pointer store, MEASURED as 0xC0000005 on Windows x64 during the CAP-9B1 Checkpoint-1 probe. Any future job pump must pass the address of a real `JSContext` variable. PWeb never calls it today.
  evidence: `deps/mormot2/src/lib/mormot.lib.quickjs.pas` (`JS_ExecutePendingJob` declaration and its TODO); CAP-9B1 Checkpoint-1 probe (crash with `nil`, clean with `@ctx`).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`

The CAP-9B1 spec is likewise over the 20 KB story budget (23 KB), which the human ratified at Checkpoint 1 alongside the equivalent CAP-9A overage (21 KB). Splitting `ci.yml` per platform is the obvious remedy and belongs to whichever shard next touches CI structurally, not to a package/module-loader shard.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget warnings on `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and on the CAP-9B1 spec; the CAP-9A final artifact's "known limitations" note carrying the same `ci.yml` overage forward.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: REVIEW DEFER (real, not caused by CAP-9B1) — `TPWebModuleGraph.IndexOf` is a linear byte-exact scan called from `Intern`, `DepthOf` and `AddEdge`, so graph bookkeeping is O(n²): at the `PWEB_PACKAGE_HARD_LIMITS.MaxModules = 4096` ceiling that is roughly 16M string comparisons per load. The ratified default is 256 modules, where it is irrelevant, and the CAP-9B1 matrix never approaches the hard cap. A host that deliberately raises `MaxModules` toward the hard maximum would want a hash index first.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9B1 diff (blind-hunter layer); `src/script/pweb.script.package.pas` `TPWebModuleGraph.IndexOf`/`Intern`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: REVIEW DEFER (bounded by a FROZEN interface, so not fixable here) — CAP-9B1's module and manifest size bounds are checked on the raw `TAssetResponse.Content` length, which is the earliest point this layer can observe, but `IAssetStore.TryRead` is frozen with no size, HEAD or streaming form: a carrier has therefore already materialised the asset by then, and for `TZipAssetStore` a highly compressible entry is inflated in full before the bound refuses it. The limits record's header states this scope honestly rather than claiming a bound it does not have. A carrier-side materialisation cap (an additive `IAssetStore` capability, or a ZIP-store uncompressed-size refusal at index time, where `TZipAssetStore` already records `fFullSize`) is the real fix and needs interface ratification. CAP-4's v1 asset policy already holds that bulk media is not an ordinary bundle asset.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9B1 diff (blind-hunter + edge-case layers); `src/assets/pweb.assets.intf.pas` (frozen `TryRead`); `src/assets/pweb.assets.zip.pas` (`fFullSize` recorded at construction, unused for bounding).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: REVIEW DEFER (ratification question) — module source shares the one `PWebStrictUtf8` validator with asset paths, so it inherits a PATH portability rule: a raw U+0080..U+009F (C1) byte sequence is refused even inside a comment or string literal, while C0 controls other than NUL pass. That is deterministic and fail-closed, is documented on `PWebPrepareModuleSource` and pinned by the `module_c1_control` fixture, but it is stricter than JavaScript requires. Whether module source should get its own validator permitting C1 is a decision to ratify, not a second silent copy of a security validator to grow.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9B1 diff (blind-hunter layer); `src/assets/pweb.assets.support.pas` `PWebStrictUtf8` (C1 rejection); `test/cap9b1/quickjspackage.pas` `module_c1_control`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: REVIEW DEFER (pre-existing, CAP-9A) — `test/cap9a/run_quickjsfoundation.ps1` extracts `MARKER_PASS` with `-CaseSensitive` but tests it with `-match`, so a marker differing only in case would satisfy the Windows gate while the bash sibling's `grep -qF` refused it; and `test/cap9a/run_quickjsfoundation.sh` runs the harness from a `mktemp -d` it never removes. Both were fixed in the CAP-9B1 runners (`-cmatch`, a `trap`-cleaned run directory); the CAP-9A pair is untouched because it is outside this shard, and its only CAP-9B1 change was the one `-Fusrc/assets` the new unit dependency required.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9B1 diff (blind-hunter layer); `test/cap9a/run_quickjsfoundation.ps1` marker test; `test/cap9b1/run_quickjspackage.ps1`/`.sh` for the corrected shape.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b1-quickjs-package-module-loader.md`
  summary: UPSTREAM/PIN NOTE (fixed PWeb-side, recorded because it was latent in CAP-9A too) — a `TThread` descendant whose constructor raises BEFORE `inherited Create` sends the automatic destructor into a never-constructed `TThread`, which dereferences uninitialised handles (measured: `EAccessViolation`, reproducible from any descriptor-validation raise). CAP-9A's `TPWebQuickJSPlugin.Create` had exactly that shape and was never exercised with invalid input, so it never crashed. Both constructors now build the base first (suspended) and `Start` last, and `PWebLoadQuickJSPackage` validates through the non-raising `PWebValidateQuickJSPackageDescriptor` so the shipped path never constructs a doomed object. Any future `TThread` descendant in this codebase should follow the same rule.
  evidence: adversarial review of the CAP-9B1 diff (verification-gap layer's "rejected-descriptor arm is never reached", which exposed the crash the moment its suggested test was added); `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` `Create`/`CreatePackage`; `test/cap9b1/quickjspackage.pas` `PDescriptorAndRuntime`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b2-quickjs-plugin-lifecycle.md`
  summary: CAP-9C SCOPE (deliberately not built here, per the frozen Never list) — CAP-9B2 owns the plugin LIFECYCLE only: an explicit state machine, host-to-plugin export calls, bounded unload with a quarantine last resort, and one transactional generation swap. There is still NO plugin discovery, NO directory scanning, NO file watching, NO automatic hot reload, NO installation or update path, NO signed packages, and NO public plugin-management API — every transition is a native call made by trusted host code. Plugins are also NOT packaged into the production release. CAP-9C owns release packaging and the CAP-9 closure.
  evidence: `src/script/pweb.script.plugin.pas` (no filesystem, timer or watcher API anywhere in the unit); the mechanical sweep over both new units for `FindFirst`/`ReadDirectoryChanges`/`inotify`/`FSEvents`/`SetTimer`/`ParamStr`, which reports none.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b2-quickjs-plugin-lifecycle.md`

The one route that does work — a synthetic `import * as ns from "./main.js"` capture module — was measured at Checkpoint 1 to cost `loader +0` but `normalize +1` plus one module-graph edge, and both are `quickjs-package-corpus.txt` fields, so it would move the FROZEN `quickjs_package_digest` on all four targets. Real ES exports become available if a future shard ratifies either a pin patch exporting the accessor or a re-baselined CAP-9B1 digest.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b2-quickjs-plugin-lifecycle.md`
  summary: PIN HAZARD TO AVOID (recorded because it is silent until it kills the process) — a `JSValue` still alive when `JS_FreeRuntime` runs trips the pinned `assert(list_empty(&rt->gc_obj_list))` and terminates the process with NO catchable Pascal exception (measured `0xE0434353` on Windows x64 during the CAP-9B2 Checkpoint-1 probe; the `try/except` around `FreeAndNil(FEngine)` never ran). Any future code that captures a `JSValue` into a Pascal field must free it on the owning thread BEFORE the engine is destroyed — `TPWebQuickJSPlugin` does exactly that for its export table in `Execute`'s epilogue.
  evidence: CAP-9B2 Checkpoint-1 probe (a deliberately leaked `JS_ParseJSON` result reproduced the abort; freeing it made the same run exit 0); `src/script/pweb.script.quickjs.pas` `Execute` epilogue ordering.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9b2-quickjs-plugin-lifecycle.md`
  summary: RESOURCE-LIMIT CLASSIFICATION IS A TEXT MATCH (deliberate, with no API alternative in this pin) — `FEngine.TimeoutAborted` is an engine fact and outranks everything, but a stack overflow and an out-of-memory arrive only as ordinary catchable `InternalError` exceptions, so `CallExportOnOwnThread` recognises them by the exact literals the pinned C throws (`stack overflow`, `out of memory`, `interrupted`). Ambiguity resolves TOWARDS the limit: a plugin that fakes the text only gets its own generation unloaded, whereas mistaking a real bound for an ordinary throw would keep a degraded engine serving. A pin that grows a structured limit signal would let this become an API check.
  evidence: CAP-9B2 Checkpoint-1 probe (`InternalError: stack overflow`, `InternalError: out of memory`, `InternalError: interrupted` with `timeoutAborted=yes` only for the last); `deps/mormot2/res/static/libquickjs/quickjs.c` `JS_ThrowStackOverflow`/`JS_ThrowOutOfMemory`.

CAP-9C1 therefore generates the native registry PER TARGET at build time (a committed registry could not be true on four targets), proves determinism where it is real by staging the payload twice on each target and requiring byte equality, and cross-compares the SEMANTIC corpus plus `cap9c1_inventory_digest` instead of the archive bytes. If a future shard wants one archive byte-identical everywhere it needs a deterministic DEFLATE implementation of its own, which is a dependency decision, not a packaging one.

The C4 injection rows therefore record `code=registry`, the FIRST gate, and assert exactly that. The literal writer stays because it is the last line between a future unvalidated field and generated Pascal, in the same category as CAP-9B1's `plcThread`/`plcEngine`, which are likewise unreachable without fault injection.

It is the same class of defect `tools/bundler/pwebbundle.pas` already records ("the RTL Ansi filesystem layer depends on runtime codepage state and mistranslates cross-unit concatenations"), and the fix is the same: the packager talks to the wide Win32 API directly on Windows and uses the RTL walk only on POSIX. Any future PWeb build tool that enumerates a directory on Windows should start from that shape rather than rediscovering this.

At the shipped scale (7 entries, 2 plugins) and at the ratified `PWEB_RELEASE_MAX_ENTRIES = 4096` / `PWEB_RELEASE_MAX_PLUGINS = 64` ceilings this is irrelevant, but a host that packaged thousands of modules would want an index. This is the same class as the CAP-9B1 `TPWebModuleGraph.IndexOf` entry above and would be fixed with it.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c1-quickjs-release-package.md`
  summary: RATIFIED POSITION, revisit only with evidence — the runtime verifier MATERIALISES the whole archive under `PWEB_RELEASE_PACKAGE_MAX_BYTES` (64 MiB, checked against the OS-reported size BEFORE a byte is read) rather than streaming it twice. That is deliberate: a streaming digest followed by a separate open is exactly the check/open race the brief asks to close, and reading once means the bytes that were hashed ARE the bytes that are parsed. The cost is that memory is bounded by the cap rather than by a small window. A release that legitimately needs a larger plugin archive should raise the cap knowingly, not switch to a two-pass read.
  evidence: `src/script/pweb.script.release.pas` unit header ("no check/open TOCTOU window to race, because there is no second read") and `ReadAndHashOnce` (both platform bodies bound the size before allocating).

Both need the registry to be a PARAMETER so the EXPECTATION can be mutated instead of the bytes, which is exactly how CAP-9C1 proves them (`archive_invalid`, `inventory_mismatch`, `inventory_count`, `registry`) on a mutated registry copy, in the SAME job that runs the CAP-9C2 gate. The CAP-9C2 negative matrix therefore covers missing, truncated, over-long, wrong-digest and reparse-point, and records `negative_inventory_and_registry=cap9c1` in its own corpus so a reader is never left to infer coverage it does not have.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`

Putting the outcome in the four-way digest would break cross-target equality for a reason that has nothing to do with the product, so it is carried as its OWN evidence field (`cap9c2_negative_reparse`) which the aggregator requires to read exactly `yes` — a waiver is recorded honestly by the runner and refused by the aggregator, the same "SKIP never promotes" discipline the CAP-7F matrix already applies. A dev-host run therefore passes locally with `waived`; a hosted run that waived it goes red.
  evidence: `test/cap9c2/run_quickjsgui.ps1` (the comment and the json-only field); `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` CAP-9C2 REPARSE NOT PROVEN; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` case c46.

CAP-9 tests QuickJS against the UI backend, not React against Pas2JS again, so the plugin-enabled acceptance page is React only; every existing Pas2JS release regression (CAP-5, CAP-7L L17, CAP-7M2's second `.app`) keeps running unchanged and the pas2js logical-inventory equality across the three POSIX targets is untouched. A future shard that wants a plugin-enabled Pas2JS product needs only its own frontend directory: the host is frontend-agnostic and takes whatever `app.pwb` the bundler produced.
