- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: BUILD HAZARD worth recording because it fails SILENTLY and blankly — importing the canonical CAP-5 `App` component across the example boundary makes node resolve its bare `react` import from `examples/04-react/frontend/node_modules` while the importing page resolves its own from `examples/07-quickjs/frontend/node_modules`. Both directories exist once CI has built the CAP-5 frontend, so the bundle contained TWO React instances: the component's hooks belonged to one and `createRoot` to the other, React threw "invalid hook call" during the first render, and the page came up completely BLANK with no console anyone reads and no build error. MEASURED exactly that way. `examples/07-quickjs/frontend/build.mjs` now pins `react`, `react-dom` and `@pweb/runtime` to that package's own resolution through esbuild `alias`; any future cross-example component import needs the same three lines.
  evidence: `examples/07-quickjs/frontend/build.mjs` (the alias block and its comment); the CAP-9C2 `ui_rendered` gate, which asserts BOTH acceptance elements exist rather than that the document is non-empty — the empty-body false positive is what hid the defect for two runs.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: PROOF DIVISION, stated so neither shard is credited with the other's evidence — CAP-9C2 proves that concurrent UI and plugin completions do not CROSS (distinguishing operands, 3 vs 11, each landing on its own source under a forced barrier). It does NOT re-prove that a LATE completion from a retired generation dies at the exactly-once gate, nor that a reload never exposes two active generations: those are `cap9b2_stale_completion` and `cap9b2_two_active_generations`, owned by CAP-9B2 and re-run green in the same job. CAP-9C2's reload leg asserts the generation id changed, the result stayed 42 and the UI kept working — the GUI-process containment of a contract B2 established headlessly.
  evidence: `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas` no_cross_delivery / reload_generation_changed rows; `test/cap9b2/quickjslifecycle.pas` for the two rows cited.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: DELIBERATE MEASUREMENT SHAPE — the UI/plugin overlap is FORCED by a barrier inside `CalculatorService.Add` (both invocations must be inside the one service instance before either may leave) rather than observed statistically. That is on purpose: a timing-only overlap is exactly the evidence this project refuses, and a forced barrier makes "they ran concurrently" a counter (`peak_in_service >= 2`) instead of a probability. The cost is that the concurrency legs measure that the architecture PERMITS overlap, not how much overlap a real workload achieves; a throughput or contention benchmark is a different exercise and is not attempted here.
  evidence: `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas` `TCalculatorService.Add` (the armed barrier) and the concurrent_overlap / concurrent_barrier_peak rows.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: DOCUMENTATION BUDGET — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` remains over the 100 KB default budget (167 KB after the four CAP-9C2 gate steps, their frontend builds and their uploads), carried forward from CAP-9A, CAP-9B1, CAP-9B2 and CAP-9C1. Splitting `ci.yml` per platform is still the obvious remedy and still belongs to whichever shard next touches CI structurally — CAP-10, which will restructure the build path anyway, is the natural owner.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget warnings on `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the identical entry in the CAP-9C1 block above.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: SECURITY DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED in CAP-9C1 production code, recorded because the SHAPE recurs — `pweb.script.release.pas` opened the plugin archive on Windows with `CreateFileW(..., OPEN_EXISTING, FILE_FLAG_SEQUENTIAL_SCAN, 0)` and then refused `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` on the returned handle. Without `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` the kernel FOLLOWS the link and the handle refers to the TARGET, whose attributes carry no reparse bit, so the refusal could never fire; the POSIX body was always correct through `O_NOFOLLOW`. It went unnoticed because no gate had ever pointed a real symlinked `plugins.zip` at a real Windows release layout — CAP-9C2's negative matrix is the first, and the host accepted it and exited 0. Exposure is bounded by the digest (the redirect target must be a byte-identical archive), but a documented refusal was inoperative on one platform for a whole shard. FIXED by adding the flag; the CAP-9C1 gate re-runs green with byte-identical digests, so the change is inert for every legitimate archive. THE LESSON: on Windows an attribute test on a handle says nothing about the path unless the open refused to follow indirection — `TFolderAssetStore` closes the same class differently and correctly, by comparing the kernel's FINAL normalized path of the open handle to the expected one. Any future PWeb code that opens a trusted file on Windows should do one or the other, deliberately.
  evidence: `src/script/pweb.script.release.pas` `ReadAndHashOnce` (the flag and its comment); the CAP-9C2 `negative_reparse` field, now a required-`yes` aggregator gate on all four targets; `src/assets/pweb.assets.folder.pas` `FinalPathOfHandle` for the alternative mechanism already in the codebase.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: PLATFORM LINK NOTE — CAP-9C2 is the first Linux binary that links BOTH the pinned `quickjs.o` and `libwebview`. `quickjs.o` references `__divti3` (128-bit division, a libgcc intrinsic); with quickjs alone the linker resolved it from the `libgcc_s` it pulls in implicitly, but once libwebview joined the line `ld` refused with `libgcc_s.so.1: error adding symbols: DSO missing from command line`. The fix is to NAME it (`-k-lgcc_s`), which costs nothing on a target that already links it. Any future Linux artifact combining the QuickJS static with a shared library needs the same flag.
  evidence: hosted run 33017160449 (the refusal) and 33017918894 (green with the flag); `test/cap9c2/run_quickjsgui.sh` Linux `compile_pas` and its comment.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: TEST-TIMING LESSON, twice in one shard, worth stating once — an ITERATION COUNT IS NOT A TIMEOUT. The service barrier re-signalled the event it was waiting on, so a 20-second budget was spent in microseconds and the concurrency row reported "no overlap" for a run that had never waited; and the render gate retried sixty times, each attempt returning as soon as its own report landed, so "sixty attempts" collapsed to about three seconds — enough for React to mount on a fast developer machine and not on a hosted runner, where every other row was green and that one alone was red. Both are now bounded by `GetTickCount64`. Any future gate that waits for something asynchronous in this repository should be written against a clock, and a retry loop whose body can return instantly should be treated as a bug until its bound is a tick.
  evidence: `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas` `TCalculatorService.Add` (the barrier) and the `ui_rendered` loop, both with the comment recording the measurement; hosted run 33019488934 (the render row red with all others green).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: CLOSED — the CAP-8B/CAP-8C "REVIEW DEFER (consolidation)" entry is retired. `pweb.openExternal` had four byte-similar private copies by the close of CAP-9 (`examples/08-release/releaseapp.pas`, `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas`, `test/cap8b/navmatrix.pas`, `test/cap8c/multiprincipal.pas`); the decision now lives once in `src/rpc/pweb.rpc.command.pas` as a `TPWebRuntimeCommandBridge` decorator over the FROZEN `IInvocationBridge`, with the platform opener and each host's own logging or counting injected. No interface changed, no scheduler path was added, the CAP-8 capability mapping (`pweb.openExternal` -> `external.open`) is untouched, and the layer carries zero platform conditionals by construction. The consolidation is proven behaviour-preserving by three frozen digests reproducing byte-exactly on the dev host after the migration.
  evidence: `test/cap10a/check_cap10a_contracts.ps1` Rule A (the literal is declared only in `pweb.rpc.command.pas` across src/examples/tools) and Rule B (no other file tree-wide carries the shared validator plus a constructed invalid_request envelope); dev-host reruns after migration — navigation_policy_digest 360d69f282e9d8b053d1ef8a5052f76860875c723b9e6512b0d38685f0c7212e, security_corpus_digest c5fc378bc3c6eb6aa6db753e35287db5cb7ed6332aeac77b75767919e5adbdf4, quickjs_gui_digest 67e08c692f2290e16721ba9c866162f3b5ac0f17338236977917d8a25a967b36, each equal to its CAP-8/CAP-9 closure value, with open_ok=2 open_refused=1 open_failed=0 (CAP-8B) and opener_main=3 opener_login=0 opener_plugin=0 (CAP-8C) unchanged.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (requirement graph) — `pweb doctor` implements exactly ONE mode, `source`: what must be true to open, edit and compile the project. The build and release requirements — Inno Setup, the pinned WebView2 artifacts, Xcode 16.4 / SDK 15.5, the mORMot and webview pins, the Windows normal/offline/fixed profiles, the QuickJS trusted-input state — emit NO ROW at all, and that absence is deliberate rather than an oversight: schema 1 carries no dependency model, so where a GENERATED project's mORMot or webview library lives is a question this build genuinely cannot answer, and a not_applicable row would read as a considered verdict on a question nobody asked. CAP-10C/D own adding the build and release modes, and they will need a dependency/toolchain model in the descriptor (a schema bump) before those rows can be honest.
  evidence: `docs/cli-contract.md` section 3 (the requirement-graph table and the paragraph stating the absence); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.doctor.pas` header; `TPWebCliMode` has one value, pdmSource.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION — npm is diagnosed by PRESENCE on every platform and never by version. On Windows its only entry point is npm.cmd, a batch file CreateProcess cannot execute; the only way to ask it for a version is cmd.exe, which this CLI does not have and, per the ratified no-shell rule, will not grow. Probing it on POSIX and not on Windows would make one check answer honestly on three targets and lie on the fourth, so no version claim is made anywhere. The `frontend.dependencies` row (node_modules present) is the optional companion that tells a developer whether `npm ci` still needs running. A future shard wanting an npm version must either resolve npm-cli.js and run it through node (path archaeology across nvm/volta/corepack layouts) or accept a Windows-only shell exception — both are decisions, not implementation details.
  evidence: measured on the dev host — the extension-less `npm` shell script is not a PE and fails CreateProcess, and npm.cmd exits nonzero when launched directly; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.doctor.pas` AddPresenceRow header.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION (POSIX) — the CLI carries filesystem paths as UTF-8 across its platform seam. On POSIX a path is bytes, so a project root or descriptor path whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 (a Latin-1 filename on an old volume) fails closed rather than being transcoded or guessed at. That is a deliberate fail-closed choice, recorded rather than hidden: the alternative is a locale-dependent conversion inside the one layer whose whole job is exact, kernel-verified path identity. A future shard that must support such trees needs a byte-preserving path type through pweb.cli.paths / pweb.cli.project, not a conversion.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` header, section PATHS; PWebStrictUtf8 is the shared validator the descriptor reader applies.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: RECORDED (test-fixture hazard) — FPC 3.2.2's RECURSIVE SysUtils.ForceDirectories returned False for a two-level creation inside the CAP-10A suite while succeeding for the identical call in a standalone program with the same unit paths, and while the same suite's other two-level calls worked. The failure is silent by nature and was diagnosed only because a confinement assertion then counted three missing segments instead of two — a fixture that fails to exist looks EXACTLY like a working refusal in a suite half of whose expected answers are "the path is not there". The fixture helpers now create one level at a time through a MakeDir that RAISES when a directory does not appear. Any future suite building fixture trees under the mORMot UTF-8 RTL regime should do the same rather than trust the recursive form.
  evidence: `test/cap10a/pweb.test.cli.pas` MakeDir; the standalone reproduction (same FPC, same unit paths, junction present in the parent directory) returned True where the suite returned False.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: DOCUMENTATION BUDGET — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` reaches 178 KB after the four CAP-10A step blocks, carried forward from CAP-8B, CAP-9A, CAP-9B1, CAP-9B2, CAP-9C1 and CAP-9C2. The per-platform split is still the obvious remedy and CAP-10A is NOT its owner: splitting the file rewrites every step whose line numbers ratified artifacts cite, mid-shard, for no CAP-10A gate, and it is on the ask-first list. CAP-11 is the natural owner — widening the CI matrix and adding the upstream watcher is that capability's entire subject, so it touches this file structurally by definition.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget hook reports 178.4 KB; the CAP-9C2 ledger entry named CAP-10 as the natural owner, and this entry re-assigns it with the reason.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10a-cli-foundation.md`
  summary: CAP-10B HANDOFF — schema 1 fixes the inputs a generator needs and deliberately stops there. bundleId is the single application-identity anchor; the Windows Inno AppId and the macOS CFBundleIdentifier must be DERIVED from it by a rule CAP-10B/D writes down and cross-checks, never invented from a display name. The Pascal program identifier and the executable base name are already fixed (the basename of native.program without its final extension, matching the lowercase-alphanumeric grammar). What schema 1 does NOT carry, and what a generator will need first: dependency locations (mORMot, the webview library, static assets), the Windows profile selection (normal/offline/fixed), the QuickJS plugin input set, and the macOS bundle metadata. Each is a schema bump, not an optional field — schema 1 has no optional keys by design.
  evidence: `docs/cli-contract.md` sections 2 and 5; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.project.pas` header, SCHEMA 1 - EIGHT REQUIRED KEYS, NO OPTIONAL KEYS.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: MEASURED RESIDUAL (POSIX commit window), recorded rather than smoothed over — the creation transaction commits with a rename that must not replace. Windows uses `MoveFileExW` WITHOUT `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`, which is exclusive in the kernel and has no window at all. POSIX `rename(2)` has no portable no-replace form: `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` is Linux-only and `renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)` is Darwin-only, and linking either would pin a libc floor this project has not agreed. The destination is therefore lstat'ed immediately before the call, and the residual is exactly one case: an EMPTY directory created inside that window would be replaced. Nothing holding data can be, because rename onto a non-empty directory is ENOTEMPTY and onto a file is ENOTDIR, so the property that matters — creation never destroys existing user CONTENT — holds unconditionally on both families. Revisit when a libc floor is ratified for other reasons; the fix is two external-'c' declarations and a per-family branch in `PWebCliRenameDir`.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` `PWebCliRenameDir` (both bodies, with the reasoning in place); `docs/template-contract.md` section 10 "The one recorded residual"; the A8 corpus row proves the primitive refuses an existing destination and leaves both trees intact.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION (the fixture is not a frontend) — the CAP-10B0 fixture template declares `ui = react` because schema 1 requires exactly `react` or `pas2js` and a template must fill the field, but its content is deliberately NEUTRAL: plain HTML and JS, no framework, no package.json and no lockfile. So running the real `pweb doctor --project <generated>` against a fixture-generated project reports `frontend.lockfile: fail / package_absent` and exits 4. That is the DOCTOR being right, not the engine being wrong — a schema-1 react project genuinely does need a lockfile — and it is the honest shape of shipping an engine before a template. What the same run DOES prove, and is the reason it was run at all: `project.refusal = ok`, `name = demo`, `bundleId = com.example.demo`, `programIdent = demo`, `ui = react`, and all three path fields resolved. The frozen CAP-10A reader accepts a generated project and derives the identifier the generator planned. CAP-10B1 closes the row by shipping the real React template with its exact lockfile.
  evidence: dev-host run of `build/cap10a/bin/pweb.exe doctor --project <fixture-generated project> --json` — 13 rows, `project.refusal=ok`, the only required failure being `frontend.lockfile`; `tools/templates/templates.list` declares `ui = react` for the private fixture.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: CAP-10B1 AUTHORING CONSTRAINT — a doubled opening brace ANYWHERE in a text template opens a placeholder token. There is no escape sequence and no literal form, which is what makes "unknown token" and "unresolved token" hard failures rather than something that survives silently into somebody's new project. The cost is real and lands on CAP-10B1: JSX inline-style syntax is a doubled brace, so the React template must separate the braces with a space or keep styles out of JSX attributes. The alternative — passing unrecognised braces through — was rejected because it turns a typo in a token name into a permanent string in a generated project. If CAP-10B1 finds the constraint intolerable, the correct fix is a DIFFERENT delimiter for the whole contract (a template-pack schema bump), never a per-template exception.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.scaffold.pas` `PWebScaffoldRender` and the unit header; `docs/template-contract.md` section 7; corpus rows `R2 render unknown-token token_unknown` and `R2 render lowercase-token token_empty`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: RECORDED (defect found and fixed in-shard) — the first placeholder renderer appended one byte at a time to a managed string. `PWEB_TPL_FILE_MAX_BYTES` permits a 1 MiB text template, and byte-at-a-time appending reallocates on nearly every append: roughly 5e11 byte copies for a file the limits explicitly allow, which is a hang rather than a failure and would have been diagnosed as "CI is stuck". It was found by adversarial review rather than by a test, because every fixture file is about 1 KB and nothing in the suite exercised the bound. The renderer now grows its buffer by doubling and copies runs of ordinary bytes with one Move, and a corpus row (`R1 scale`) renders 512 KB with a token on every line so the linear behaviour is measured rather than argued. Any future engine work that walks bounded-but-large input should assume the bound will be reached.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.scaffold.pas` `PWebScaffoldRender` (the `Emit` nested procedure and its comment); corpus row `R1 scale input=... output=...`; the suite runs in 73 ms wall clock on the dev host with the row present.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: RECORDED (test-harness hazard, and a general one) — FPC does not define the evaluation order of an argument list, and MEASURABLY evaluates a `Check(Call(out x), Message(x))` message-first on this toolchain. The CAP-10B0 suite was written that way at first and read `code` and `detail` BEFORE the callee had set them, then indexed a code-text array with whatever the stack held; it faulted with an access violation rather than reporting a wrong verdict, which was luck and not design. Every assertion in the suite that names an out parameter in its message now binds the call to a local `ok` first. Any future suite in this repository should assume the same: a diagnostic message that reads an out parameter of the call it is describing is reading uninitialised memory.
  evidence: `test/cap10b0/pweb.test.template.pas` — the note in `TTestPWebTplPack.TrustedList` explaining the shape, and the `ok := ...; Check(ok, ...)` pattern throughout; the failing run reported `ExceptionOS EExternalException` inside `Substitution` and `Path rendering` before the fix.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (the exit-code mapping) — CAP-10B0 adds NO exit code. The engine returns typed refusal codes (`TPWebTplCode`, `TPWebScaffoldCode`, `TPWebCreateRefusal`) and CAP-10B1 ratifies how they map onto the public taxonomy when it exposes `pweb create`, so the frozen CAP-10A precedence 6 > 5 > 4 > 3 > 2 > 0 is untouched by this shard and `cli_exit_taxonomy` stays green by construction rather than by re-measurement. The open question CAP-10B1 must answer: whether a creation refusal is a new category (a 7) or reuses 2/3/4 — "the destination already exists" fits none of the existing descriptions well, and inventing a category is a visible act that belongs with the command it serves.
  evidence: `docs/template-contract.md` section 5, last bullet; `docs/cli-contract.md` section 4 is unchanged; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.write.pas` `TPWebCreateRefusal` has twelve values and no exit code among them.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (a shared-guard carve-out, not yet needed) — the CAP-7L Linux job guards that EVERY committed shell script carries mode 100755, because its own gates are invoked as programs and a 100644 script fails with "Permission denied" before a single assertion runs. Template sources are shell scripts too, and `tools/templates/fixture/tools/check.sh` therefore had to be committed executable. Today that is also CORRECT — the registry declares that file `mode = executable`, so the source mode and the generated mode agree — but they are INDEPENDENT facts: the builder reads only the bytes, and the generated mode comes from the registry alone. The day a template needs to ship a shell script with `mode = normal`, the repository guard will force the source to +x and the two will disagree, which is confusing rather than wrong. The fix at that point is a `tools/templates/**` carve-out in the Linux guard with this reason attached; doing it now would weaken a total rule for a case that does not exist.
  evidence: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` Linux job, "Guard - no floating upstream ref in the Linux build/gate path" (the executable-bit half); `tools/templates/templates.list` declares `mode = executable` for `tools/check.sh`; hosted run 33093107727 failed that guard before the bit was set, which is the guard working.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: DOCUMENTATION BUDGET — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` reaches 184.6 KB after the four CAP-10B0 step blocks, up from the 178.4 KB the CAP-10A entry recorded. The per-platform split remains the obvious remedy and CAP-10B0 is NOT its owner, for the identical reason CAP-10A was not: splitting the file rewrites every step whose line numbers ratified artifacts cite, mid-shard, for no CAP-10B0 gate. CAP-11 remains the natural owner — widening the CI matrix and adding the upstream watcher touches this file structurally by definition. Recorded here so the growth is visible per shard rather than discovered later as one large number.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget hook reported 180.6, 182.0, 183.3 and 184.6 KB across the four job-block edits; the CAP-10A ledger entry above re-assigned ownership to CAP-11 with the same reasoning.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: CAP-10B1 HANDOFF — the engine is frozen and the pack contract is schema 1; what CAP-10B1 adds is a template and a command, in that order. Concretely: (1) a `react` template block in `tools/templates/templates.list` with `visibility = public`, its own tree under `tools/templates/react/`, and an EXACT `package-lock.json` so `pweb doctor` on a generated project is green; (2) a release pack built with `--include public`, which is already implemented and already proven to refuse an empty selection; (3) the `create` command in `pweb.cli.args.pas` and a dispatch arm in `pweb.pas`, which is the first moment the engine is linked into the CLI at all — and the moment `test/cap10b0/check_cap10b0_contracts.ps1` rule 1 must be INVERTED rather than deleted, so "the engine is linked" becomes as measured as "it is not"; (4) the exit-code mapping (see the entry above); (5) the doubled-brace authoring constraint (see the entry above). What CAP-10B1 must NOT need to touch: the pack format, the registry shape, the identity mapping, the placeholder set, the transaction, or any bound.
  evidence: `docs/template-contract.md` sections 1-11 (the frozen contract); `tools/pweb/pwebtemplates.pas` `--include` is implemented and gate B7 proves `--include public` refuses today; `test/cap10b0/check_cap10b0_contracts.ps1` rule 1 names the CLI unit set it measures.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b0-scaffold-engine.md`
  summary: MEASURED, and it CORRECTS a Checkpoint-1 decision — the template pack is deterministic PER OS FAMILY, not across all four targets, because mORMot stamps the creating operating system into the ZIP "version made by" field: `ZIP_OS = ( {$ifdef OSDARWIN} 19 {$else} 3 {$endif} ) shl 8`, one byte per entry, which mORMot's own reader then explicitly ignores. Checkpoint 1 ratified `template_pack_digest` as a four-target equality field on the strength of a dev-host measurement across the i386-win32 and x86_64-win64 toolchains, which produced byte-identical archives. That measurement was sound and the inference from it was not: both toolchains are Windows, and it was read as proving OS-independence. Hosted run 33093385300 measured the truth — windows-x86_64 and linux-x86_64 both produced `65180990...3993ea`, macos-x86_64 and macos-arm64 both produced `55cd65bf...b5e0cc`, all four with the SAME length (6177 bytes) and the SAME semantic inventory `61f381c0...e90259`. Two different CPUs agreeing exactly is what shows this is an OS-family property and not a per-machine one. The evidence now follows the CAP-9C1 split, reached by measurement rather than inherited by assumption: the semantic inventory is compared across four targets, the pack SHA-256 and the registry digest are pinned per target and reported side by side, and determinism is proved where it is real — the gate rebuilds the pack on each target and requires byte equality there as a must-PASS field. Two corpus lines that embedded the registry digest were removed for the same reason, so the decision corpus carries only content-derived values.
  evidence: hosted run 33093385300 — all four platform jobs green, `cap7 aggregate` refusing with exactly three FIELD DISAGREES rows (`template_digest`, `template_pack_digest`, `template_registry_digest`) and nothing else; `deps/mormot2/src/core/mormot.core.zip.pas` `ZIP_OS` and `ZIP_VERSION`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.template.pas` DETERMINISM header; `docs/template-contract.md` section 3; the equality-list comment in `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-9-cap9c2-quickjs-gui-release.md`
  summary: RESOLVED (a latent CAP-9C2 harness defect, found by a CAP-10B0 docs commit and fixed under it) — the G7 real-concurrency gate parks a plugin invocation inside a service barrier until its UI peer arrives, and a plugin's `pweb.invoke` is itself bounded by `InvokeWaitMs`. The two bounds were INVERTED: `BARRIER_WAIT_MS = 20000` against `PWEB_QUICKJS_DEFAULT_LIMITS.InvokeWaitMs = 15000`. A slow rendezvous therefore tripped the plugin's defensive completion cap FIRST, and the gate reported `plugin_result_isolated code=threw detail=PWebError: Internal error` — a runtime-internal-error signature for what was really "the peer was late", which sends the next reader into the wrong half of the system. The cap's own comment says it is "unreachable in a correct runtime", and that is true of the RUNTIME; it is not true of a harness that deliberately parks a completion behind a peer, which is the one condition the cap was never meant to meet. `BARRIER_WAIT_MS` is now 8000, comfortably inside the plugin's 15000 so that a late peer always fails as `concurrent_overlap` — the row that names the actual condition — and `RunGate` now REFUSES TO START if the ordering is ever inverted again, as a raise rather than a gate row so the frozen CAP-9C2 corpus digest is not re-baselined for a fact about the harness file.
  evidence: hosted run 33099868501 attempt 1 (windows) failed `plugin_result_isolated`/`host_exit_zero (exit=1)` on a DOCS-ONLY commit (438d76e changes four Markdown files and CAP-9C2 reads none of them); attempt 2 on the identical commit was green and produced the frozen `quickjs_gui_digest 67e08c692f2290e16721ba9c866162f3b5ac0f17338236977917d8a25a967b36`, so nothing behavioural had changed - only the order in which two timeouts could fire. `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas` BARRIER_WAIT_MS and the RunGate bound-ordering guard.
  lesson: two timeouts that can both cover the same wait are one timeout and one misleading error message. Whenever a harness makes an operation wait on a peer, the harness's own bound must expire FIRST, or every failure is reported by the layer that was merely collateral. The same family as the ledgered CAP-9C2 render-wait lesson ("bound the wait by the clock, not by an attempt count") - and this one shows the clock is not enough on its own: the bounds also have to be ORDERED, and the ordering has to be enforced rather than remembered.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: DESIGNED AND NOT EXPOSED (`--output`) — CAP-10B0 designed `--output` to name the destination's PARENT, and CAP-10B1 does not ship it. The reason is that B0 ratified the option's MEANING but neither a default nor a gate over it, so exposing it now would ship two destination rules on the strength of one measurement. CAP-10B1 therefore ships one: `NAME` inside the captured working directory, read once through the single CAP-10A CWD seam. The engine already takes the parent as a parameter (`PWebCreateProject(ParentDir, Name, ...)`), so adding the option later is a parser change and a gate, never an engine change. The shard that needs it should also decide whether the parent may be created — CAP-10B0's answer was no, because creating an intermediate chain is an unbounded mutation the commit cannot undo.
  evidence: `docs/template-contract.md` section 5, second bullet; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.args.pas` has no `--output`; `tools/pweb/pweb.pas` `RunCreate` canonicalizes `StartDir` and passes it as the parent.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: KNOWN LIMITATION (the SDK root is half-staged) — `test/cap10b1/prove_cap10b1.*` compiles the generated project against a STAGED PWeb SDK root (`build/cap10b1/sdk/share/pweb/src` plus the staged TypeScript SDK), which is what proves no PWeb path in a generated project is repository-relative. mORMot and the platform webview library are still resolved from `deps/`. That is not an oversight: schema 1 carries no dependency model, `docs/template-contract.md` section 4 records exactly which paths a PWeb host compile needs, and CAP-10D owns packaging them into a distributable SDK root. What CAP-10B1 does prove is the half that is provable today — the FRAMEWORK is not vendored and is not reached through the checkout.
  evidence: `test/cap10b1/build_cap10b1.ps1` step 4 copies `src` into the staged root; `prove_cap10b1.ps1` builds its SDK unit paths from the staged tree alone and names `deps/mormot2` separately; `docs/template-contract.md` section 4.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: MEASURED (a freshly created project cannot be installed standalone, and the failure is loud rather than silent) — the generated `frontend/package.json` declares `"@pweb/runtime": "file:.pweb/sdk/typescript"`, and npm records that in the lockfile as `link: true` with a project-relative `resolved`. So npm LINKS it and never fetches it: point the target at nothing and `npm ci` still exits 0, having made a dangling link, and the failure arrives at the first typecheck as `Cannot find module '@pweb/runtime'`. The security property is therefore stronger than "it is pinned" — there is no code path on which a package registry answers for that name — and the ergonomic cost is one confusing step for a developer who runs `npm ci` before any PWeb build. The generated README now states exactly this, having first stated it wrongly. CAP-10C/CAP-10D remove the cost by staging the SDK as part of `dev`/`build`.
  evidence: measured on the dev host — `npm ci` with `.pweb` removed added 27 packages, exit 0, and `node_modules/@pweb/runtime` was a dangling link; `tsc -p` then reported TS2307. `test/cap10b1/prove_cap10b1.*` now resolves the link's REPARSE TARGET and requires it to be the staged SDK (`runtime_from_sdk_root`), because `Resolve-Path` does not follow a Windows junction and would otherwise compare a link with itself.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: CAP-10C HANDOFF (HMR, and the two things it will have to change here) — the React template is a Vite template on purpose, because `SPEC.md` names React/Vite and `phase-plan.md` has `pweb dev` running Vite HMR behind `pweb://app`. Two deliberate absences are CAP-10C's to fill: (1) `@vitejs/plugin-react` is not a dependency, because it exists for Fast Refresh and CAP-10B1 must not implement HMR — adding it is a template-pack supersession like this one, and its peers (`oxc-transform-react`, `@rolldown/plugin-babel`, `babel-plugin-react-compiler`) should be measured before they are accepted; (2) `vite.config.ts` pins fixed asset names and configures no dev server, so the dev flow is entirely CAP-10C's to add. The dev-only `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>` CSP allowance is already ratified in `docs/cli-contract.md` section 5 and pinned absent from production by `test/cap10a/check_dev_trust.ps1`.
  evidence: `tools/templates/react/frontend/package.json` has four devDependencies and no plugin; `docs/template-contract.md` section 12 records the Vite 8 vs Vite 7 measurement (70 lock entries and one install script against 94 and two).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (the three example hosts are not migrated onto `pweb.webview.host`) — CAP-10B1 adds the reusable host composition and deliberately leaves `examples/08-release/releaseapp.pas`, `examples/07-quickjs/quickjsapp.pas` and the CAP-8 harnesses composing themselves by hand. Migrating them would re-baseline `navigation_policy_digest`, `security_corpus_digest` and `quickjs_gui_digest` — three frozen closure values — for no CAP-10B1 gate, which is the opposite of what a freeze is for. The cost is real and should be paid deliberately: there are now two compositions of the same runtime, and only one of them is exercised by a generated project. CAP-10C or CAP-10D is the natural owner, in a shard whose gates already re-measure those three digests for its own reasons.
  evidence: `git diff --name-status` against the CAP-10B0 closure `a996751` reports exactly one change under `src/`: `A src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas`; `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` gains one allowlist row (30 directives) and every other row is re-ratified unchanged.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: RECORDED (`app.ready` is the STARTER's channel, not the framework's) — the four-target runtime gate needs `html`, `css`, `secure` and `handshake`, and none of them is observable natively. Rather than add a diagnostics hook to the runtime, the generated starter owns one zero-capability method that its page calls once with what it observed, and the generated host prints it as one deterministic line. That is the established `example.report` shape and it is a real part of the starter's first-run experience — but it IS scaffolding, and a developer deletes it with the demo. If CAP-10C ever adds a framework-level diagnostics channel, this is the row to revisit: the gate would then read the framework's channel and the starter would lose a method it does not need.
  evidence: `tools/templates/react/src/app.services.pas` `APP_METHOD_READY` and `TAppBridge.Invoke`; `test/cap10b1/prove_cap10b1.*` parses the `demo: ready {...}` marker and requires every flag.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: DOCUMENTATION BUDGET — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` reaches 190.0 KB after the four CAP-10B1 step blocks and the build reordering, up from the 184.6 KB the CAP-10B0 entry recorded. CAP-11 remains the owner for the same reason CAP-10A and CAP-10B0 recorded: splitting the file rewrites every step whose line numbers ratified artifacts cite, mid-shard, for no gate of this one. Recorded so the growth stays visible per shard.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget hook reported 185.6, 187.9, 189.8 and 190.0 KB across the job-block edits; the CAP-10A and CAP-10B0 ledger entries above assign ownership to CAP-11 with the same reasoning.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: OPEN MEASUREMENT (`create_help_digest` differs between Windows and POSIX, and nobody knows why yet) — `pweb create --help` renders to one SHA-256 on Windows (`98600c90…`, reproduced on the dev host and on the hosted Windows runner) and another on Linux and both macOS runners (`8f653529…`). The text is a compile-time constant, pure ASCII, LF-only and 1011 bytes on Windows, and `create_stdout_digest` — produced through the SAME `Emit` path in the same executable — agrees on all four targets. So the difference is in that one string rather than in the console seam, and neither a line-ending normalization, a missing final newline nor an extra one reproduces the POSIX value from the Windows text. CAP-10B1 therefore RECORDS the digest per target instead of comparing it, and emits `create_help_bytes` beside it so the next reader can tell a length change from a substitution without a hosted run. What is still asserted on every target is the help's CONTRACT — `create` advertised, `This build supports: react` present, `pas2js` absent, `dev`/`run`/`build` absent — and `advertised_ui` is parsed back OUT of the text and absolute-pinned to `react` by the aggregator, so a build that advertised a different frontend on one target still turns the matrix red.
  evidence: hosted run 33126638202, `build/cap10b1/cli-<target>.json` on all four targets; the equality-list comment in `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` records the demotion and its reason; `test/cap10b1/run_cap10b1_gates.ps1` section 1 carries the same note at the point of measurement.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`
  summary: RESOLVED, and worth keeping as a general lesson — two implementations of ONE projection disagreed because they disagreed about a COMPARER, not about the bytes they were measuring. `generated_tree_digest` came out `87999e1a…` from the PowerShell build proof and `1ca77cbb…` from its bash twin on the same generated tree, because PowerShell's default `Sort-Object` is culture-aware and case-insensitive while `LC_ALL=C sort` is bytewise: `App.tsx` sorts before `app.css` under one and after it under the other. A four-target equality field over that projection was unsatisfiable by construction. Every PowerShell sort that feeds a digest in `test/cap10b1/` is now `[Array]::Sort(..., [StringComparer]::Ordinal)` behind a named `SortOrdinal` helper, with the measurement written at the definition. The general rule for this repository: a digest whose ORDER comes from a comparer must name that comparer, because the default one is a property of the runner rather than of the data.
  evidence: hosted run 33126638202 — Linux `proof-linux-x86_64.json` recorded `1ca77cbb…` while its own `cli-linux-x86_64.json` recorded `87999e1a…` for the identical tree; after the fix both read `1ca77cbb…` on the Windows dev host, matching the bytewise value Linux had produced all along.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b1-react-scaffold.md`

The React smoke passed in the SAME step, the same step was green on the implementation commit minutes earlier, and a re-run of the identical commit was green. So the page never reported inside its auto-close window on one attempt, which is a timing observation and not yet a cause. CAP-10B1 changed no file under `examples/05-pas2js/`, no CAP-5 host and no scheduler, so this is pre-existing. The shape is familiar from the CAP-9C2 entry above — a bounded wait that can expire before the thing it is waiting for arrives — and the honest next step is to instrument the pas2js smoke's own wait rather than to raise a constant and hope. What must NOT be concluded from the green re-run: that the failure is gone. One green run is not evidence about an intermittent failure; it is only evidence that the failure is not deterministic.
  evidence: hosted runs 33129150289 (Windows red at that step, then green on `gh run rerun --failed` of the identical commit) and 33127976094 (the same step green on commit `35f288d`); `build/cap5/smoke-pas2js.log` is uploaded as `cap5-diagnostics` on the failing attempt.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: RATIFIED DEVIATION (the generated `.gitignore` differs between the two UIs) — the CAP-10B2 objective listed `.gitignore` among the files the two projects must share byte for byte, and the human ratified a difference at Checkpoint 1 instead. The React copy names `node_modules/` and the `/frontend/.pweb/` staging directory a build materialises the TypeScript SDK into, and explains both in a comment paragraph; a Pas2JS project has no package manager, no lockfile and no in-tree SDK staging at all, because its build stage is external. Shipping those bytes would ship a false statement about the project's own build, which is a worse defect than the one byte-identity was meant to prevent. `.gitattributes`, `src/app.services.pas` and the stylesheet ARE byte-identical and gated as such; `README.md` was already a permitted difference; `src/demo.lpr` differs only inside its header comment, and the stylesheet differs by one header sentence for the same honesty reason (the React copy names `App.tsx`, which a Pas2JS project does not have). The parity gates are POSITIVE allowlists — they require the files they name to match and say nothing about a file nobody has named — so what actually closes the gap is the exact-set check over the generated tree, in both directions, on every target.
  evidence: `test/cap10b2/check_cap10b2_contracts.ps1` section 2 (the byte-identical list, the comment-stripped `program.lpr` comparison and the 4-line raw-diff bound); `docs/template-contract.md` §13 records the deviation and its reason.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND WORKED AROUND (Pas2JS writes its output through the host's text layer) — the compiled `assets/app.js` begins with a UTF-8 BOM and carries CRLF on Windows, and LF on POSIX. MEASURED on the dev host: `EF BB BF` plus 1935 CRLF pairs on a freshly compiled generated frontend, and the committed CAP-5 `examples/05-pas2js/frontend/dist/assets/app.js` carries 2000 of the same. The compiler exposes no line-ending switch. Packing those bytes would make the Pas2JS `app.pwb` an OS-family artifact and `pas2js_static_inventory_digest` unsatisfiable across four targets by construction. The build stage therefore strips the BOM and converts CRLF to LF before anything is packed — the same decision, for the same reason, that already makes the CAP-5 build write `boot.js` byte-exactly with LF rather than through `Set-Content`. What is NOT settled by this shard: whether the normalised bytes agree across the four hosts for every other reason. If the hosted matrix says they do not, the ratified fallback is the CAP-10B0 `ZIP_OS` shape — pin the bytes per target, compare a semantic projection, and record the measurement — never quietly drop the field.
  evidence: `test/cap10b2/prove_cap10b2.ps1` and `.sh` section 4, and the `pas2js_output_normalised` row, which travels per target and records what each host's compiler actually emitted; the equality-list comment in `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` states the dependency at the point of comparison.

CAP-10B2 stages the PWeb Pas2JS SDK unit into that same root (`share/pweb/sdk/pas2js/pweb.native.pas`, named rather than copied wholesale so the SDK's own test program does not ship), and resolves the pinned Pas2JS COMPILER and its RTL from `deps/pas2js*` exactly as CAP-5, CAP-7L and CAP-7M2 already do. So the framework half of the claim is proven for both frontends and the toolchain half is proven for neither. CAP-10D owns packaging a distributable SDK root; recorded here so the gap stays one entry rather than two.

The dev-only loopback CSP allowance is already ratified in `docs/cli-contract.md` §5 and pinned absent from production by `test/cap10a/check_dev_trust.ps1`, and it applies to both frontends identically because the native host does not branch on frontend kind.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: FIXED, AND THE CLASS IS WORTH NAMING (`set -e` made three failure paths unreachable) — the adversarial review found that `prove_cap10b2.sh` captured `$?` after a bare simple command for the Pas2JS compile, the `app.pwb` build and the FPC compile. Under `set -euo pipefail` a failing simple command TERMINATES the shell, so `pas2js_frontend_build=FAIL`, `'the app.pwb build FAILED'` and `pas2js_native_build=FAIL` could never be written: a real failure would have surfaced as a bare compiler exit status with no evidence file at all. The same shape killed four `grep`-based command substitutions, and one of them was the branch this shard added specifically to keep an intermittent timing failure distinguishable from a runtime defect — so the mitigation for the shard's own carried-in risk was dead code on three of four targets. Every step that is ALLOWED to fail is now written in a condition context (`if cmd; then rc=0; else rc=$?; fi`) and every optional `grep` is `{ grep … || true; }`. The general rule for this repository: under `set -e`, `cmd` followed by `$?` reads a status the shell already acted on.
  evidence: `test/cap10b2/prove_cap10b2.sh` sections 3, 5, 6 and 7; the same idiom exists in `test/cap10b1/prove_cap10b1.sh` and is recorded in the entry below.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`

The defect is identical to the one CAP-10B2 fixed in its own twin and it is invisible on a green run, which is why it survived a closure. It is NOT fixed here because doing so touches a closed shard's proof on failure paths this shard cannot exercise; the fix is mechanical and named above. CAP-10C or CAP-10D is the natural owner, in a shard whose gates re-run that proof for their own reasons.
  evidence: `test/cap10b1/prove_cap10b1.sh` — `built=$?`, `typecheck=$?`, `native_built=$?`, and the `report="$(grep -o …)"` assignment.

The bundler's output was never opened, so the field could not disagree with its neighbour and a bundler that dropped or renamed an entry would have been invisible to it. Both proofs now OPEN the archive and project it in CAP-7F's own shape (`entry=<name> size=<n> sha256=<hex>`, bytewise-sorted), which also brings `manifest.json` — the entry the bundler owns and the dist never contained — inside the measurement. `pas2js_app_pwb_entries` travels beside it and is pinned to 5, so "the digest was computed over the dist again" is a named refusal rather than a silent equality.

The React starter could have grown or lost a report field with the parity claim still green and the aggregate still printing "same report shape". `prove_cap10b1.{ps1,sh}` now emits `report_fields` using the same ordinal projection, and the CAP-10B2 parity block requires the two sets byte-equal — a comparison between two measurements rather than between one measurement and a constant.

That is exactly how the CAP-10B1 POSIX proof kept a repository-relative platform path for a whole shard while its own header claimed every path was staged: reverting the fix would have left every gate green. And the run waited for the application with an unbounded `WaitForExit`/`wait`, so a hung page would have consumed the 30-minute CI step timeout and emitted no evidence at all — the exact "no report received" conflation this harness exists to prevent. The wait is now bounded at three times the auto-close window, after which the process is killed and the failure is named as a HUNG RUN.

Nothing breaks — `-text` means git converts nothing, so the committed LF bytes survive a Windows checkout — but a developer who edits the file on Windows can commit CRLF into a compiler configuration. The one-line fix is blocked by the parity gate: `.gitattributes` is byte-identical between the two templates and the React copy's bytes are pinned to the CAP-10B1 closure corpus, so adding `*.cfg text eol=lf` would re-baseline a frozen value. The same file also lists `*.ts`/`*.tsx` for a template the contract check forbids from ever containing TypeScript. The shard that next has human approval to move the React template should fix both.
  evidence: `tools/templates/pas2js/gitattributes`; the byte-identical list in `test/cap10b2/check_cap10b2_contracts.ps1`; the React corpus pin in `test/cap10b2/run_cap10b2_gates.ps1`.

What still does not exist is a test that executes it: the self-test legs all mutate an already-emitted evidence file and exercise the AGGREGATOR, never the harness's own report parser or its timeout path. The honest shape is the one the entry above describes for the CAP-5 smoke — feed the report parser a captured stdout with no `demo: ready` line and require the distinguishable cause — and it belongs with whoever finally instruments that intermittent failure.

So `template_native_code_digest`, `native_comment_diff_lines`, `app_raw_binding_hits`, `sdk_binding_declarations` and the rest are diagnostics in an artifact, not fields in the four-target comparison. That is not a hole in the claims — every one of them is ALSO enforced as a violation inside the check that computes it, and the check is must-pass on every target — but the header comments overstate what happens to the file. CAP-10B2 renamed the one genuinely dangerous case (`shared_native_source_digest` meant two different things in two artifacts of this shard; the contracts copy is now `template_native_code_digest`) and leaves the rest for the shard that decides whether these facts should travel.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: OBSERVED AND DIAGNOSED, SUPERSEDED BY THE RESOLVED ENTRY BELOW (the CAP-6b3 uninstall gate races the WebView2 browser processes it just spawned) — the Markdown-only commit that recorded the CAP-10B2 closure went red on the Windows runner at `CAP-6b3 fixed setup gates`, twice, on the identical commit. Gates 1 through 8 PASS on both attempts, including gate 7 (`the installed app selected the bundled runtime, OBSERVED 151.0.4129.78, spawned 1 browser image(s) INSIDE the bundled tree and returned 42`) and gate 8's two deliberate `FIXED RUNTIME REFUSED (step=tree_incomplete)` legs, which are the gate's own negative probes and NOT the failure. The failure is gate 9 at `test/cap6b3/run_fixed_setup_gates.ps1:477`: `uninstall left 11 file(s) behind: dxcompiler.dll, dxil.dll, ffmpeg.dll, mip_core_gn.dll, msedge_elf.dll` on the first attempt and `left 8 file(s): ffmpeg.dll, msedge_elf.dll, msedge.dll, msedgewebview2.exe, mspdf.dll` on the second. A DIFFERENT subset each time, every one of them a WebView2 browser image or one of its DLLs, is the signature of files still mapped when Inno Setup tried to remove them - Windows cannot delete a mapped image, the uninstaller reports success anyway, and the gate's 300-second poll for an empty directory then never converges. The gate runs three applications that each spawn a browser image inside the bundled tree and nothing waits for those processes to exit before it uninstalls. The likely fix is one step, not a timeout increase: drain (and if necessary kill) every `msedgewebview2.exe` rooted under the install directory before invoking the uninstaller, and only then poll. It is NOT applied here because CAP-6b3 is a closed shard outside this shard's ratified scope, because the race cannot be reproduced or verified on the dev host (it needs the real 689 MB fixed-runtime install into a Windows user profile), and because a harness change made blind to a race is how a flaky gate becomes a quiet one. What must NOT be concluded: that this is caused by CAP-10B2. The same step passed on three consecutive runs of this branch (33155622286, 33158296971, 33160209188), the CAP-6b3 steps run BEFORE any CAP-10 step in the Windows job, and the commit that first went red changed 376 lines of Markdown and one comment line in a self-test header.
  evidence: hosted run 33163186945 on commit `5dc1754`, Windows red twice at that step (original attempt and `gh run rerun --failed`), with the two differing leftover sets above; run 33160209188 green at the same step on the parent commit `13d9e68`; `test/cap6b3/run_fixed_setup_gates.ps1:459-480` is the uninstall leg and carries no process drain.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: RESOLVED (the CAP-6b3 uninstall/process-drain race, corrected in CI teardown only) — supersedes the OBSERVED entry above, whose measurements stand: hosted run 33163186945 on commit `5dc1754` went red twice on the identical commit with `uninstall left 11 file(s) behind: dxcompiler.dll, dxil.dll, ffmpeg.dll, mip_core_gn.dll, msedge_elf.dll` and then `left 8 file(s): ffmpeg.dll, msedge_elf.dll, msedge.dll, msedgewebview2.exe, mspdf.dll`. Gates 1-8 passed both times; the failure was gate 9. Gates 7 and 8 run three applications that each spawn a browser image INSIDE the bundled tree, nothing waited for those processes to exit, Windows cannot delete a mapped image, the Inno uninstaller reports success anyway, and the 300-second filesystem poll had become the only thing waiting for the browser to shut down. The correction adds `test/cap6b3/wv2procdrain.ps1` and calls it immediately before the uninstaller: enumerate through `Win32_Process` (ProcessId, ParentProcessId, ExecutablePath, CommandLine), select ONLY images whose canonicalised path lies under this installation on a COMPONENT boundary, give a bounded graceful window, terminate the remaining path-matched set BY PID with the image re-checked against a fresh record at kill time, re-enumerate until empty or a bounded timeout, and REFUSE to run the uninstaller at all if anything remains. The filesystem poll is unchanged and is now what it always should have been - a verification of cleanup - and its failure message re-enumerates the scoped set so residue-with-processes and residue-without are different sentences. NOTHING GLOBAL: `taskkill /IM`, `Stop-Process -Name` and any machine-wide WebView2 or Evergreen action are absent by rule and by test, because the runner's unrelated Evergreen processes are exactly what CAP-6b1, CAP-6b2 and gate 8's own no-fallback proof depend on. No CAP-6b3 production source, Fixed Runtime selection, WebView2 lock, Inno semantic, CAP-13 profile or CAP-10B2 file is touched.
  evidence: `test/cap6b3/wv2procdrain.ps1`; the drain call and the enriched residue diagnostic in `test/cap6b3/run_fixed_setup_gates.ps1` gate 9; `test/cap6b3/check_wv2procdrain.ps1` proves T1-T10 on injected records plus a live CIM query, and runs in the Windows job BEFORE the gate it protects.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: RESOLVED, AND KEPT AS A LESSON (`return ,$array` is wrong for a function consumed as `@(f ...)`) — the first draft of the drain returned `,$out.ToArray()`, the standard idiom for stopping PowerShell unrolling an empty array to `$null`. MEASURED: `@(f)` where `f` does `return ,$array` is **Count 1 for every input size**, because the comma's outer wrapper is what the pipeline unrolls and `@()` then collects the inner array as ONE item. In this function that meant an empty scope reported one match - in the code that decides which processes may be terminated. It was caught not by the unit tests, which used one-element inputs throughout, but by a throwaway live check against this dev host, which has 12 unrelated Evergreen browsers and reported `1 selected` for an install root that does not exist. The fix is `return $array` with `@()` at every call site: 0 for empty, N for N. Two tests now hold it: T1b selects from a MIXED set and asserts the count is 2, and T5c runs the real enumeration against a non-existent root and requires 0. The general rule for this repository: a unit test whose every fixture has one element cannot see a cardinality bug, and a selection function that decides what may be destroyed deserves a fixture with two.
  evidence: `test/cap6b3/wv2procdrain.ps1` `Select-PWebScopedProcess`, which carries the measurement at the point of the return; `test/cap6b3/check_wv2procdrain.ps1` T1b and T5c.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10b2-pas2js-scaffold.md`
  summary: RESOLVED, AND KEPT AS A LESSON (`Set-StrictMode` in a DOT-SOURCED PowerShell helper applies to the CALLER, not to the helper) — the first draft of `test/cap6b3/wv2procdrain.ps1` opened with `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, which reads as ordinary hygiene for a file whose job is deciding which processes may be terminated. But the file is dot-sourced by `test/cap6b3/run_fixed_setup_gates.ps1`, and strict mode is a SCOPE setting: sourcing it imposed strict mode on 590 lines of gate that had never run under it, with gates 9 and 10 still to execute AFTER the drain call. MEASURED with a standalone probe: a dot-sourced `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest` makes the sourcing script throw on its next uninitialised variable or missing property, i.e. the drain would have reddened the gate for a reason with nothing to do with process teardown. The helper needs no strict mode of its own - every property access in it is already inside try/catch, because an unreadable `ExecutablePath` must degrade to "not matching" rather than to an exception. Strictness now lives in `check_wv2procdrain.ps1`, where it is that script's own choice and affects only that script. Hosted run 33166770973 carried the defect and was cancelled before it could produce misleading evidence. The general rule for this repository: a dot-sourced helper may define functions and constants and nothing else - no `Set-StrictMode`, no `$ErrorActionPreference`, no `Set-Location`/`Push-Location`, no `$ProgressPreference` - because each of those is a side effect on a caller that never asked for it.
  evidence: `test/cap6b3/wv2procdrain.ps1` carries the reasoning where the `Set-StrictMode` line used to be; `test/cap6b3/check_wv2procdrain.ps1` T11 asserts the helper's executable text contains no `Set-StrictMode`, and T11b extends the same rule to the four other caller-visible preferences.

The resolution rule ratified for C1: canonical `node` executable → its directory D → Windows `D\node_modules\npm\bin\npm-cli.js`, POSIX `parent(D)/lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npm-cli.js`; probe as `node <npm-cli.js> --version` through the engine's probe profile; when the file is absent (Volta shims, unusual layouts) fall back to the present presence row with cause `npm_cli_unresolved`. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION (Windows Ctrl+Break) — the application is created in a new process group so a terminal's Ctrl+C reaches only the supervisor, which forwards it as WM_CLOSE; Windows never disables Ctrl+Break for a new group, so a Ctrl+Break typed at a real console also reaches the application and ends it through the C runtime's default handler rather than gracefully. Recorded, not worked around: the only remedy is a handler inside the generated host, which is beyond the ratified POSIX-only helper.
  evidence: `docs/supervision-contract.md` §7; the stop-signal driver deliberately delivers Ctrl+C (`CTRL_C_EVENT` to the console), which is the mechanism the contract promises.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: MEASURED LIMITATION (macOS supervisor SIGKILL) — a supervisor ended by SIGKILL forwards nothing, so on macOS the application outlives it; SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP are forwarded as a graceful stop and are what the S11 driver measures there. Linux covers the direct child with PR_SET_PDEATHSIG(SIGKILL) and Windows covers the whole tree with JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE (measured: tree gone 266 ms after TerminateProcess on pweb). A macOS equivalent would be a kqueue EVFILT_PROC/NOTE_EXIT watch on the parent inside the host — a host change beyond this shard's ratified helper, and CAP-10C1's to weigh if `pweb dev` needs it.
  evidence: `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` SupervisorTerminatedTreeDies and its per-target `s11_mechanism` observation.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: MEASURED (the auto-close smoke bound delays a requested stop) — while `PWEB_SMOKE_AUTOCLOSE_MS` or `--pweb-autoclose-ms` is armed, the reusable host's closer thread sleeps for the whole bound and the teardown joins it, so a stop requested meanwhile completes only when the bound expires: measured as five seconds of grace and then a forced termination when the CAP-10C0 stop driver first ran with the bound armed. `pweb run` never arms it and the drivers unset it; it is a test knob, and making the closer interruptible is a host change beyond the ratified helper. If CAP-10C1 wants a bounded dev run that also stops on request, the closer should wait on an event rather than Sleep.
  evidence: `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` DrivePweb (the UnsetEnv and its comment); `src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas` PWebHostAutoCloseThread.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`

The filter now carries the six spellings, `src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas` is re-ratified at 40 directives (was 30) and `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` at 36 (was 24), both with new fingerprints; no other row moved.
  evidence: `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` allowlist comments; local sweep PASS at 198 conditionals.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE CLOSURE (the drain's pass bound competed with its grace window) — the first drain bounded the graceful window and the forced re-enumeration by ONE pass counter; at 20 passes of 250 ms the 5000 ms grace window consumed every pass, so the forced branch killed the tree and reported the survivor it had just ended. S10 (a grandchild `forever` under a parent that exited at once) caught it on Windows, where the grandchild has no window to close. The grace window is now bounded by time and the forced window by passes counted from the kill; the injected-record cases assert the new arithmetic (13 passes for a 1000 ms grace at 250 ms plus 8 forced).
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.process.pas` PWebCliDrainTree; `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` DrainSelector and GrandchildDiesWithTree.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: RECORDED (a test-driver spawn option in the product seam) — `PWebCliChildSpawn` carries `SeparateConsole` (Windows: CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE), used only by the CAP-10C0 stop-signal driver so a console control event has a console of its own to travel through without reaching the suite that sent it. No public command sets it. It is the smallest honest way to drive a real Ctrl+C at a real `pweb run` on a hosted runner; the alternative was a driver that allocates a console for itself and loses its own standard handles.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` PWebCliChildSpawn header; `test/cap10c0/pwebchild.pas` ctrlbreak mode.

The per-platform split remains the remedy and remains assigned to CAP-11, for the reason every shard since CAP-9C2 has given: splitting rewrites every step whose line numbers ratified artifacts cite, mid-shard, for no gate of the shard doing it.
  evidence: the PostToolUse documentation-budget hook on this session's ci.yml edit.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: CAP-10C1 HANDOFF (what `pweb dev` gets from this shard, and what it must add) — the engine's supervise profile is the only thing a dev command should run a toolchain through: exact path, argv, explicit working directory, forwarded streams, stop propagation, bounded escalation, membership drain. Two things C1 must add on top: the npm-through-node resolution recorded above, and the single ratified `ws://127.0.0.1:<native-selected-port>` CSP allowance, which lives in the DEVELOPMENT configuration only and which `check_dev_trust.ps1` will keep refusing from the production profile. The engine's `Started` notification (the child pid) and the `StopCheck` hook are the seams a dev loop will use to restart a watcher without a second execution path.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.process.pas` TPWebCliExecSpec; `docs/supervision-contract.md` §4.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: REVIEW-DRIVEN FIXES, recorded so the reasoning survives (three independent reviewers over the diff, before closure) — (1) on POSIX a stream at EOF stayed in the poll set and answered HUP at once, so a child that closed its stdout but kept running would have spun the supervisor at full CPU and the drain's sleeps would have been no-ops: the platform seam now closes and forgets a descriptor the moment it reports EOF; (2) the supervisor ignores SIGPIPE for its own sake and an ignored disposition survives execve, so the application was receiving a signal table the contract said it would not: the forked child resets SIGPIPE to SIG_DFL and closes every descriptor above 2 but the exec-status pipe; (3) the host helper's installed flag was read in a finally it could reach uninitialised, a partial sigaction installation leaked the pipe, and removal reset dispositions to SIG_DFL instead of restoring what the embedding application had: all three fixed, the previous actions are saved and restored slot by slot; (4) a member appearing after the single forced kill was counted and marked but never ended: the tree is killed on every forced pass; (5) a child streaming faster than the supervisor reads could starve the exit, stop and bound checks: one pass reads at most 64 chunks per stream; (6) a sink that raised (a closed forwarding target) unwound past a running tree: the engine records it, stops forwarding, asks the child to stop, and its finally kills a still-running child on any abnormal unwind; (7) a Windows close/logoff/shutdown handler returned at once, and Windows ends the process the moment such a handler returns: it now waits (bounded, 4.5 s) for the engine's acknowledgement; (8) a directory or a non-executable file where the executable must be reached the spawn and answered 4: `layout_shape` and `layout_not_executable` answer 3 before any spawn; an unratified host answers `target_unsupported` (4) rather than "not built"; (9) the ECHILD reap path invented `SIGKILL`: it now carries the marker -1 and `run` says the application vanished; (10) `GetExitCodeProcess` and `ResumeThread` failures could read as success; (11) an unreaped child was excluded from its own drain count; (12) the doctor's outcome switch had no branch for a tool that died by a signal (`probe_died` now); (13) the gate's `run_dev_allowance_present` and `shutdown_order` rows were literals: the first is now the development-trust sweep's own verdict, the second the order the contract gate read out of the host's source; the contract gate's `global_name_kill_present` and `dev_build_absent` facts are derived from what the sweeps found; (14) six branches had no test: the probe's working directory and its typed death, the trailing fragment without a newline, a directory at the executable's path, the Linux PR_SET_PDEATHSIG guarantee (SIGKILL on the supervisor, measured on Linux only), and the forced and signal-death categories driven through the REAL `pweb run` with the fixture child standing in for the application (`PWEBCHILD_MODE`).
  evidence: the reviewer reports of 2026-09-02 (verification-gap, edge-case and blind layers); `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` cases DeathIsNeverExitZero, LinesInOrderAndBounded, WorkingDirectoryExplicit, LayoutRefusals, SupervisorTerminatedTreeDies, StopIgnoredIsForcedThroughRun, ApplicationDeathThroughRun.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (review findings judged real and out of this shard) — (a) a layout whose native path exceeds MAX_PATH is handed to CreateProcessW in display form and would fail as `supervision_unavailable`; the `\\?\` form for lpApplicationName and a prefix-free lpCurrentDirectory need their own measurement; (b) `test/cap10c0/run_cap10c0_gates.ps1` and every CAP-10 gate before it pass `Start-Process -ArgumentList` an array that pwsh joins without quoting, so a repository path with a space would split `--project`; no runner has one, and a fix belongs to all the gates at once; (c) the listening-socket sampler measures the APPLICATION pid, as the CAP-10B1/B2 proofs do, not every job / group member; a browser helper opening a socket is outside it, and a membership-scoped sampler is the honest upgrade; (d) `Get-CodeLines` in `check_cap10c0_contracts.ps1` is the CAP-10A shape and shares its blind spot (a brace inside a string literal, a `(* *)` comment); (e) on POSIX the graceful stop is SIGTERM to the whole group, so WebKit helper processes receive it in the same instant as the host that is starting its orderly teardown - the hosted R10 leg on Linux and macOS is the measurement of whether that races the CAP-9 order, and SIGTERM to the direct child first with the group in the drain is the fallback if it does; (f) a second Ctrl+C does not shorten the ladder (documented in the contract §7), and a host that arms the auto-close smoke bound still joins its closer thread before honouring a stop (ledgered above).
  evidence: the reviewer reports of 2026-09-02; `docs/supervision-contract.md` §7.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c0-run-supervision.md`
  summary: MEASURED (a generated host's stdout is block-buffered on a POSIX pipe until exit) — under `pweb run` the application's stdout is a pipe, and FPC's text layer flushes a pipe per line on Windows but block-buffers it on Linux and macOS, so the starter's `demo: ready {...}` line and the host's own diagnostics reach the supervisor only when the host exits (hosted run 33622404228: the CAP-10C0 stop drivers waited out their bound for a line that could not arrive, while the gate's own legs saw it after the auto-close). The drivers now arm on the pid `pweb` names (stderr, flushed per line) after a settle interval and accept the ready line only as an early trigger. The honest fix is a `Flush(Output)` after the starter's report and after the host's diagnostic lines — a template supersession (B1/B2 inventories) and a host change, both beyond this shard's ratified deltas; CAP-10C1, which will read a live host's output to drive a dev loop, is the natural owner. Until then, `pweb run`'s forwarded lines on Linux and macOS are exact but late for a host that never exits.
  evidence: `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` DriverArmed and PWEB_C0_DRIVER_SETTLE_MS; the Linux and macOS arm64 gate logs of run 33622404228.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: TEMPLATE SUPERSESSION (recorded, never claimed unchanged) — CAP-10C1 closes the CAP-10C0-ledgered POSIX stdout debt by adding `Flush(Output)` in three places: after the reusable host's stdout diagnostics (`src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas` PWebHostObserveOpen), after the starter's ready report (`src/app.services.pas` in BOTH public templates, which stay byte-identical and are measured so by `shared_native_source_digest`), and after the startup line in both `src/program.lpr`. FPC's text layer flushes a pipe per line on Windows and BLOCK-BUFFERS it on Linux and macOS, so before this change a supervisor read a host's lines only when the host exited — which CAP-10C2's dev loop cannot work with. The fields that MOVE are the ones that digest template BYTES: `generated_inventory_digest`, `react_generated_inventory_digest`, `pas2js_generated_inventory_digest`, `generated_tree_digest`, `pas2js_tree_digest`, `generated_file_count`, `generated_total_bytes`, `pas2js_generated_file_count`, `pas2js_generated_total_bytes`, `public_semantic_digest`, `public_file_count`, `template_digest`, `template_semantic_digest`, `template_file_count` and `shared_native_source_digest`. The fields that DO NOT move, and are re-measured rather than assumed: every `app.pwb` digest (the change is in NATIVE source, not in the frontend bundle), the `pweb.json` and lockfile digests, `cli_digest` and `doctor_schema_digest`. The exact before/after values are recorded in the CAP-10C1 final artifact from the closure run, because they are four-target measurements and this ledger entry is written before it.
  evidence: `src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas`; `tools/templates/react/src/app.services.pas` and `tools/templates/pas2js/src/app.services.pas` and both `src/program.lpr`; `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` DB1 (the ready report must arrive WHILE the host is alive).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE CLOSURE (a resolved path is not an argument) — this CLI canonicalizes Windows paths into the extended-length long-path form because that is the headroom the confinement walk needs, and `pweb.cli.platform` already strips it for the executable, for argv[0] and for the working directory. It does NOT strip it from the OTHER arguments, which are the caller's strings — and a tool handed one does not necessarily understand it. MEASURED on the dev host: `node` given the prefixed path to `npm-cli.js` dies inside `realpathSync` with `Error: EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, lstat 'C:'`, because Node splits the prefixed path and asks the filesystem about the drive letter alone. The whole first end-to-end run refused at stage 2 with `tool_probe_failed` for that reason. Every path the pipeline puts into an argument now goes through `PWebCliArgPath` (display form; identity on POSIX), and `pweb.cli.pipeline`'s RunStage REFUSES to spawn a vector that still carries the prefix (`arg_longpath_form`, exit 6) — so a forgotten conversion is a typed refusal rather than a mystery inside somebody's tool. The same defect had a second face: the evidence's redaction prefixes were the canonical forms only, so the first driver report carried absolute paths in every `cmd.*` row; `PWebCliPipeRedactions` now registers BOTH forms, canonical first because the display form is its suffix.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.stage.pas` PWebCliArgPath; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.pipeline.pas` RunStage; the driver report, whose `cmd.*` rows carry only the logical tokens for node, the npm entry point, the bundler, fpc, the SDK root and the project root.

  summary: RESOLVED (the CAP-10B1 "mORMot and the webview library are still resolved from deps/" limitation) — the staged SDK root now carries `share/pweb/deps/mormot2/src`, `share/pweb/deps/mormot2/static/<fpc-target>` and `share/pweb/lib/<os>-<arch>/`, and the pipeline reads mORMot, the statics, the webview library and the macOS Cocoa bridge object from there and from nowhere else. Two measurements decided the staging rule. (1) Only the eight unit directories `pweb.cli.native` names plus the include root's own files are staged, never the whole checkout: an installation ships what a project compiles against, and a staging rule that copies whatever happens to be there will one day ship somebody's test. (2) `static/delphi` IS staged on Windows, and only there, because two of the objects a Win64 build links are reached from INSIDE mORMot's own sources by a relative path — `sha512-x64sse4.obj` and `crc32c64.obj` under `../../static/delphi` — which resolves against the STAGED tree; staging only `static/<fpc-target>` produced `Error: Can't open object file`. On Windows the staged mORMot is the CAP-3U-PATCHED source plus its `x64callmethod.obj`, applied once at staging time and asserted (the staged `mormot.core.interfaces.pas` must DIFFER from the pristine pinned source and the object must be present), so the build path never runs `tools/patch-cap3u.ps1` and never edits a framework checkout.

The human ratified this at Checkpoint 1 with one rider, honoured: the C1 corpus records `npm_cli_path` and `npm_version` as typed observations, so the presence-only row is not the only trace of the rule.
