- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: RESOLVED (the auto-close smoke bound delayed a requested stop) — the CAP-10C0 ledger measured the reusable host's closer thread sleeping for the whole `PWEB_SMOKE_AUTOCLOSE_MS` bound while the teardown joined it, so a stop requested meanwhile completed only when the bound expired: five seconds of grace and then a forced termination in the first C0 stop-driver run. `PWebHostAutoCloseThread` now waits on a `TSynEvent` with the same bound, and the teardown sets that event immediately after `webview_run` returns and before the join. Semantics-neutral by construction and ratified as such at Checkpoint 1: the event is only ever set AFTER `webview_run` has returned, so no auto-close that would have happened stops happening, and the existing `InterlockedExchange(HostAutoCloseHandle, nil)` still guarantees exactly one terminate dispatch. Measured locally: with a 12 000 ms bound the host still exits at 12.6 s with a clean exit and status 0.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: RECORDED (the compiler's DEFAULT target is checked only where it decides anything) — `pweb.cli.toolset` probes `fpc -iTO` and `-iTP` on every platform and records the answer, but REFUSES a mismatch on Linux and macOS only. On Windows one FPC installation routinely carries both the i386 and the x86_64 compiler and the default is whichever the installer wrote last, while every Windows compile in this repository — the harness's and now the pipeline's — names `-Px86_64 -Twin64` explicitly, so the default decides nothing there. MEASURED on the dev host, whose `fpc` on PATH defaults to win32/i386 and which nevertheless builds and runs the whole pipeline: `pipeline_fpc_target=i386-win32` is recorded beside a green run. On POSIX no `-P`/`-T` is passed, the default IS the target, and a compiler that answered something else would produce an executable the `<os>-<arch>` layout names and the host can never launch — so there it is a refusal.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolset.pas` PWebCliResolveToolset; `test/cap10c1/build_cap10c1.ps1`, which checks the SELECTED target rather than the default for the same reason.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: CAP-10C2 HANDOFF (what a dev loop gets from this shard, and what it must add) — the pipeline is complete and private: `PWebCliRunPipeline` takes an opened project and a notify sink and produces the ratified run layout through the C0 engine, with typed per-stage outcomes, the mutation gate and the temp-and-rename commit. C2 calls it from `pweb dev` and adds three things it deliberately does not have: a file watcher, the Vite dev server behind the existing `pweb://app` handler, and the single ratified `ws://127.0.0.1:<native-selected-port>` CSP allowance, which lives in the DEVELOPMENT configuration only and which `check_dev_trust.ps1` keeps refusing from the production profile. Two seams are already there for it: the flush closed in this shard makes a host's lines arrive LIVE on a POSIX pipe, which is what a watcher must react to, and the auto-close closer now honours a requested stop at once. What C2 must NOT reuse is the pipeline's stage ORDER: a dev loop reruns the frontend stages without the pack, the native compile or the layout, and the resumability this pipeline is shaped for but deliberately does not perform is C2's decision to make explicit.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.pipeline.pas` TPWebCliPipeNotify and the ten stage kinds; `docs/pipeline-contract.md`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: REVIEW-DRIVEN FIXES, recorded so the reasoning survives (three independent reviewers over the diff, before closure) — (1) the POSIX emitter's new `c1_str`/`c1_num` helpers shipped a raw 0x01 byte where the sed backreference belongs and a literal two-character `\n` where a line continuation belongs, so all three POSIX legs would have read a file named `n` and emitted empty CAP-10C1 fields; the same edit had renamed the CAP-6 `app_pwb_react_sha256` output row, producing a duplicate JSON key and dropping a required field from the POSIX evidence only. (2) `build_deterministic` compared the second run's archive with itself, because the ST2 re-run happened before the hashes were taken: the first run's digest is now captured before anything re-runs. (3) `project_tree_unchanged` could not read false - `TreeAfter` was seeded from `TreeBefore` and only re-assigned on the path that had already proved them equal - so the row that proves a build does not touch its own sources was satisfied by construction; `TreeAfter` now starts EMPTY and the gate takes its own before/after digest of the read-only half, the shape it already used for the SDK root. (4) a verification failure in the layout stage left the COMMITTED release on disk, because the rename had already happened; it is now reclaimed, and stage 10 re-resolves INDEPENDENTLY rather than re-reading the field stage 9 already refused on, which had made it unreachable. (5) the registry-override lock FAILED OPEN on an unreadable or over-large tree - the trees most worth checking - and restated the writable set as literals instead of deriving it from the descriptor, so a project whose `frontend.root` is not `frontend` would have walked its own node_modules and refused on an .npmrc a dependency ships; it now takes the derived set, reports `Unreadable` as its own refusal, and compares names ASCII-case-insensitively because npm reads `.NPMRC` on a case-folding volume. (6) the react `build` stage did not check its own output, so a project whose vite.config.ts writes elsewhere failed opaquely in the bundler one stage later. (7) `pipeline_units_linked` was a literal in the gate rather than the contract gate's own measurement, which made an absolute pin guard a constant. (8) the host's closer read the event global twice where the teardown nils and frees it; one local read closes that. (9) the Pas2JS normalisation wrote into its own input alias, correct only by copy-on-write timing. (10) an interrupt between the layout commit and the verification was unobserved, and the stage signal number never reached the record.
  evidence: the reviewer reports of 2026-09-02 (blind and verification-gap layers); `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` ST5, ST11 and the DB4 leg; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.layout.pas` the plrVerify reclaim; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.frontend.pas` PWebCliRegistryOverridePresent.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a membership sampler can starve the application it samples) — the first CAP-10C1 gate sampled listeners and connections for every member of the tree on every 400 ms pass. MEASURED on the dev host: a WebView2 tree has SEVEN members, so one pass issued about fifteen `Get-NetTCPConnection` / `Get-NetUDPEndpoint` enumerations, and the load made a healthy Pas2JS run miss its own 20 s auto-close window and be killed by this very gate as a HUNG RUN - while two isolated runs of the same layout exited cleanly at 12.58 s twice. Membership is still enumerated on every pass, the sockets every fifth, and the run bound is 180 s rather than the CAP-10C0 gate's 90 because this gate samples a TREE where that one sampled a pid. A bound a slow sampler can reach is a bound that reports a healthy run as hung.
  evidence: `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` RunApplication; `test/cap10c1/listener_members.ps1`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: DEFERRED (review findings judged real and out of this shard) — (a) the toolset's own `tool_inside_project`, `tool_not_found` and `probe_failed` branches are shadowed by the CAP-10A doctor run that precedes them and emits the same causes, so the TC2/TC3 legs assert the doctor's verdict rather than the toolset's; the outcome is correct and the causes identical, but the duplication costs one extra probe per tool per build and deserves collapsing when CAP-10C2 needs the resolution without a full requirement graph. (b) The layout's `plrCommit` rollback - putting the old release back rather than leaving a project with neither - is a handled partial-failure path no gate exercises. (c) `PWebCliSdkManifest` re-emits a JSON string from its SOURCE bytes, which is byte-exact for the pinned SDK manifest and for every manifest without a redundant escape, but would differ from `JSON.stringify` for `\/`, for `\u0041` and for uppercase hex in a `\u` escape; the ST1 gate compares against the real script on four targets on every leg, so a drift is caught rather than assumed away, and a narrower reader is the honest follow-up. (d) `app.pwb` and `node_modules` are each spelled in two units, and nothing asserts the pairs equal. (e) `arg_longpath_form` and `pipeline_mutation` are production refusals no test seeds. (f) The `Flush(Output)` fix is applied per call site; a line-buffering setup would make the property hold for lines nobody has written yet.
  evidence: the reviewer reports of 2026-09-02.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (an SDK that stages only the SHIPPED library name produces a release nobody can link) — the first hosted CAP-10C1 run failed the native compile on all three POSIX targets, and the reason is a distinction the Windows leg does not have. On Linux `tools/build-webview-so.sh` produces TWO files: `libwebview.so.0.12`, the SONAME a release ships and DT_NEEDED records, and `libwebview.so`, the dev-only LINK-TIME name FPC's `external 'libwebview.so'` makes the linker resolve. On macOS there are THREE - the versioned name `LC_LOAD_DYLIB` records through `@rpath`, the plain `libwebview.dylib` that `-k-lwebview` resolves, and the real file both of those are. The staging rule copied only the shipped name, so `-Fl<sdk>/share/pweb/lib/<target>` pointed at a directory the linker could find nothing in. The SDK's lib directory now carries every name the target's dist provides; the LAYOUT still copies only the one a release ships, which is what `PWebCliWebviewLibName` returns. Windows has one name for both jobs and never showed the difference.
  evidence: hosted run 33670746536, `CAP-10C1 lifecycle pipeline gates` on linux-x86_64, macos-x86_64 and macos-arm64; `test/cap10c1/build_cap10c1.sh` lib_files.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a gate that echoes only the supervisor's lines throws away the compiler's) — the driver forwards a stage's own output to stdout and writes only its `pweb: `-prefixed lines to stderr, and the first CAP-10C1 gate printed stderr alone. So hosted run 33670746536 reported `compile: FAILED stage_exited 1` on three targets and not one word of what the compiler said, and the cause had to be re-derived from the build scripts rather than read. The gate now prints the stage transcript whenever a pipeline exits nonzero, and the CI step uploads `pipe-*-stdout.txt` beside the stderr it already kept. A failure that cannot be read from its own evidence is a failure that costs a hosted run to diagnose.
  evidence: `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` ShowPipe; the four `pipe-*-stdout.txt` entries in the CAP-10C1 upload step of every native job.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a green suite verdict over nothing at all) — the CAP-10C1 gate invoked its headless suite as `c1tests /noenter` on every platform. `/noenter` is the WINDOWS switch that skips mormot.core.test's interactive ENTER wait; the POSIX runner has no such wait and reads the argument as the FILENAME to redirect console output to. MEASURED on hosted run 33672783286: the Linux suite printed its usage, ran not one case, exited 0 — and the gate recorded `pipeline_suite: PASS` beside `pipeline_corpus_lines: 0`, with a `pipeline_digest` over a file containing only its header comment. The four-target equality would have caught the digest divergence, but only after the fact and without naming the cause. CAP-10A and CAP-10C0 had already ratified the rule (`/noenter` on Windows, NO argument on POSIX) and this gate simply did not follow it. Two things changed: the invocation, and a guard neither of those shards has — the corpus must carry at least one decision, because an exit code alone cannot tell "every assertion held" from "the runner printed its usage and stopped". The corpus and the observation file are also removed before the suite runs, so a stale one can never be read as this run's answer.
  evidence: hosted run 33672783286, `build/cap10c1/cli-linux-x86_64.json` (`pipeline_suite: PASS`, `pipeline_corpus_lines: 0`) and its `c1tests.log`, which holds the runner's usage text; `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` section 1.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c1-lifecycle-pipeline.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a BUILD was refusing a RUN-TIME requirement) — the pipeline adopts the CAP-10A requirement graph's verdict, and the first implementation adopted EVERY required row that failed. `platform.webview` asks whether this machine can DISPLAY a WebView - the Evergreen runtime on Windows, WebKitGTK on Linux, the WebKit framework on macOS - which is what `pweb run` needs and not what a compile needs: a compiler needs the binding SOURCES, and those are in the SDK root. MEASURED on hosted run 33674212855: both macOS pipelines refused at stage 2 with `framework_absent`, on the very runners where the CAP-10B1 and CAP-10B2 harnesses build and run the same two projects successfully, and where the CAP-10B2 gate already records `doctor_host_failures_react: platform.webview/framework_absent` as an observation rather than a failure. The row is still RESOLVED and now travels in the corpus as `doctor_platform_webview` (`pass/ok` on Windows), so nothing is hidden; it simply does not block a compile. Every other required row still does, with the doctor's own cause. The gate's project-mutation assertion was scoped in the same commit: a pipeline that refused at stage 2 has re-measured nothing, and requiring its `project_tree_unchanged` row to read true was requiring a measurement that had not been taken.
  evidence: hosted run 33674212855, `cap10c1-pipeline-macos-arm64` gates log; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolset.pas` FirstRequiredFailure and PWEB_CLI_ROW_WEBVIEW.

MEASURED on hosted run 33676507937: 45 corpus lines on Linux and 46 on both macOS targets, and therefore two different `pipeline_digest` values for a field the aggregate compares across four. The resolver is a pure function of a directory tree plus (Os, Arch) - building the fixture is creating directories and files, which no platform makes special - so the case now runs against macos-arm64 on EVERY host, chosen because it is the richest ladder. Windows now records the same 46 lines and the same digest `f890424a5ef95646b9a48355819e32f49e608c7f5ecc4e69766b4182978e839a` the two macOS targets measured. The general rule this keeps: a corpus line may describe a RULE and never the machine that ran it, and a case that reads `PWebCliHostOs` is a case writing the machine into the corpus.

Four ambient declarations cost one file and keep a Node surface out of a typecheck that also covers `src/`, where a filesystem import would be a mistake nobody caught until it failed inside a WebView. `frontend/tsconfig.json` includes it, and `src/program.lpr` selects `pweb.webview.devhost` under a PWEB_DEV conditional. The generated React inventory moves from `ef5c09d08f69a62f7603eddd6d49a9762fa602f81fe94f8e0e2db5a470505917` / 15 files / 66355 bytes to `ef9a9312f3d18efd80dd10bab5baa0774454ee99263b5f06427d01e753d5a617` / 16 files / 71416 bytes. The Pas2JS template is untouched.

  summary: MEASURED AND RECORDED (model A was refused on data, not on preference) — serving Vite's own module graph behind `pweb://app` was spiked against the real dev server on the real generated project, and refused on two findings that are each a relaxation of a frozen layer: `/src/app.css` is 2563 bytes of `text/javascript` while `/src/app.css?direct` is 1938 bytes of `text/css` — one path, two bodies — so model A needs `PWebParseAppUri` to preserve and forward the query; and `.tsx` and `.css` modules are served `text/javascript` while `PWebAssetMimeType` derives `application/octet-stream` from `.tsx`, so the MIME type would have to come from the proxied response. A third finding is recorded because it is what an HMR shard has to solve first: the HMR client derives its socket host from the page URL, giving an unusable one under `pweb://app`, and with no `@vitejs/plugin-react` in the template there is no Fast Refresh, so a `.tsx` edit already ends in a full reload. The ratified WebSocket data-channel allowance therefore stays RATIFIED, UNUSED and pinned ABSENT from every profile on every leg.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap10c2-model-a-spike.md`; `docs/cli-contract.md` section 5; `test/cap10a/check_dev_trust.ps1` sections 2 and 5.

Whether a re-navigation re-requests SUB-RESOURCES is therefore an engine property. MEASURED on the dev host (windows-x86_64): three generations published, three `generation N loaded` acknowledgements, and the page re-reported css/secure/value=42 after every switch — including after a STYLE-only edit, which is the sub-resource case. The other three targets are measured by the first hosted run. If a target serves a stale sub-resource the in-scope fallback is a query on the same origin — the query is cut by `PWebParseAppUri` and maps to `index.html`, the origin is unchanged, and no grammar moves; if that too is insufficient somewhere the shard reports it rather than restarting the host, which would fail the acceptance criterion the whole design exists for.
  evidence: `build/cap10c2/devdrv-report.txt` (`dev3_styles_applied`, `dev2_host_pid_unchanged`); the Windows no-store header in the frozen adapter.

The fix is the ambient-declaration file recorded in the template supersession above rather than `@types/node`, and the reason is not size: the same typecheck covers `src/`, where a PWeb frontend has no Node runtime under it and a filesystem import would typecheck cleanly and fail inside a WebView. The general rule: a build-configuration dependency must not become a frontend-source dependency by sharing one typecheck.

DEV5 now types five edits 50 ms apart from the engine's own stop-check callback and rewrites the template's `SUM_B`, so the claim is the PAGE's arithmetic rather than a comment nobody can see: `value` is 20 + SUM_B, the last edit sets 27, and 47 is the only report that says the last edit is the one still standing (MEASURED on Windows: five edits, ONE extra generation, which is the debounce doing its job, and `dev5_final_value = 47`). - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: THE CAP-10C3 HANDOFF — `ui = pas2js` is refused with `dev_ui_unsupported` (exit 3) and nothing is started or written. Everything in `docs/dev-contract.md` except section 5 is frontend-agnostic and is what CAP-10C3 inherits: the layout, the generation, the publish-by-rename, the forward-only poller, the acknowledgement line, the one stop flag and both ladders, the exit mapping, and the whole of `pweb.cli.devlayout` and `pweb.webview.devhost`. What CAP-10C3 has to decide is the COMPLETION SIGNAL: the Pas2JS compiler has no `writeBundle`, and the CAP-10C1 static assembly (`PWebCliAssemblePas2jsDist`) runs in the CLI rather than in the build, so there is no third party to write a sentinel. The two candidates are a CLI-owned sentinel written after the assembly step (which makes the CLI both the watcher and the signaller, and needs a watch mechanism the CLI does not have) and a compiler-driven one; neither is ratified here, and the shard that ratifies one owns the measurement.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.dev.pas` pdvOpen refusal; `docs/dev-contract.md` section 12.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: FOUND IN REVIEW AND FIXED (a reload dispatch could outlive the webview it names) — the ratified seam is `PWebHostRequestReload`, which reads the live handle out of `HostAutoCloseHandle` and `webview_dispatch`es a navigate onto it. The auto-close thread does the same thing and is safe for a reason that does NOT transfer: the teardown JOINS the closer before it disowns the handle and destroys the view. The dev poller is not a thread `pweb.webview.host` owns — it is started by `PWebDevHostRun` BEFORE `PWebHostRun` and joined AFTER it returns — so a poller that read a non-nil handle one instruction before `InterlockedExchange(HostAutoCloseHandle, nil)` could dispatch onto a view the teardown had already destroyed. Narrow, and real: it needs a generation published in the instant a stop begins, which is precisely DEV6's "Ctrl+C while a rebuild or pack is in flight". The fix is a DRAIN rather than a join, because the caller is a composition's thread and not this unit's: `PWebHostRequestReload` raises an interlocked busy count before it reads the handle and lowers it after the dispatch returns, and the teardown waits for that count to reach zero — bounded by `PWEB_HOST_RELOAD_DRAIN_MS`, placed immediately after the disown and before the first teardown step, so the CAP-9 shutdown order below it is reached unchanged and the CAP-10C0 contract gate's order check is untouched. A dispatch that never drains marks the view unsafe to destroy and fails the run rather than destroying it underneath a caller. In production nothing calls the function and the count is zero on the first look.
  evidence: `src/webview/pweb.webview.host.pas` `HostReloadBusy`, `PWebHostRequestReload` and the drain before `binding.Close`; `test/cap10c0/check_cap10c0_contracts.ps1` section 6, whose five-step order is unchanged.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a SECOND `pweb dev` on the same project could not publish) — a session numbers its generations from 1 and publishes each by ONE rename that MUST NOT REPLACE, and start-up reclaimed `.gen.tmp` and nothing else. MEASURED on the dev host once the gate began sampling a live second run rather than killing it as soon as one line appeared: `pweb: generation 1 publish failed: dev_publish_failed`, then `first_generation: FAILED`, and no window. Numbering from the highest surviving generation instead would be worse rather than better — the dev host is told to open `gen-1`, so a leftover `gen-1` is not a stale generation of this session, it is a DIFFERENT session's content under the name this one promises to write. `PWebCliDevEnsureLayout` now reclaims every published generation, by ENUMERATING `<dev>` and removing only entries whose name is exactly `gen-` followed by at least one digit: the cost is what is on the disk rather than a walk to the generation ceiling, `gen-x` and `gen-` survive because they are not generation names, and `app/`, `units/` and `obj/` cannot be matched by the shape at all. The count is recorded on the layout so a report can say the disk was reused rather than leaving it to be inferred from a counter that restarted at 1.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.devlayout.pas` `PWebCliDevResetGenerations` and the `pdlGenerationReclaim` refusal; `test/cap10c2/pweb.test.dev.pas` `AStartReclaimsAPreviousSessionsGenerations`; `build/cap10c2/dev14-stderr.txt` of the run that showed it.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: RATIFIED AND CORRECTED (a development compile is INCREMENTAL) — the first draft built the dev vector as the release vector plus `-dPWEB_DEV`, which meant it also inherited `-B` and rebuilt the whole mORMot surface on every `pweb dev` start. The ratified spec says the opposite in as many words — "native dev compile: always (bounded, cached units under `<output>`)" — and the cost is real twice over: minutes before a window can open, and three of them per CI job now that DEV7 and DEV8 are two further supervised runs. `-B` is therefore pushed for a RELEASE only. The reason it is safe here and load-bearing there: a release is required to be a function of its sources alone and `build_deterministic` measures that over two real runs, while a development compile writes into `<dev>/units` and `<dev>/obj` that no release build ever reads, and the define never varies inside them — it is always `-dPWEB_DEV` — so there is no mixed-define staleness for FPC's dependency check to miss. The suite case that pinned "release plus exactly one element" now pins the two-way delta instead, and the CAP-10C2 CI step's bound moved from 45 to 70 minutes for the three supervised runs.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.native.pas` `BuildFpcCommand` (`if not Dev then Push(args, '-B')`); `test/cap10c2/pweb.test.dev.pas` `DevCommandIsTheReleaseVectorInDevMode`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` the four CAP-10C2 gate steps.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: FIXED IN PASSING (`test/cap10b1/build_cap10b1.ps1` refused to run on a host whose FPC default is not win64) — the script guarded on `fpc -iTO`/`-iTP`, the compiler's DEFAULT target, while its CLI compile named `-Px86_64 -Twin64` explicitly and its BUNDLER compile named nothing at all. On a Windows dev host carrying both compilers the default is regularly win32/i386, so the script threw before doing anything and the CAP-10C2 work could not rebuild the CLI it had just changed. CAP-10C1 had already ratified the rule for exactly this reason ("the compiler's DEFAULT target is checked only where it decides anything"; `build_cap10c1.ps1` checks the SELECTED target): the guard now asks `fpc -Px86_64 -Twin64 -iTO`, and the bundler compile names its target like every other Windows compile in this repository. On the CI runner the default IS win64, so nothing this script produces there changes.
  evidence: `test/cap10b1/build_cap10b1.ps1` the target guard and the bundler compile; the CAP-10C1 ledger entry that ratified the rule.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: FOUND IN REVIEW AND CLOSED (two PASS criteria had no measurement at all) — acceptance 8 requires that no member of the supervised set holds a listener and that no loose asset is served, and the aggregator's own stated refusals name both; neither had a row. `dev_listener_members_max` now samples the LIVE set through the CAP-10C1 membership sampler, dot-sourced rather than reimplemented, over BOTH members by the pid the loop prints for each — which is why the watcher's pid is now printed in the same shape the host's already was. On POSIX each is the head of its own process group, so the scope is exact; on Windows each is the root of its own descendant closure. `dev_listener_members_seen` travels beside it because a sampler that never sampled reports a clean zero for any host. `dev_loose_assets_used` is the two halves together: every published generation holds exactly `app.pwb`, and the directory the development binary runs from carries the binary and the engine library and NO bundle — which is DEV9's claim stated as a layout rather than as a refusal. `dev_loop_model`, `cli_dev_available`, `dev_interrupt_clean` and `dev_interrupt_mechanism` were added in the same pass; `dev_interrupt_clean` is the row CAP-10C1 could only record as `not_measured` on Windows, and it is ONE row rather than four so a leg that half-held cannot read as a pass.
  evidence: `test/cap10c2/run_cap10c2_gates.ps1` the live-set sampling in section 7 and the loose-asset block; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.dev.pas` `DevWatcherStarted`; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` absolute pins; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` c69-c71.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a DEV14 leg that only passed while the thing it measured was broken) — the second-run leg polls `pweb dev`'s redirected stderr for its `install:` line, and it polled with `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText` inside a `try/catch`. On Windows `Start-Process -RedirectStandardError` holds that file, so every read throws a sharing violation for as long as the process lives and the text stays empty. The leg passed anyway — because until the generation reclaim landed, the second run CRASHED at its first publish, the handle was released, and the very last poll finally read the file. Fixing the product therefore turned a green leg red, which is the honest direction: with the run healthy the reads threw forever, `dev14_second_install_line` read `unmeasured` and the live listener sample saw no member at all. The gate now reads live logs through a `FileShare.ReadWrite` stream. The general rule this keeps: a poll over a file another process is still writing is not `ReadAllText`, and a leg whose evidence arrives only when its subject dies is a leg that measures the crash.
  evidence: `test/cap10c2/run_cap10c2_gates.ps1` `ReadLive`; the v7 local run, where `dev14_second_install_line` was `unmeasured` and `dev_listener_members_seen` was 0 against a `dev14-stderr.txt` that plainly carried `install: skipped`, `generation 1 ready` and both pids.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED ON THE FIRST HOSTED RUN AND FIXED (the closed-shard gates that still pinned `dev` as unknown, and the local blind spot that hid them) — hosted run 33705234187 failed on all four targets at one step with one message: `--help advertises an unimplemented command: dev`, from `test/cap10b0/run_cap10b0_gates.ps1`. CAP-10C0's ledger describes exactly this ritual — "three closed-shard gates that pinned `run` as an unknown command now pin `dev` and `build` only" — and this shard had moved the CAP-10A copy and the two CONTRACT checks while leaving three GATE copies behind: B0's `--help` list, B1's `--help` list, and B1's `unknown_command` refusal loop. All three now name `build` alone. A fourth was hiding behind them: `test/cap10b1/run_cap10b1_gates.ps1` names the generated file set in full, and the template supersession had moved CAP-10B2's copy of that literal and not this one.
  evidence: hosted run 33705234187, the `CAP-10B0 scaffold gates + evidence` step on all four targets; `test/cap10b0/run_cap10b0_gates.ps1`, `test/cap10b1/run_cap10b1_gates.ps1` the `--help` list, the refusal loop and `$expected`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: THE REASON THOSE FOUR REACHED A HOSTED RUNNER, and the sweep that closes it — every CAP-10 build script guarded on `fpc -iTO`/`-iTP`, the compiler's DEFAULT target, while naming `-Px86_64 -Twin64` in the compiles that matter. On a Windows dev host carrying both compilers the default is regularly win32/i386, so `build_cap10a`, `build_cap10b0`, `build_cap10b1`, `build_cap10b2` and `build_cap10c0` all threw before doing anything and NONE of their gates could be run locally — which is precisely why four stale pins were discovered by a hosted runner instead of by the machine that wrote them. Worse, the failure was silent in the wrong direction: `build_cap10b0` threw, the gate that follows it compared the pack it had NOT rebuilt, and reported "the pack is not deterministic" — a frightening message for a stale input. All five now ask `fpc -Px86_64 -Twin64 -iTO`, CAP-10B1's bundler compile and CAP-10A's isolation compile name their target like every other Windows compile in this repository, and the whole CAP-10 chain — B0 build, B0, B1, B2, 10A, C0, C1, C2 — runs on this host. On the CI runner the default IS win64, so nothing any of them produces there changes. The general rule: a guard that refuses to run is a gate that cannot be run, and a build script that names its target must not ask about a default it never uses.
  evidence: `test/cap10a/build_cap10a.ps1`, `test/cap10b0/build_cap10b0.ps1`, `test/cap10b1/build_cap10b1.ps1`, `test/cap10b2/build_cap10b2.ps1`, `test/cap10c0/build_cap10c0.ps1`; the local chain run of 2026-09-03 (B0 build, B0, B1, B2, 10A, C0 all PASS).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: TEMPLATE SUPERSESSION, THE SECOND HALF (the PWEB_DEV region belongs in BOTH templates) — the first draft put the development region in the React `src/program.lpr` alone, and `test/cap10b2/check_cap10b2_contracts.ps1` refused it on all four targets with the invariant it exists to hold: "src/program.lpr differs between the templates in CODE, not only in comments -- the generated native host must not branch on frontend kind". The invariant is right and the change was wrong. `PWebDevHostRun` takes an options record, a policy and a bridge and knows nothing about React; the development composition is frontend-AGNOSTIC, so shipping it to one template and not the other IS the generated host branching on frontend kind. Both `src/program.lpr` files now carry the identical region, they differ only in the four-line data-path comment the invariant permits, and CAP-10C3 inherits a generated Pas2JS program that already selects the development host — it has to implement the LOOP, not the composition. `test/cap10b2/run_cap10b2_gates.ps1` reduces BOTH sides to production form before comparing (it reduced React alone, which is why it passed while only React carried the region) and now requires the region on both. The Pas2JS inventory digests moved with it, in the same commit, as the supersession rule requires.
  evidence: hosted run 33730264368, `CAP-10B2 contract cross-checks` on linux-x86_64; `tools/templates/{react,pas2js}/src/program.lpr`, byte-identical outside the data-path comment; `test/cap10b2/run_cap10b2_gates.ps1` `ProductionForm` applied to both, and `pas2js_dev_region_present`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: THE VERIFICATION LESSON THIS SHARD PAID FOR TWICE — two hosted runs were spent on failures a local run would have caught, and both times the reason was the same: a SUBSET of the local gates was run and reported as "the regressions". The first spent run failed on four closed-shard pins because five build scripts refused to start on this host and their gates were therefore never executed; the second failed on a contract check (`check_cap10b2_contracts.ps1`) that was simply not in the list of six that had been run. The rule this repository now has, and the one that closes both: run EVERY `test/**/check_*.ps1` and the whole CAP-10 chain in CI ORDER (B0 build, B0, B1 build, B1, B2, 10A, C0, C1 build, C1, C2 build, C2) before a push, and read the ones that fail rather than the ones that pass. Measured after the fix: all eight CAP-10 contract checks, `check_dev_trust`, `check_divergence` and every gate in the chain PASS on this host; `test/cap3u/check_unwind.ps1` and `test/cap4w/check_cap4w.ps1` fail for environment reasons that predate this shard (mandatory parameters CI supplies, and an MSVC probe compile), and the two `cap7f` scripts need the four-target evidence tree that only the aggregate job has.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED, WITH RATIFICATION (the two WebKit adapters cached the privileged bundle, so a generation switch changed nothing the user could see) — the spec recorded this as "a measured risk, stated before it is run": only the WebView2 adapter sends `Cache-Control: no-store`, the WebKitGTK and Cocoa handlers sent none, and whether a re-navigation re-requests its sub-resources is an engine property. It is now MEASURED, on WebKitGTK, with the development host and three prepared archives and NO CLI involved: without the header the handler is asked for `assets/app.js` EXACTLY ONCE - at the first document load - and every later re-navigation re-runs the page against the PREVIOUS bundle's JavaScript. The archive changed, the host acknowledged the switch, the page re-executed, and it computed the old answer. With the header the store is asked on every navigation and the page tracks the archive exactly: 42 -> 47 -> 42 across three generations, which is the discriminating sequence, because a stale reader answers 42 -> 42 -> 42 and a reader that merely "reloads once" answers 42 -> 47 -> 47. The platform adapters are frozen surface and the spec's own boundaries mark this Ask First; the human ratified the change. It is NOT a development affordance: `app.pwb` is a replaceable, privileged bundle and an engine cache is not something this runtime can invalidate, which is why the Windows adapter has sent the header since CAP-4W. `check_cap10c2_contracts.ps1` section 9 now pins all three adapters in source, in their three languages, so the parity cannot drift back.
  evidence: `src/platform/linux/pweb.platform.webkitgtk.pas` and `src/platform/macos/pweb_cocoa_bridge.mm` (the header and the measurement in its comment); `src/platform/windows/pweb.platform.webview2.pas` `BuildHeaders`; `test/cap10c2/check_cap10c2_contracts.ps1` section 9; the WSL reproduction of 2026-09-03 and the `TRACE store asked for [assets/app.js] gen=N` instrumentation that showed one call before and three after.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: A MEASUREMENT ERROR WORTH KEEPING (three conclusions drawn from a stale binary) — while chasing the above, `Cache-Control: no-store` was tried once and pronounced ineffective, then WEBKIT_CACHE_MODEL_DOCUMENT_VIEWER likewise, then a trace was added to the store's `TryRead` and reported as never called. All three were wrong, and for one reason: the reproduction script copied its host binary from `<output>/<os>-<arch>/dev/app`, which is what the LAST CLI RUN produced, rather than from `build/cap10c2/probe/dev-host`, which is what `build_cap10c2.sh` had just compiled. Every rebuild was real and every run used the binary from before it. What caught it was the trace's own absence: the string was present in the probe binary and absent from the one being executed, which is a question worth asking of any instrumentation that reports nothing - is the instrument in the thing under test? The general rule: a reproduction must name the artifact the build just produced, and "I rebuilt" is not evidence that the thing that ran is the thing that was built.
  evidence: `grep -c 'TRACE store asked'` over `build/cap10c2/probe/dev-host/demo` (1) and over the dev layout's `app/demo` (0), on the same tree, at the same moment.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (a required field whose ratified value was the empty string) — `csp_transport_terms` records which banned transport terms the CSP carries, and its correct value is "none of them". It was emitted as `''` and listed among the aggregator's REQUIRED fields, which require every value to be non-empty precisely so a target that silently failed to emit a row is told apart from one that measured nothing. So the aggregate refused on all four targets at once with `REQUIRED FIELD MISSING/EMPTY: field=csp_transport_terms`, and - worse than the failure itself - that refusal displaced the branch the negative self-test legs were asserting, so `cap9c2-digest` reported "refused, but not through the expected branch" and the real cause was two removes away. The row now says `none`, the convention this repository already uses for `network_stages_pas2js`. The general rule: a field that means "nothing found" must SAY nothing found, because absence and emptiness are the two things a required-field check exists to separate.
  evidence: hosted run 33744615598, the `cap7 aggregate` job; `test/cap10c2/run_cap10c2_gates.ps1` T1; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` absolute pins.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c2-react-dev-loop.md`
  summary: FOUND BY RUNNING THE AGGREGATE LOCALLY, AND FIXED (two holes the four native jobs could never show) — the aggregate job is the only place the cross-target aggregator and its negative self-test run, so a shard that changes their inputs cannot verify them from a native job. Downloading the four `cap7f-evidence-*` artifacts of a finished run and running both scripts against them locally closes that, and it immediately found two defects this shard had introduced or inherited. (1) `cap10c1-private-surface` (leg c51) perturbed `pipeline_units_linked` to `true` to prove the aggregator refused a pipeline linked into the shipped CLI - but CAP-10C2 INVERTED that pin, so the mutation was no longer a mutation and the aggregator correctly exited 0 where the leg demanded a refusal. A shard that inverts a pin owns its negative leg too; the leg now perturbs to `false`. (2) `dev_corpus`, `dev_suite` and `dev_option_matrix` were absolute pins and REQUIRED fields but not must-PASS fields, so a target reporting `dev_corpus=FAIL` was refused by the pin and not by the verdict rule the `cap10c2-corpus` leg names. Both are now in the must-PASS list, where a verdict belongs. The committed self-test reaches 160 aggregator refusals + 2 divergence refusals (136 + 2 before this shard).
  evidence: `gh run download <run> -n cap7f-evidence-<target>` into `ev/` plus `cap7m2-release-*` into `inv/`, then `check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` and `check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` with absolute roots; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` c51 and c55; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` the must-PASS list.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: RATIFIED AT CHECKPOINT 1 (the change detector is a POLL, and its fingerprint is CONTENT) — pas2js has no watch mode and no `writeBundle`, so the CLI owns detection, and two decisions were taken rather than assumed. (a) POLLING over native APIs: `ReadDirectoryChangesW`, `inotify` and `FSEvents`/`kqueue` would be three bodies in `pweb.cli.platform`, three semantics (buffer overflow, watch-descriptor exhaustion, the FSEvents/kqueue split) and three ways for one loop to behave differently on four targets - for an input set of five files. `test/cap10c3/check_cap10c3_contracts.ps1` section 3 sweeps `src/` and `tools/` for those identifiers as CODE, so the decision cannot be quietly reopened. (b) CONTENT over `(path, size, mtime)`, which was the recommended fingerprint and is the wrong one: FPC's portable timestamp layer is SECOND-granular, so two edits inside one second that leave the length alone are one fingerprint and the change is never seen - the adversarial question "can the detector miss a change" answered by "it can". Hashing the bytes costs a few kilobytes of reads four times a second, is immune to that AND to a same-size edit, and buys a property worth having on its own: a `touch`, a `git checkout` or a mode change that alters no byte costs no generation. Both halves are asserted by the headless suite (`devprint|same_bytes_new_timestamp|unchanged`, `devprint|same_size_edit|changed`) rather than argued for.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.devinputs.pas` header and `PWebCliDevInputScan`; `docs/dev-contract.md` section 5b; `test/cap10c3/pweb.test.devpas2js.pas` `FingerprintIsContentNotTimestamp` and `ASameSizeEditMovesTheFingerprint`; `build/cap10c3/devpas2js-corpus.txt`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE THE FIRST PUSH (a source that does not compile was rebuilt forever) — the first working loop answered a broken `const` by recompiling the whole frontend every few seconds for as long as it stayed broken, printing the same `compile failed` line each time. React has no equivalent because Vite's `writeBundle` does not fire on a failed build, so a broken source produces no new sentinel at all; the fingerprint, by contrast, stays changed. The fix is that a failed attempt RECORDS THE INPUT STATE IT ANSWERED - and it is `before`, the fingerprint taken ahead of the compile, never a fresh one taken after it: a fix that landed DURING the failed build must still look like a change on the next pass, and it does exactly because the recorded value predates it. MEASURED on windows-x86_64 both ways: three failure lines in fifteen seconds before, exactly one after, and the fix still published on the next edit. The gate pins the count in a range (1..3) rather than at 1, because a debounce boundary can legitimately answer one input state twice and a range still refuses a poll loop.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.dev.pas` `MakePas2jsGeneration` (`seenSentinel := before`) and the loop's failure arm; `test/cap10c3/run_cap10c3_gates.ps1` `pd5_compile_failures`; the local runs of 2026-09-03.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: RECORDED (the input-set walk has its OWN bounds, and does not borrow the pipeline's) — `PWebCliPipeTreeLines` already walks a tree, digests content and bounds depth and file count, and reusing it was the obvious move. It is the wrong shape for this caller in three ways, and each is a bound rather than an opinion: its per-file ceiling is `PWEB_CLI_PIPE_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (256 MB), which is right for a mutation gate that runs once per stage and wrong for a walk that repeats four times a second; its tree bound is 4096 entries, which a PD10 leg would have to write 4097 files to reach; and it RECORDS a link as a line where this caller must REFUSE one, because following a link inside `<frontend>/src` would let the compiler read outside the project root. So `pweb.cli.devinputs` has its own walk with `PWEB_CLI_DEV_MAX_INPUT_FILES` (512), `_MAX_INPUT_DEPTH` (16), `_INPUT_FILE_MAX` (4 MiB) and `_INPUT_PATH_MAX` (512), and a link is `dev_input_link`.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.devinputs.pas` `ProjectTree` / `ProjectFile`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolchain.pas` CAP-10C3 block; `docs/dev-contract.md` sections 5b and 9.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: RECORDED (a Pas2JS session supervises ONE long-lived member, and that is not a second ladder) — CAP-10C2 froze "two long-lived children, one ladder", and pas2js has no watcher child to supervise. The watcher slot is simply empty: the stop flag, `DevStopCheck`, the CAP-10C0 graceful-then-forced ladder, the join, the drain and the exit categories are the same code reached with one member instead of two, and the detector is a loop on the main thread rather than a process - so a Pas2JS session has nothing to leave behind. The exit mapping is unchanged; "the watcher exited" simply has no watcher to fire. `docs/dev-contract.md` section 7 gained a paragraph and no mechanism.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.dev.pas` stage 7 and its `finally` block, whose `watcherStarted` guard already existed; `docs/dev-contract.md` section 7; `pd12_descendants_remaining=0` on every leg.

It is the same aliasing `pweb.cli.devlayout` documents at `PWebCliDevResetGenerations` ("a LOCAL out-parameter: `Result` is the const argument, and handing the same variable's field as the out one would alias them"), met a second time in a test. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: SUPERSESSION, AND THE CLOSED-SHARD LEG THAT DID NOT GO RED BUT HUNG (`dev11_*` inverted) — CAP-10C2's DEV11 leg ran `pweb dev` on a scaffolded pas2js project and required exit 3 with `dev_ui_unsupported`, pinning `dev11_exit=3`, `dev11_cause=dev_ui_unsupported` and `dev11_nothing_written=true`. CAP-10C3 implements that loop, so the claim is false - and the failure mode is worse than a red row: `RunCli` uses `Start-Process -Wait`, and what used to refuse in milliseconds now starts a real development session, so the leg HANGS with a live host and a live supervisor. MEASURED on the dev host: eighteen minutes, no output past the DEV9 leg, and the gate never reached its own verdict. On a hosted runner it would have consumed the job's whole timeout to say one thing. The three rows are INVERTED into one, `dev11_pas2js_supported=true`, measured from the public surface rather than by running the command to completion; the CAP-7F negative leg is inverted with them; and what DEV11 used to prove - that the refusal PATH exists - is now pinned in SOURCE by `check_cap10c3_contracts.ps1` (the `PWebCliDevUiSupported` predicate, the `dev_ui_unsupported` literal and the one call site) and exercised as a RULE by the CAP-10C3 headless suite, which reaches the branch with an unratified UI ordinal. The general lesson, and it is the CAP-10C2 lesson met from the other side: a closed shard's gate that asserts a REFUSAL will not merely fail when a later shard implements the thing - if the refusal was fast and the implementation is a long-lived loop, it will hang, and only running the whole regression set locally finds that before a runner does.
  evidence: `test/cap10c2/run_cap10c2_gates.ps1` section 6; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` (`dev11_pas2js_supported` in the required list and the absolute pins); `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` leg c75; the local run of 2026-09-03 that hung at DEV11.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED ON THE FIRST HOSTED RUN AND FIXED (the gate never put the pinned pas2js on PATH, and the local harness hid it) — hosted run 33787727548 failed the Linux leg with fifteen PD rows false at once, and the driver's own forwarded lines said why in three: `pweb: toolchain: FAILED tool_not_found frontend.pas2js`. The loop resolves tools on PATH by ratified design, and every CI job fetches the pinned compiler into `deps/` WITHOUT putting it there - which is why `test/cap10b2` and `test/cap10c1` each carry an explicit block that prepends `deps/pas2js/bin`, `deps/pas2js-linux/bin` and `deps/pas2js-darwin/bin`. The CAP-10C3 gate had no such block. It passed locally on every run because the LOCAL invocation set PATH in its wrapper before calling the gate - a harness more generous than the one under test, which is the CAP-10C2 stale-binary lesson wearing different clothes: what ran locally was not what CI would run, and the difference was in the harness rather than in the artifact. The fix is the same block the two sibling gates carry, plus a REFUSAL at the top when no pas2js can be found at all, plus a `pas2js_on_path` evidence row pinned true on four targets with its own negative self-test leg - so the next time this happens the matrix says one thing instead of fifteen. The gate was then re-run locally with NO PATH help at all, which is the only run that proves the block works.
  evidence: hosted run 33787727548, `CAP-10C3 pas2js development-loop gates` on linux-x86_64, and its `p2jdrv-report.txt.lines`; `test/cap10c3/run_cap10c3_gates.ps1` pinned-pas2js block; `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` lines 72-84, the block it was copied from; the CAP-7F self-test leg C3-20.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: MEASURED ON THE SECOND HOSTED RUN AND FIXED (a parity leg measured on a generation the loop is entitled to delete) — hosted run 33790870889 was green on all four targets for every PD, RD, T and CL row EXCEPT PD14, which failed on linux-x86_64, macos-x86_64 and macos-arm64 with "generation 1 is absent". The loop was blameless and the leg was wrong: the driven session publishes six generations, and `PWebCliDevCleanGenerations` removes `gen-M` for `M <= N - PWEB_CLI_DEV_KEEP_GENERATIONS` the moment generation 6 is acknowledged - so gen-1, gen-2 and gen-3 are gone by the time the gate looks. It passed on windows-x86_64 for a reason that makes it worse rather than better: the running host still had the archive open, Windows refuses to unlink an open file, the bounded cleanup quietly failed and gen-1 survived. A claim that depends on the platform's unlink semantics is not a claim. PD14 now measures on the ONE-GENERATION run the gate already performs for the listener sample - the dev directory is reclaimed, `pweb dev` reaches generation 1, the set is sampled and stopped, and the archive is compared then. Cleanup cannot have run at N=1, on any platform. The general rule: never measure a property of an artifact the system under test is entitled to garbage-collect; arrange for a run in which it cannot have been.
  evidence: hosted run 33790870889, `CAP-10C3 pas2js development-loop gates` on the three POSIX targets, and their `p2jdrv-report.txt.lines` (six generations, six acknowledged); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.devlayout.pas` `PWebCliDevCleanGenerations`; `test/cap10c3/run_cap10c3_gates.ps1` section 8.

What the POSIX legs measured, live: six generations published and six acknowledged, `42` on generation 1 and `47` after the burst, the host pid unchanged across every switch, a compile error answered ONCE with the previous generation live, the consistency rule firing five times under a moving input set and then recovering, exit 0 on interrupt and 5 on an externally killed host with zero descendants either way, zero listeners, zero network calls, and the two typed input-set refusals. The per-target normalisation row is the CAP-10B2 finding restated by the dev loop: `bom=true cr=true` on Windows, `bom=true cr=false` on Linux and macOS.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10c3-pas2js-dev-loop.md`
  summary: INFRASTRUCTURE, NOT DRIFT (the pinned FPC installer step timed out on a Windows runner) — the CAP-10C3 closure commit's own run, 33799537793, went red on `windows` at step 7, `Install FPC (pinned Lazarus 3.4 installer, sha256-verified)`, with `The action has timed out after 20 minutes`. It is a DOCS-ONLY commit whose parent was green on all six jobs, and the step runs before any source of this repository is compiled - so nothing it measures could have changed. `gh run rerun --failed` was green, and the run closed 6/6. This is the second flavour of pinned-artifact flake this repository has met: the CAP-6b ledger records the WebView2 Evergreen Standalone fetch failing on the network, and the remedy there is the same one - re-run the job, never re-ratify the lock. The distinguishing question in both cases is whether the failing step runs BEFORE the thing under test exists; when it does, a red job is a fact about the runner. Recorded so the next person meeting a 20-minute install timeout on a green tree does not go looking for a regression.
  evidence: run 33799537793, `windows` job 100795305136 step 7, and the same run green after `gh run rerun --failed`; the CAP-6b ledger entry on the WebView2 fetch flake.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: RATIFIED AT CHECKPOINT 1 (the release-replacement window cannot be zero, and the contract says so instead of pretending) — the CAP-10D0 brief required that "at no instant is there neither an old nor a new `release/`". That is unreachable on all three filesystems, and `docs/pipeline-contract.md` section 8 already ratified the opposite at CAP-10C1. Windows has no atomic directory swap and `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING` does not apply to a populated directory; POSIX `rename(2)` replaces a directory only when the destination is EMPTY and answers `ENOTEMPTY` otherwise; Linux's `renameat2(RENAME_EXCHANGE)` would do it and is Linux-only, which would make one rule three; and an indirection through a junction or symlink at `release` is refused by the CAP-10C0 resolver itself, which fails a reparse point anywhere on the layout chain — so a swap-by-link produces a layout `pweb run` will not accept. The human ratified the C1 sequence as-is. What CAP-10D0 promises instead is stronger where it matters and honest where it cannot be: never a PARTIAL or a MIXED layout, and exactly one bounded instant between two renames in which there is no layout at all, which `pweb run` answers `not_built`. The evidence field the brief named `build_never_without_release` is emitted as the two fields that are true: `build_never_partial_release` and `build_replacement_window = one_rename_no_release`.
  evidence: `docs/build-contract.md` section 3 and its failure table; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` `PWebCliRenameDir` at 1085 (Windows, no `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`) and 2333 (POSIX, `lstat` then `rename`); `test/cap10d0/pweb.test.build.pas` `TTestPWebBuildFs`, five cases over the production `PWebCliAssembleRelease`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: MEASURED, AND DIFFERENT ON THE TWO FAMILIES (a build racing a running application) — `pweb run` launches the application with the release directory as its working directory, so what a concurrent `pweb build` can do to that directory is a platform fact rather than a design choice. On POSIX a directory that is a process's current directory renames freely and the running process keeps its inode: the build replaces the layout underneath a live application, the old application runs to completion, and a run started afterwards gets the new one. On Windows a directory that is any process's current directory cannot be renamed at all, so the retiring rename of step 2b fails, the build is refused with `layout_reclaim_failed` (exit 6) and the running application is untouched. Neither can produce a partial layout, which is the property the rule exists for — but they are DIFFERENT, so the corpus carries a typed `build_replace_while_running` per target rather than one claim that would be false somewhere. The driver adds one advisory line on that refusal naming the likely cause; no cause code and no exit category moved for it, because an ergonomic hint is not a contract change.
  evidence: `docs/build-contract.md` section 3, "Racing a running application"; `test/cap10d0/run_cap10d0_gates.ps1` section 10 (B6), which asserts the POSIX and the Windows answer separately and requires 42 out of the running application either way.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: C0-12 (b) / C1-11 (a) RESOLVED (one pwsh argument-quoting helper, adopted by every CAP-10 gate at once) — `Start-Process -ArgumentList <array>` joins the array with single spaces and quotes nothing, so a path carrying a space splits into two arguments and `--project` takes half of it. No hosted runner has a space in its workspace, which is exactly why this survived four shards: it is invisible until it is catastrophic. `test/cap10d0/psargs.ps1` implements the C runtime's rules in reverse — the same ones `PWebCliWindowsCommandLine` implements and the CAP-10C0 golden table proves on four targets — and `Start-PWebProcess` wraps `Start-Process` so a call site cannot forget, including the empty-vector case that `-ArgumentList ''` refuses outright. All 24 call sites in `test/cap10a`, `b0`, `b1`, `b2`, `c0`, `c1`, `c2` and `c3` were converted in one commit, `check_cap10d0_contracts.ps1` refuses a CAP-10 gate that calls `Start-Process` directly, and the helper was verified against all fourteen CAP-10C0 golden rows before a single call site moved. L2a makes the proof unforgettable rather than occasional: the CAP-10D0 gate scaffolds both projects inside a directory whose NAME carries a space, so every `--project` on every leg of every job is a spaced path.
  evidence: `test/cap10d0/psargs.ps1`; `test/cap10c0/pweb.test.supervise.pas` `QuotingGoldenTable`, the fourteen rows it was checked against; `check_cap10d0_contracts.ps1` section 5; the `gate_project_path_has_space` and `gate_quoting_space_path` corpus rows.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: C1-11 (b) SPLIT AND HALF-CLOSED (the layout's `plrCommit` refusal is now exercised; the rollback inside it still is not) — CAP-10C1 recorded that the commit's rollback path, which puts the previous release back when the committing rename fails, was a handled partial-failure path no gate exercised. The REFUSAL is now seeded portably and measured on four targets: `test/cap10d0/pweb.test.build.pas` pre-seeds `release` as a FILE, so the rename that must not replace cannot succeed on either family, and the case asserts `layout_commit_failed`, an empty `ReleaseDir`, a reclaimed staging tree and the seeded file byte-identical afterwards. The `hadOld` ROLLBACK inside that refusal is still unexercised, and closing it needs a fault injected BETWEEN the two renames — a fault-injection seam in production code that the CAP-10D0 freeze forbids. Assigned to CAP-10D1 with that reason rather than claimed closed. What IS proven meanwhile is the property a user cares about: a failure at any earlier point leaves the previous release byte-identical, which the same suite measures for a blocked reclaim and an absent input, and which the gate measures for a seeded stage failure and a real interrupt.
  evidence: `test/cap10d0/pweb.test.build.pas` cases `AFileNamedReleaseRefusesTheCommit`, `AFileNamedOldRefusesTheReclaim` and `AMissingInputRefusesBeforeAnythingIsStaged`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.layout.pas` `PWebCliAssembleRelease`, the rollback branch after the committing rename.

It is not: `pweb build` calls `PWebCliRunPipeline`, which calls `PWebCliResolveToolset` exactly once inside stage 2, which is the same call `pweb dev` makes and the same doctor-first resolution CAP-10C1 ratified. The public build therefore adds no toolset caller at all, and the duplication it was supposed to justify collapsing costs exactly what it cost before. Assigned to CAP-10D1, which is the first shard that could plausibly need a resolution without the requirement graph (a profile selection that has to answer before the doctor runs), with the note that if it does not need one either, the entry should be closed as RECORDED-ONLY rather than carried further.

  summary: TEMPLATE SUPERSESSION, RECORDED (both generated READMEs now document the five commands) — the B1/B2 READMEs described creation's next steps as "separate, deliberate steps" and never named the command that takes them, because until CAP-10D0 that command did not exist: `pweb build` was an unknown command and a README that named it would have been a promise the CLI refused on the next line.
