- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: SUPERSESSION, RECORDED (`cli_digest` moves, and nine "build is unknown" pins invert rather than disappear) — the CAP-10A parser corpus records `args|build|ok` where it recorded `args|build|unknown_command`, plus seven option rows the new command brings, so `cli_digest` moves exactly as it moved for `run` at CAP-10C0 and for `dev` at CAP-10C2: from `c4c54b3c...` to `9eb329ae...`, re-pinned in `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` with both previous values in the comment above it. Nine closed-shard gates pinned `build` as an unknown command; every one of them is INVERTED rather than deleted, and the subject of each moves to a name no shard will ratify (`publish`) — because the claim those legs have really been making since CAP-10B0 is that this parser has a CLOSED command set, not that `build` in particular is missing. `build_still_unknown` becomes `build_available` and `build_still_unknown_c3` becomes `build_available_c3`, each with its CAP-7F negative self-test leg inverted with it; `advertised_commands` gains `build` in the C0, C1, C2 and C3 rows; and `dev_build_unknown` reads `none`, which is the surface matrix's own verdict over a command name that is genuinely unknown.
  evidence: `test/cap10a/pweb.test.cli.pas` C3 block; `test/cap10c1/run_cap10c1_gates.ps1` `$cliClosure`; the nine inverted pins in `test/cap10b0`, `b1`, `b2`, `c0`, `c1`, `c2`, `c3`; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` legs c74 and C3-14.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: MEASURED DURING LOCAL VERIFICATION AND FIXED (a seeded failure that did not fail, and the two legs it poisoned) — the B4 leg seeds a broken frontend source and requires the build to stop with exit 5 and the previous release byte-untouched. The first version APPENDED its garbage to `frontend/src/app.pas`, after the unit's final `end.` — where the compiler ignores it. So the "failing" build SUCCEEDED, published a release built from a source the page could not run, and the failure surfaced two legs later as "B6: the application never reported ready inside the arming window", which is a diagnosis nobody would connect to a seed. The seed is now PREPENDED, where no compiler tolerates it. The general rule, which is the CAP-10C2 measurement-error lesson in a new costume: a seed must be VERIFIED to have seeded — a leg that asserts a failure must fail for the reason it names, and a green neighbour is not evidence that it did.
  evidence: the local WSL run of `test/cap10d0/run_cap10d0_gates.ps1` before the fix (B4 exit 0, B6 no ready) and after; `test/cap10d0/run_cap10d0_gates.ps1` section 8.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: METHOD, RECORDED (a POSIX gate run needs a WSL-NATIVE working tree, not `/mnt/c`) — the standing rule in this repository is to validate the Linux leg under WSL before pushing, and CAP-10D0 could not do it in place: `pweb create` refuses on a DrvFs mount with `mode: .gitattributes`, because the creation transaction verifies the POSIX file mode it just set and DrvFs does not keep one by default. The whole CAP-10D0 chain was therefore built and run from a `rsync`ed copy at `$HOME/mtron-d0` — 215 MB excluding `build/` and `.git/`, plus `build/cap7l/webview-dist` copied across because the pinned webview library is a build artifact the C1 staging needs, and `tools/get-pas2js.ps1` re-run there because the pinned Linux Pas2JS lives in `deps/pas2js-linux` while a Windows working tree carries only `deps/pas2js`. That run is what found the two gate defects above; without it they would have been two hosted runs. Recorded so the next shard does not rediscover the DrvFs refusal from the inside.
  evidence: `pweb create` answering `mode: .gitattributes` under `/mnt/c`; the same command succeeding at `$HOME/mtron-d0`; `pas2js.lock` `linux-url`, and the system Pas2JS 2.1.1 that a bare `pas2js` on PATH resolves to.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: RECORDED (three CAP-7F field-name collisions, and the namespacing rule they force) — the CAP-10D0 evidence set proposed `build_deterministic`, `project_tree_unchanged` and `template_supersession_recorded`, and all three already existed in the platform matrix, emitted by CAP-10C1 about the private pipeline. The first two were caught while reading the aggregator; the third was caught by the PowerShell parser refusing a duplicate key in a hash literal, which is a better outcome than the alternative — a silently overwritten field that would have made two shards' claims one. They are namespaced `d0_build_deterministic`, `d0_project_tree_unchanged` and `d0_template_supersession_recorded`. The rule for the next shard: the matrix is one flat namespace across every shard that has ever emitted into it, so a new field name must be checked against `emit_evidence.ps1` before it is chosen, and a shard-prefixed name is the cheap answer whenever the unprefixed one is a claim somebody else already makes.
  evidence: `test/cap7f/emit_evidence.ps1` lines 1356, 1370 and 1400 (the CAP-10C1 originals) against the CAP-10D0 block; the duplicate-key parse error that found the third.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: THE CAP-10D1 HANDOFF — CAP-10D0 exposes `pweb build` and closes the CAP-10 command surface at five: create, doctor, run, dev, build. What CAP-10D1 inherits verbatim and may not renegotiate: the CAP-10C1 ten stages, their bounds and the Pas2JS assembly; the CAP-10C0 run layout, its resolver and its exit mapping; the release layout per platform; the ratified project-mutation set; the supervision engine and its one ladder; the whole of `docs/dev-contract.md`; and now the CAP-10D0 build grammar, its release-replacement rule and its six-field summary, all of `docs/build-contract.md`. What CAP-10D1 owns: the CAP-13 normal/offline/fixed profiles and how a build selects one, what a distributable Linux artifact is beyond the directory the pipeline commits, and the macOS bundle identity and signing posture. It also inherits three ledger items by name — C1-11 (a), the toolset collapse whose premise CAP-10D0 measured false; C1-11 (b), the `hadOld` rollback that needs a fault-injection seam; and C1-11 (f), the line-buffering setup — plus whatever the Windows long-path measurement below leaves open. It must not touch the dev loop, the dev host, the change detector, the seam, `PWEB_NATIVE_CSP`, the privileged origin, schema 1, the seven interfaces, the adapters, the build grammar or any pin.
  evidence: `docs/build-contract.md`; `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap10d0-final-artifact.md` section 4.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: MEASURED ON A HOSTED RUN AND FIXED (a host-shaped value in a corpus four targets must agree on) — the CAP-10D0 suite recorded `commit|fresh|ok|<N>` where N is the number of files a committed release holds, and a release holds THREE on Windows and Linux and FOUR on macOS, because a macOS bundle carries an `Info.plist` the flat layouts do not. So `build_digest` came out `96f650da…` on windows-x86_64 and linux-x86_64 and `04cfa320…` on both macOS targets — a corpus recording the host that ran it rather than the rule it decided, which is CAP-10C1's ledger entry C1-16 in a new costume. The count moved to `build-observed.txt`, where per-target values belong, and the corpus keeps the decision alone; both families now produce `1d5230a9…`. THE MEASUREMENT ONLY EXISTED BECAUSE THE FOUR ARTIFACTS WERE READ: the aggregate passed the run in which the digests differed, because the CAP-10D0 fields had been added to the aggregator's REQUIRED list (present and non-empty) and not to its four-target EQUALITY list — two lists in one file, and the one that matters is the second. Both are now correct, and the equality list carries a comment naming every D0 field deliberately absent from it and why.
  evidence: run 33845772574 artifacts `cap7f-evidence-{windows,linux,macos-x64,macos-arm64}`, `build_digest` in each; `test/cap10d0/pweb.test.build.pas` `ACommitWithNoPreviousRelease`; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1`, the `$required` list at 50 and the four-target equality list at 897.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: C0-12 (a) MEASURED ON THE HOSTED WINDOWS RUNNER (a project past MAX_PATH refuses with a TYPED cause, and the CLI's own spawn is not what refuses) — the L1 leg creates a Pas2JS project whose root is 233 characters, so `<root>/dist/windows-x86_64/release/demolp.exe` passes MAX_PATH comfortably, and runs the real `pweb build` against it. The measurement: `long_path_exit = 5`, `long_path_cause = stage_exited`, `long_path_rule = typed_refusal_stage_exited`. That is the answer the entry existed to obtain and the opposite of the one it feared: exit 5 is "a stage's child failed and its real status is printed", NOT the untyped `supervision_unavailable` (4) that an unprefixed `lpApplicationName` would have produced. PWeb's spawn reached the tool; the tool answered nonzero. So the `\\?\` change the entry proposed is not what this measurement calls for, and CAP-10D0 makes none. What it adds is the row that names WHICH stage refused, so the next reader knows which tool to take it up with rather than re-deriving it; the disposition is re-ledgered to CAP-10D1 with that name once the row is populated. Recorded rather than closed, because a limitation whose owner is a third-party compiler is not a limitation this repository can close.
  evidence: run 33845772574, `cap10d0-build-windows` artifact, `long_path_*` rows; `test/cap10d0/run_cap10d0_gates.ps1` section 11; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` line 1453, unchanged.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: MEASURED (a spaced-path re-run of a whole gate chain finds harness assumptions, not product defects, and all three it found were mine) — L2b copies the prepared workspace to a path carrying a space and re-runs the CAP-10 chain there. Three hosted iterations, three findings, none of them a defect in `pweb build`: (1) `robocopy /XD .git` excludes every directory of that NAME at any depth, so `deps/mormot2/.git` went with it and `tools/patch-cap3u.ps1` refused with `mORMot checkout not found` — a name-based exclusion cannot say "the top-level one", and the fix is the full path; (2) `check_cap10b0_contracts.ps1` and `check_cap10b1_contracts.ps1` ask `git ls-files` whether the template corpus is tracked, so in a copy that is not a checkout all 35 template files read as untracked — they spawn nothing and exercise no quoting, so they are excluded with that reason rather than made to pass; (3) the CAP-10C0 and CAP-10C1 gates require `build/cap10b{1,2}/{project,stage,release}`, which the two GUI build proofs leave and the build scripts reclaim, so both proofs belong IN the chain rather than beside it. The general lesson: a leg that re-runs a chain in a relocated tree is measuring the chain's assumptions about its own location, and every one of them has to be made explicit before it measures the thing it was written for.
  evidence: runs 33829174302, 33834503015 and 33845772574, the `CAP-10D0 L2b` step of the `windows` job in each; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the L2b step's scope comment.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d0-public-build-command.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED (twenty negative self-test legs landed inside the function they call) — the CAP-10D0 legs were appended to `check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` with a `.Replace()` on an anchor that occurs TWICE in that file: once as the first statement of `Invoke-AggExpectFail` and once at the end of the script. Both were replaced, so a copy of all twenty legs became the body of the function each of them calls, and the self-test died with `The script failed due to call depth overflow` rather than with anything about a leg. Every other edit script in this shard checked its match count and refused an ambiguous anchor; this one did not, which is the whole of the defect. The legs are now in one place and all twenty-one fire: verified locally against the four evidence artifacts of run 33845772574, 201 aggregator refusals against a floor of 200. The rule worth keeping: when an edit is applied by string replacement, the count of matches is part of the edit, and an anchor that matches twice is not an anchor.
  evidence: run 33845772574, `cap7 aggregate` job, `CAP-7F negative self-test`; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_selftest.ps1` `Invoke-AggExpectFail`; the local self-test run over the downloaded `ev/` and `inv/` artifacts.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: C1-11 (a) CLOSED AS RECORDED-ONLY, ON THE SECOND MEASUREMENT ITS DISPOSITION ASKED FOR (the toolset's shadowed branches are on no path that needs collapsing, in this shard either) - CAP-10D0 sent this to CAP-10D1 with an explicit condition: CAP-10D1 is "the first shard that could plausibly need a resolution without the requirement graph (a profile selection that has to answer before the doctor runs)", and if it did not need one either, the entry should close as RECORDED-ONLY rather than be carried further. It DOES need one: `pweb build --profile` runs a packaging PREFLIGHT before the pipeline, precisely so a developer whose machine lacks a 212 MB pinned installer learns it in one second rather than after a fifteen-minute build. And that preflight resolves exactly ONE executable - the Inno Setup compiler, plus the cabinet expander for the fixed profile - through `PWebCliResolveTool` and `PWebCliRunProbe`, the CAP-10A primitives that already sit BELOW `PWebCliResolveToolset`. So the requirement-graph-free resolution the entry predicted exists, it is served by a primitive that was already there, and the toolset duplication it proposed collapsing costs exactly what it cost before. Measured rather than argued: `check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` refuses a `pweb.cli.package` that names `PWebCliResolveToolset` at all, and the row `pack_resolves_toolset` reads false on four targets.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` (`PWebCliPackPreflight`); `test/cap10d1/check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` section 7; `build/cap10d1/contracts.json` `pack_resolves_toolset`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: C1-11 (b) CLOSED - THE hadOld ROLLBACK IS EXERCISED, AND THE PREVIOUS RELEASE COMES BACK BYTE-IDENTICAL - CAP-10C1 recorded the rollback as a handled partial-failure path no gate exercised; CAP-10D0 seeded the `plrCommit` REFUSAL portably (a `release` pre-seeded as a FILE) but could not reach the rollback INSIDE it, because that refusal happens with `hadOld` False and reaching the branch needs a fault injected BETWEEN the two renames - a production seam the D0 freeze forbade. CAP-10D1 adds that seam and closes the item. `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.layout.pas` declares `PWebCliLayoutFailCommit` inside `{$ifdef PWEB_LAYOUT_FAULTS}`, so a production build has no such variable AT ALL rather than one defaulting to False; the D1 suite arms it over a real fixture that already holds a committed release, requires `plrCommit` with `Detail = release`, and then requires the previous release BACK IN PLACE, byte-identical by the pipeline's own `<rel>|<size>|<sha256>` projection, with neither `.pweb-release.tmp` nor `.pweb-old.tmp` surviving. Exactly one compile command in this repository passes the define, and the contract check sweeps every other build command, every CI step and every tool script for the spelling - with the needle concatenated so the sweep cannot satisfy itself.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.layout.pas` (the guarded seam and the commit branch); `test/cap10d1/pweb.test.pack.pas` `TheHadOldRollbackPutsThePreviousReleaseBack`; the corpus rows `rollback|previous_release_restored|true` and `rollback|byte_identical|true`; `check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` section 5.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: C1-11 (f) CLOSED AS NOT NEEDED, WITH THE REASON TURNED FROM AN OBSERVATION INTO A MEASUREMENT (the per-call-site Flush) - CAP-10D0 closed the question with an observation: `RunBuild` writes through `pweb.pas`'s `Emit`/`EmitErr`, both of which flush, so a line-buffering setup at program start would be an ergonomics change with no failing property. CAP-10D1 adds a second command path and the same observation would have had to be re-made by hand, so it is a GATE instead: `check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` requires that no unit under `tools/pweb` writes to the console at all except `pweb.pas` (with `pwebtemplates.pas` excluded by name - the build-time pack builder is a separate program never linked into the CLI, which `check_cap10b0_contracts.ps1` already measures), and that `pweb.pas` itself has exactly TWO write sites and TWO flushes. A line-buffering setup remains a change with no failing property; what is new is that a gate now fails the day one appears.
  evidence: `test/cap10d1/check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` section 7 (`console_writers`, `console_write_sites`, `console_flush_sites`); `tools/pweb/pweb.pas` `Emit`/`EmitErr`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: THE WINDOWS LONG PATH: THE CLI NO LONGER LETS THE PINNED COMPILER BE THE THING THAT FAILS - CAP-10D0 measured a Pas2JS project rooted at 233 characters on the hosted Windows runner and recorded `long_path_exit = 5`, `long_path_cause = stage_exited`, `long_path_stage = build`: PWeb's own spawn reached the tool and the PINNED COMPILER answered nonzero. The owner of that limitation is a third-party compiler this repository cannot fix, and CAP-10D0 re-ledgered it on that name. CAP-10D1 cannot fix it either; what it does is stop the compiler being the thing a developer hears from. `pweb.cli.pipeline` refuses at the `open` stage - BEFORE the read-only tree is digested, before anything is written and before any child is spawned - with `project_root_too_long` and exit 3, on `build` and on `dev` alike, when the canonical project root exceeds `PWEB_CLI_PIPE_MAX_ROOT_CHARS` (the ninth entry that bounds table has ever had). The bound is MEASURED rather than chosen: `test/cap10d1/windows_legs.ps1` builds one Pas2JS project at three root lengths on the hosted runner, records the largest that builds (`long_path_ok_chars`) and the smallest that fails (`long_path_fail_chars`), and FAILS unless the pinned value lies strictly between them - so the number cannot decay into one somebody typed. Typed `not_applicable` on the three POSIX targets, which have no MAX_PATH, and expressed as ordinary runtime code over the target rather than as a platform conditional.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolchain.pas` `PWEB_CLI_PIPE_MAX_ROOT_CHARS`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.pipeline.pas` (the `open` refusal); `test/cap10d1/windows_legs.ps1` section L1; the rows `long_path_bound_chars`, `long_path_ok_chars`, `long_path_fail_chars`, `long_path_refusal`, `long_path_refusal_cause`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE IT COULD SHIP: `dist/<profile>/` WOULD HAVE LANDED INSIDE THE Pas2JS ASSEMBLY DIRECTORY - the CAP-10D1 brief specifies artifacts under `<output>/<os>-<arch>/dist/<profile>/`, and the first implementation used exactly that. The gate's own first real run on Linux caught it: `<output>/<target>/dist` is ALREADY owned by the frozen CAP-10C1 pipeline, which puts the Pas2JS static assembly there (`PWEB_FE_DIST`, `pweb.cli.pipeline.pas` line 711), so a packaging artifact of that name sits inside the frontend staging tree and the next build's assembly is free to replace it. The frozen layout does not move, so the NAME moved: artifacts land under `<output>/<os>-<arch>/artifacts/<profile>/`, which collides with nothing the pipeline owns. Recorded rather than quietly renamed, because the brief's stated layout and the shipped one differ and the reason is a measurement.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.packpins.pas` `PWEB_PACK_ARTIFACTS`; `docs/distribution-contract.md` section 6; the gate rows `d0_no_artifacts_without_profile` and the artifact path in the eight-field summary.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: THE PROFILE NAME IS `fixed-runtime`, NOT `fixed`, AND THAT IS A RATIFICATION RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE - the CAP-10D1 brief writes `--profile normal|offline|fixed`. The canonical name is `fixed-runtime`: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-pweb/deployment.md` names it, `tools/setup/pwebappsetup.issi` validates `|normal|offline|fixed-runtime|` CASE-SENSITIVELY, that exact string is the value written to a user's machine as the HKCU profile marker, and `test/cap6b4/check_cap6b4_contracts.ps1` pins it. A CLI spelling of `fixed` would put two names on one mode and make the command's vocabulary disagree with the machine's own record - which is the defect the CAP-6b4 marker gate exists to forbid. So `--profile fixed` is refused exactly like `--profile msix`, and the gate measures the refusal (`profile_fixed_shorthand_refused`).
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` `PWEB_PACK_FIXED`; `test/cap10d1/pweb.test.pack.pas` `TheProfileAllowlistIsCompiledAndByteExact`; `docs/distribution-contract.md` section 1.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: THE CAP-10D2 HANDOFF - CAP-10D1 exposes `pweb build --profile` and closes the CAP-10D surface at one option. What CAP-10D2 inherits verbatim and may not renegotiate: everything CAP-10D0 handed over (the ten stages, their bounds, the run layout and its resolver, the release layout, the mutation set, the supervision engine and its one ladder, `docs/dev-contract.md`, `docs/build-contract.md`), PLUS the whole of `docs/distribution-contract.md`: the four profile names, the identity derivation, the pinned offline inputs and their two refusals, the deterministic archive, the macOS posture and the `<output>/<target>/artifacts/<profile>/` layout with its CAP-6b4 index. What CAP-10D2 owns: SDK packaging - and it inherits ONE ledger item by name, C1-11 (c), the manifest re-emitter's escape forms, which CAP-10D0 assigned to it because SDK packaging owns the distribution manifest. It must not touch the dev loop, the dev host, the change detector, the seam, `PWEB_NATIVE_CSP`, the privileged origin, schema 1, the seven interfaces, the adapters, the build grammar, the packaging identity rules or any pin.
  evidence: `docs/distribution-contract.md`; `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap10d1-final-artifact.md`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: KNOWN LIMITATION, STATED: THE OFFLINE AND FIXED-RUNTIME PROFILES ARE PROVEN AS BUILDS, NOT AS INSTALLS, FOR A GENERATED APPLICATION - the hosted Windows leg installs, launches, proves 42 from and uninstalls the NORMAL profile of a generated project, and builds the other two with their payloads inspected and their indexes cross-checked. The runtime behaviour of the other two - the offline provisioning gate and the fixed-runtime ACL/Authenticode verdict gate - is proven by CAP-6b2 and CAP-6b3 over the IDENTICAL `[Code]` bodies, and CAP-10D1's contract check requires those bodies to stay byte-identical to their CAP-13 twins on every leg. What is therefore NOT measured for a generated application is the interaction of those gates with an identity derived from `pweb.json` - and the reason it is not is cost: three full install matrices would re-prove CAP-6b2 and CAP-6b3 at roughly an hour of hosted Windows time for no new claim. A shard that wants that claim should say so and pay for it.
  evidence: `test/cap10d1/windows_legs.ps1` (W1 installs only the normal profile); `test/cap10d1/check_cap10d1_contracts.ps1` section 3 (the two byte-identical `[Code]` twins); `test/cap6b2` and `test/cap6b3` gates.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: KNOWN LIMITATION, STATED: THE ARCHIVE'S BYTE-DETERMINISM IS A PER-TARGET CLAIM, NOT A CROSS-TARGET ONE - two builds of the same release on ONE machine produce a byte-identical `.tar.gz`, and the gate requires it (`archive_deterministic`). Two DIFFERENT targets do not, and cannot: the ustar half is byte-identical by construction, but the gzip half is whatever deflate implementation mORMot bound on that host (libdeflate where available, zlib otherwise), and the released executable inside the archive is a different binary on every target anyway. The archive's PAYLOAD is compared instead - inventory and per-file digests against the committed release - which is the claim that means something. The compressed size is recorded per target as an observation and deliberately never compared.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.tar.pas` (`PWebTarGzip`); `test/cap10d1/pweb.test.pack.pas` `TwoWritesAreByteIdentical` and its `archive_gzip_bytes` observation; the aggregator's per-target list (`archive_deterministic`).
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE IT SHIPPED: A WINDOWS SYSTEM UTILITY MUST NOT BE RESOLVED BY PATH - the fixed-runtime profile expands its pinned cabinet with `expand`, and the first implementation resolved it through the CAP-10A executable resolver like every other tool this CLI runs. On the dev host that resolved to Git for Windows' GNU coreutils `expand.exe` - the TAB EXPANDER - because its usr/bin precedes System32, and the cabinet tool never ran at all: `expand: unknown option -- F`. The CAP-10A resolver is for TOOLCHAIN tools a developer installs and may legitimately shadow (node, fpc, pas2js); an OS component is not one of them. The fix is a new primitive in the unit that owns every platform conditional, `PWebCliSystemDir`, which asks the kernel via GetSystemDirectoryW and canonicalizes the answer - never %SystemRoot%, never PATH, because both are environment and an environment a build path trusts is an environment somebody can change. It answers False on POSIX, where nothing on the packaging path needs one. Found by running the Windows gate on a real Windows host rather than by spending a hosted run on it.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` `PWebCliSystemDir`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` `ResolveExpand`; the local Windows gate run of 2026-09-04 whose forwarded line read `expand| expand: unknown option -- F`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE IT SHIPPED: THE FIXED PROFILE HAS ITS OWN MAX_PATH CEILING, AND IT IS STRICTER THAN THE PIPELINE'S - the first implementation staged the expanded Fixed Version Runtime under `<output>/<target>/.pweb-pack.tmp/runtime`, and a project at 97 characters - `...\build\cap10d1\spaced work\demopack`, an ordinary developer path - put the cabinet's deepest entry at exactly 260. ISCC is not extended-length aware, so it failed with `the specified path could not be found` AFTER compressing 690 MB, which is the worst possible moment and the least informative possible message. Two changes, both measured: the staging names lost 19 characters (`.pwpack.tmp` and `rt`), and the profile gained its own PREFLIGHT refusal, `pack_root_too_long_for_fixed` (exit 3), computed from PWEB_PACK_FIXED_TREE_MAX_REL - the 119-character deepest entry of the pinned cabinet, measured over its 256 files. The pin is re-measured on every Windows leg against the tree CAP-6b3 already expanded in the same job, so it stays a property of Microsoft's cabinet rather than a number somebody typed. This is the CAP-10D0 long-path lesson arriving a second time in a different place: the answer is always a typed refusal before the child, never a third-party tool's own failure afterwards.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.packpins.pas` (`PWEB_PACK_STAGE`, `PWEB_PACK_RUNTIME`, `PWEB_PACK_FIXED_TREE_MAX_REL`, `PWEB_PACK_MAX_PATH`); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` `FixedRootBound`; `test/cap10d1/windows_legs.ps1` leg F1 and the rows `fixed_tree_max_rel_measured` / `fixed_tree_max_rel_pinned`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: A PATH THIS CLI BUILDS IS CHECKED LIKE A DESCRIPTOR VALUE (the Inno metacharacter vector the adversarial review found) - every `pweb.json` value that becomes a /D define is re-checked against nine bytes and every control byte before it is passed. The STAGING PATHS were not, and they are not this CLI's to guarantee either: a Windows path may legally contain an Inno constant opener, and a developer controls where the project lives, so `{#PWEB_PAYLOAD_DIR}` expanded into a [Files] Source would have put a CONSTANT where a directory name belongs. `PWebCliPackStagingPathAcceptable` closes it: the separator and the drive colon are allowed because a path needs both, and the four bytes an Inno directive reads as syntax are not. It is checked twice - on the project ROOT in the preflight, and on the paths actually handed to the compiler at the point of use - and REFUSES rather than escaping, for the reason every other identity check refuses: a path that quietly became a different path is an installer that embeds something else.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` `PWebCliPackStagingPathAcceptable`; `test/cap10d1/pweb.test.pack.pas` `AStagingPathMetacharacterIsRefused`; the corpus row `identity|staging_path_refused|brace,quote,semicolon,control`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: RATIFYING AN OPTION IS A SUPERSESSION IN TWO PLACES, AND THE FIRST HOSTED RUN FOUND THE ONE THAT WAS MISSED - CAP-10D0 froze `--profile` twice over: once as a name its parser must not define (the contract cross-check's unratified list) and once as an invocation its public surface must refuse (gate leg B9's option matrix, which runs the real executable and reads `unknown_option` back). The implementation moved the first and not the second, so three of four hosted legs failed with `pweb build --profile offline answered 2/profile_not_for_target: archive -- expected 2/unknown_option`. The lesson is not "update both lists": it is that a ratified option must not simply LEAVE the matrix, because a shorter matrix measures less than the one before it. B9 now holds `--profile` to the same grammar every other build option obeys - bare is `missing_value`, empty is `empty_value`, and `run --profile` is `option_not_for_command` - so what the leg still measures is "the surface refuses everything it has not ratified" rather than "the surface got one option shorter". Whether the named profile can be built HERE has its own typed cause and belongs to the D1 gate.
  evidence: `test/cap10d0/run_cap10d0_gates.ps1` leg B9 and its supersession comment; `test/cap10d0/check_cap10d0_contracts.ps1` line 179; hosted run 33866065185, the linux-x64 and both macOS jobs, step "CAP-10D0 public-build gates".
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: A TEST DRIVER THAT REWRITES A SOURCE FILE TWENTY-FIVE TIMES A SECOND IS RACING THE COMPILER, NOT MEASURING THE LOOP - CAP-10C3's PD7 leg rewrites the ratified input set continuously so a build starts while writes are still arriving, and its whole-file replacement is a delete followed by a CREATE_NEW. On Windows that races PAS2JS reading the very file being rewritten: a third-party reader holding the source without FILE_SHARE_DELETE makes the delete fail, and a delete that DID succeed against such a handle leaves the name delete-pending, where CREATE_NEW answers access-denied until the last handle closes. Neither is a fact about `pweb dev`, so a driver reporting it as `move_write_failed` was reporting a compiler's file handle as a defect in the development loop. The write now retries for a bounded two seconds - small enough that a file this driver genuinely cannot write still fails the leg, larger than a compiler's read of one source. Nothing in the product changed, and nothing needed to: the platform read primitive that could have been implicated is not in this path at all.
  evidence: `test/cap10c3/pwebp2jdrv.pas` `WriteWhole` and the constants `DRIVER_WRITE_RETRY_MS` / `DRIVER_WRITE_RETRY_GAP_MS`; hosted run 33866065185, the windows job, step "CAP-10C3 pas2js development-loop gates", which failed `move_write_failed` with `pd6_burst_edits 0` after the same scenario had run green on every previous Windows run.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: OBSERVED AGAIN, WIDER AND WITH ONE NEW MEASUREMENT - the intermittent `FAIL: page/runtime verdict was not successful (state=0; 0=no report received)` ledgered at CAP-10B1 against the CAP-5 PAS2JS smoke appeared here TWICE at a different step, `CAP-6 release runtime smoke - app.pwb over pweb://app`. Two sightings at two unrelated smokes say the shared thing is the bounded wait for the page's report on a hosted Windows runner, not either example, which the earlier entry could not yet distinguish. THE NEW MEASUREMENT is the host's own stdout: on the runner the release binary printed exactly ONE line, the FAIL, so the page reported NOTHING inside the 8000 ms `PWEB_SMOKE_AUTOCLOSE_MS` budget - it is not a wrong report, a partial report or a late report but an absent one. The same commit's same binary on a Windows DEV HOST prints the whole report and passes, and every attempt ran on the identical runner image `win25-vs2026/20260824.214`, including the attempt that passed forty minutes earlier - so this is timing on a shared runner, not an image change and not a release-layout defect. CAP-10D1 wrote nothing under `src/` or `examples/`, which is the entire surface this smoke exercises, so it cannot be this shard's regression. The disposition stays the ratified one for a hosted flake - re-run the job, never re-ratify - and the owed next step is still the instrumentation the earlier entry named: make a non-report say whether the page never loaded, never ran, or ran and missed its window. Raising the constant would only move the boundary, and this shard's mutation set does not reach `test/cap6/` in any case.
  evidence: hosted run 33870028829 on `0450d53e`: the windows job failed at step 53 on two separate attempts (12:00Z, 12:58Z) with an empty `build/cap6/smoke-release.log` apart from the FAIL line, while job 101001098494 of run 33866065185 passed that same step at 11:46Z on the same image; the local Windows run of `test/cap6/run_cap6_smoke.ps1` at the same commit answered `PASS - release layout booted the UI from app.pwb and returned 42`. The SAME hosted job also lost twenty minutes to a stalled fetch of the pinned Lazarus/FPC installer (step 7, killed by its own 20-minute budget, then 51 s on the next attempt) - the network-flake class the WebView2 Evergreen fetch already occupies.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: OBSERVED, NOT DIAGNOSED (a one-off residue race in CAP-6b4's fixed-profile uninstall on a hosted Windows runner) - leg U3 uninstalls the ~690 MB fixed-runtime profile and then requires the install directory itself to be gone. On one attempt it was not: nine files survived, and every one named in the failure is a browser binary - `dxcompiler.dll`, `dxil.dll`, `ffmpeg.dll`, `mip_core_gn.dll`, `msedge_elf.dll` - together with the three runtime directories holding them. That is the shape of a `msedgewebview2.exe` image that had not finished exiting when the uninstaller reached its files, not of an uninstaller that forgot them: CAP-6b3's own drain reported `sweeps=2 graceful=True terminated=0` for the bundled browser minutes earlier in the same job, so the drain exists and works, and the question is whether U3 waits for it on a contended runner. The identical step passed on the next attempt of the same commit on the same image. What is NOT established is a cause, and the honest next step is to have U3 report the drain it observed before it measures the directory, so a residue failure can say whether a process was still holding the files. CAP-10D1 wrote no CAP-13 file - `tools/setup/app/` is a new tree that no CAP-6b gate reads - so this is not this shard's regression.
  evidence: hosted run 33870028829 on `0450d53e`, the windows job's third attempt, step 80 `CAP-6b4 profile matrix`: `U3: the install directory itself survived the uninstall (C:\Users\runneradmin\AppData\Local\Programs\PWebRelease): 9 file(s) [dxcompiler.dll, dxil.dll, ffmpeg.dll, mip_core_gn.dll, msedge_elf.dll], 3 subdirectory(ies)`, reached after I1/I3/F1/F2/F3/F3b/S1/S3/S4/S5/S6/U2 had all passed in the same run; the fourth attempt passed the same step. `test/cap6b4/run_profile_matrix.ps1:504`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d1-distributable-artifacts.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND FIXED BEFORE IT SHIPPED: THE AGGREGATE WAS COMPARING EXACTLY THE WRONG FORTY-FOUR FIELDS - CAP-7F keeps two lists that read almost identically and mean opposite things: the fields REQUIRED PRESENT on every target, and the fields COMPARED value-for-value across four. The CAP-10D1 block was written once and pasted into both, so the aggregate ended up demanding that Linux agree with Windows about `windows_install_exit`, `windows_profile_marker`, `fixed_tree_max_rel_measured` and forty-one more - 124 disagreements on four GREEN targets, every one of them the aggregate asking a machine to have installed a setup it cannot run. The second half of the defect is the one that matters more and would have shipped silently: because that block was the ONLY CAP-10D1 entry in the equality list, the thirty-one fields that genuinely must agree on four machines - `pack_digest`, the five-caller set, `pack_code_twins`, `signing_identity_used`, `secrets_read`, the foreign-profile refusal, the rollback seam, the interrupt - were compared on NO target at all. A green aggregate would have proved nothing about them. Both halves are now written from a MEASUREMENT rather than from intent: the thirty-one were confirmed equal across the four real evidence files of a hosted run before being added, and the three that are per-target by design (`profiles_for_target`, `archive_deterministic`, `pack_interrupt_stage`) are named in the deliberately-absent note beside them.
  evidence: `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1` (`\`, the CAP-10D1 entry and the deliberately-absent note that replaced the mis-pasted block); hosted run 33870028829, the `cap7 aggregate` job, step 10, whose log carries all 124 `FIELD DISAGREES` lines; the negative self-test leg `cap10d1-digest-diverged`, which then caught the second half by mutating `pack_digest` on one target and watching the aggregator exit 0 where it must fail.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE PINNED Pas2JS ARCHIVE CONTAINS NO `COPYING.FPC`, AND THAT IS WHY THE SDK SHIPS NO COMPILER - the CAP-10D2 brief recommends shipping the Pas2JS compiler under the SDK root and resolving it from there. Two reasons refuse it, and the second is a measurement rather than a preference. FIRST, the SDK ships PWeb's framework, its two frontend SDKs, its bundler and its dependencies' sources; it ships NO compiler, and FPC and Node are host requirements with `pweb doctor` rows exactly as Pas2JS is. Bundling one of the three and not the other two would make the rule "whichever one we felt like carrying". SECOND, and decisively: the pinned Pas2JS 3.0.1 release archive carries no licence text at all. Its own `packages/rtl/README.txt` says "See the file COPYING.FPC, included in this distribution" and that file is not in the archive; `tools/get-pas2js.ps1` moves the whole archive root into `deps/`, so nothing is being filtered out. Checkpoint 1's own rule - no component whose licence is not ratified - therefore refuses it, because this repository has no offline, pinned, verifiable licence text to ship beside the binary. `docs/third-party-licenses.md` is corrected on the same measurement: it used to claim the texts ship inside the archive. The disposition for a later shard is stated rather than implied - ship Pas2JS the day an offline licence text can be pinned by digest from a reviewed source, and not before.
  evidence: `deps/pas2js/` after `tools/get-pas2js.ps1` (no COPYING*, no LICENSE*, at any depth); `deps/pas2js/packages/rtl/README.txt`; `tools/get-pas2js.ps1` lines 160-170 (`Move-Item -Path $Root -Destination $Target`); `docs/sdk-contract.md` section 2 and the corrected Pas2JS section of `docs/third-party-licenses.md`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE LOCK FILES SHIP AS DIGESTS, NEVER AS FILES, AND THE FROZEN CONTRACT IS WHAT DECIDED IT - the CAP-10D2 brief lists `locks (*.lock)` as "ship as metadata, read by doctor and preflight". `docs/distribution-contract.md` section 3, which the same brief inherits VERBATIM, says the opposite in as many words: the locks carry the vendors' download addresses and "are never shipped into an SDK", and `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.packpins.pas` exists precisely so the digests can travel while the URLs stay behind. A conflict between the brief and a frozen contract is resolved for the contract, and the intent behind the brief's line is served anyway: the manifest records each lock's own sha256, so a package still states which lock revision produced it, with no address on anybody's build path. The gate measures the property rather than the promise - `sdk_lock_files_shipped` reads 0 from the manifest's own inventory on four targets.
  evidence: `docs/distribution-contract.md` section 3; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.packpins.pas` header; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.sdkmanifest.pas` `PWEB_SDK_LOCKS`; `test/cap10d2/run_cap10d2_gates.ps1` rows `sdk_lock_files_shipped` and `sdk_locks_count`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: ONE ARCHIVE RULE ON FOUR TARGETS - `tar.gz` ON WINDOWS TOO, AND THE REASON IS CAP-10D1'S OWN MEASUREMENT - the brief specifies "tar.gz on POSIX, zip on Windows". CAP-10D1 already measured what a second writer costs when it refused a supervised `tar(1)`: GNU has `--sort=name` and macOS's bsdtar does not, so one rule would have become three. The same arithmetic applies here and one term is worse: BOTH ZIP writers in this repository - mORMot's `TZipWrite` behind the CAP-6 bundler and `PWebTplWritePack` behind the template pack - set `extFileAttr` to the MS-DOS attribute plane and carry no Unix mode at all, so a Windows-written ZIP would be a second archive format with a determinism claim nobody had measured at 38 MB. `tar.exe` has been in Windows' System32 since 10 1803 and Windows 11's Explorer opens `.tar.gz`, so the cost of the single rule is a familiarity one and the benefit is one writer, one determinism claim and one inventory comparison across four targets.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.tar.pas` header (the two measured refusals); `tools/pweb/pwebsdk.pas` `ARCHIVE_STEM`; `docs/sdk-contract.md` section 6; the gate rows `sdk_archive_deterministic` and `sdk_archive_sha256` on four targets.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE INTEGRITY MODEL'S BOUND, STATED PLAINLY RATHER THAN IMPLIED - the manifest is an INVENTORY and it is trusted as one. It catches a half-copied SDK, a truncated download, an extraction that lost a file and any shipped byte that was altered, because verification is FULL - every declared file's length and digest, on every doctor run and before every build. It does NOT catch a manifest that was rewritten to describe the altered bytes, and it does not notice its own absence: an SDK root with no manifest is an UNPACKAGED root, which is exactly what this repository's own staged build tree is, and the three doctor rows say `not_applicable` there rather than inventing a verdict. Compiling the manifest's digest into `pweb` was CONSIDERED and REFUSED: it would pin one binary to one package and make a re-packaged SDK unusable with the executable inside it. The anchor that remains is the COMPILED CAP-10B0 registry, which stays the authority for the template pack, and the packager refuses to ship a pack whose digest is not the registry's, so a package's two anchors agree by construction. One further bound: an EXTRA file inside a shipped directory is not detected, because the repository's own staged root legitimately carries one (`bin/pwebpipe`) and a rule that refused it would refuse every development root. Closing the whole class needs a signature over the manifest and a key nobody has ratified a home for.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.sdkmanifest.pas` header ("WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT IS NOT"); `docs/sdk-contract.md` section 4; `test/cap10d2/pweb.test.sdk.pas` `TTestPWebSdkVerify`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE PACKAGER ASSEMBLES A DECLARED SET RATHER THAN FILTERING A DIRECTORY, AND A TEST DRIVER IS WHY - `build/cap10c1/bin/pwebpipe` is copied into `<sdk>/bin/` by `test/cap10c1/build_cap10c1`, and it has to be: it resolves its SDK root from the running image by the CAP-10B0 anchor rule exactly as `pweb` does, so a driver that took the root as a flag would be proving a different claim. A packager that shipped "whatever is in bin/" would therefore have shipped a private lifecycle driver to every user of the distribution. The alternative shapes were both worse - an exception list is a thing to forget, and refusing an unratified `bin/` entry would make the repository's own root unpackageable - so `pwebsdk` carries SHIP_TABLE, names every component, and takes `bin/` as two NAMED FILES rather than as a tree. Inside a tree it takes whole, an unacceptable file is still a REFUSAL and never a skip: a tree that quietly grew a file is how an unreviewed byte reaches somebody's installation.
  evidence: `test/cap10c1/build_cap10c1.sh:193` and `build_cap10c1.ps1:209`; `tools/pweb/pwebsdk.pas` SHIP_TABLE and its header; the gate row `sdk_test_drivers_shipped` = 0 on four targets.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE UNREACHABILITY MECHANISM IS SIX RENAMED TREES, NOT THE WHOLE CHECKOUT, AND THE RENAME IS SUFFICIENT RATHER THAN NECESSARY - the brief says "make the checkout unreachable for the duration (rename it aside; on Windows too) - ratify the mechanism". Renaming a hosted runner's own workspace out from under the shell executing the gate is destructive with no safe recovery if one handle is open, and a failure there costs the whole job rather than one leg. What the gate does instead is rename aside, under a `finally` that restores unconditionally, every directory of the checkout an SDK-driven build could resolve a framework input from: `build/cap10b1/sdk`, `src`, `sdk`, `deps/mormot2`, `tools` and `examples`. Each is one reversible filesystem operation, and together they are the whole surface a silently-checkout-resolving SDK would have reached. The claim that they are enough is measured separately and is the stronger half: CM4 requires that NO stage command line names the repository root at all and that every `-Fu`/`-Fl` path lies under the extracted root, which is the property the rename only demonstrates. `deps/pas2js` deliberately stays, because the SDK ships no compiler and a user of the distribution has one on PATH too.
  evidence: `test/cap10d2/run_cap10d2_gates.ps1` (the `$asideNames` list, the `finally` that restores it and the `checkout_restored` row); the rows `checkout_renamed_aside`, `checkout_path_in_argv` = 0 and `unit_paths_under_sdk` = true on four targets.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE SDK IS VERIFIED TWICE PER BUILD, AND THAT IS THE PRICE OF KEEPING THE DOCTOR A PURE FUNCTION - `pweb build` asks `PWebCliSdkPreflight` at the `open` stage, and stage 2's `PWebCliResolveToolset` then runs the CAP-10A requirement graph over `PWebCliRealEnv`, which measures the same fact again for the `sdk.integrity` row. Both are full verifications and each costs 57 ms on 288 files. The alternative - passing the first measurement into the second - would mean either a global, or an extra parameter through `PWebCliResolveToolset` into `PWebCliDoctorRun`, or a doctor that measures its own environment instead of receiving it; the third is the one the CAP-10A design exists to prevent. 114 ms on a build that takes tens of seconds is not a cost worth buying with any of those, and two independent measurements of one fact that must agree is a property rather than a defect. Recorded so a later reader does not "discover" the duplication and remove the wrong half.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.pipeline.pas` (the `open` stage's preflight); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolset.pas` `PWebCliSdkPreflight` and `PWebCliResolveToolset`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.doctor.pas` `PWebCliRealEnv`; the gate row `sdk_integrity_seconds`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: PWeb'S OWN LICENCE IS NOT DECLARED ANYWHERE IN THIS REPOSITORY, SO THE DISTRIBUTION SHIPS THIRD-PARTY NOTICES ONLY - there is no `LICENSE` file at the repository root, no licence header convention across `src/`, and no licence field in any manifest this product owns except `sdk/typescript/package.json`, which declares MIT for one package that is additionally marked `private`. `docs/third-party-licenses.md` is titled, and is, a THIRD-PARTY document. So `share/pweb/licenses/` carries the four third-party notices and nothing of PWeb's own, and inventing one would be exactly the silent derivation `docs/cli-contract.md` refuses when it refuses to default `--bundle-id`. This is a real gap in a distributable product and it is the owner's call, not a shard's: somebody has to choose the terms. Stated as a known limitation of the CAP-10D2 distribution rather than resolved by guessing.
  evidence: no `LICENSE*` at the repository root; `sdk/typescript/package.json` (`"license": "MIT"`, `"private": true`); `docs/third-party-licenses.md` and its new machine-readable shipped table; `docs/sdk-contract.md` section 2.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THREE STATEMENTS IN `docs/distribution-contract.md` HAD DRIFTED FROM THE SHIPPED CODE, AND CAP-10D2 MEASURED THEM RATHER THAN INHERITING THEM - the document was written before CAP-10D1's last two commits and was not brought forward with them. It said the artifact directory is `dist/<profile>/` where `PWEB_PACK_ARTIFACTS` says `artifacts/`; it named the staging siblings `.pweb-pack.tmp` and `.pweb-dist-old.tmp` where `PWEB_PACK_STAGE` and `PWEB_PACK_OLD` say `.pwpack.tmp` and `.pwold.tmp`; and it said the Inno Setup compiler is resolved from the SDK "else PATH" where `pweb.cli.package` has no PATH branch for it at all. All three are documentation corrections - NOTHING in the product moved - and each is recorded in a new section 11 of that document beside the constant that decides it. The lesson is the one a contract document is for: a frozen statement that nobody re-measures is a statement that quietly stops being true, and the CAP-10D2 handoff inherited this document "verbatim" while it disagreed with the code in three places.
  evidence: `docs/distribution-contract.md` section 11; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.packpins.pas` (`PWEB_PACK_ARTIFACTS`, `PWEB_PACK_STAGE`, `PWEB_PACK_OLD`); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.package.pas` `KitStep`/`VerifyIscc` (no PATH branch); `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap10d1-final-artifact.md` section 6, which had it right.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: `ci.yml` IS PAST 253 KB AND FIVE JOBS DEEP, AND CAP-11 OWNS THE SPLIT - the single workflow file carries the four platform jobs plus the release-inventory and aggregate jobs, and every shard since CAP-10A has added four near-identical step blocks to it. CAP-10D2 adds four more (contract check, build, gates, upload) and one artifact upload each, which is where the file passes a quarter of a megabyte. This is not a defect and nothing about it is failing; it is a maintenance budget being spent, and the shard whose whole subject is the CI matrix is the one that should spend it. The BUDGET is stated so CAP-11 inherits a number rather than an impression: 253 KB and 5429 lines at the CAP-10D1 closure, and whatever CAP-10D2's own steps add on top. What a split must preserve is what makes the current file honest - the four platform jobs run the SAME step sequence, and a per-platform workflow that let them drift would remove the property the CAP-7F aggregator exists to check.
  evidence: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (5429 lines / 253208 bytes at `3d8d1b49`); the CAP-10D2 steps added to all four platform jobs; `test/cap7f/check_cap7f_aggregate.ps1`, whose whole premise is that four jobs measure the same things.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: THE CAP-11 HANDOFF - CAP-10D2 closes CAP-10. The `pweb` CLI is five public commands (`create`, `doctor`, `run`, `dev`, `build`), one of them with one option (`--profile`), and the product now has a DISTRIBUTION: one archive per target whose extracted tree is the CAP-10C1 section 2 SDK root, a canonical manifest, three doctor rows and a build that refuses a tampered installation at exit 4 before it writes or spawns anything. What CAP-11 inherits VERBATIM and may not renegotiate: everything CAP-10D0 and CAP-10D1 handed over, plus the whole of `docs/sdk-contract.md` - the shipped set and the pin-only set, the manifest's schema and canonical form, the ratified escape rule, the three integrity rows and their causes, the `open`-stage refusal at exit 4, the tool-location rule with no fallback chain, and the installation model (extract; nothing else is mutated). What CAP-11 OWNS: the CI matrix itself - the split of a 253 KB workflow into per-platform files without letting the four platform jobs drift apart, the artifact retention policy for the four SDK archives this shard now uploads, and the upstream `webview/webview` watcher, whose contract is REPORT AND NEVER RE-PIN: it compiles the binding against upstream head and publishes an API diff, and it must not move `webview.lock`, regenerate a binding or touch a build. The flakes it must INSTRUMENT rather than re-ratify are the three this phase carried: the hosted-Windows `state=0` non-report (make a non-report say whether the page never loaded, never ran, or ran and missed its window), the CAP-6b4 U3 fixed-profile uninstall residue (have U3 report the drain it observed before it measures the directory), and the pinned-installer fetch stalls (Lazarus/FPC and WebView2 Evergreen), which want a bounded retry with its own row rather than a longer timeout. What CAP-11 MUST NOT TOUCH: the seven interfaces, `TInvocationContext`, `ICapabilityPolicy`, the scheduler, the mORMot bridge, the nine-code taxonomy, protocol v1, the SDK wire, `app.pwb`, `plugins.zip`, `pweb.json` schema 1, the CAP-10A parser grammar and exit taxonomy, the CAP-10B0 engine and its compiled registry, the CAP-10C0 supervision engine and layout, the CAP-10C1 ten stages and mutation set, the CAP-10C2/C3 dev host and ladder, the CAP-10D0 build grammar, the CAP-10D1 identity rules and pinned inputs, the CAP-10D2 shipped set and manifest, the CAP-8A policy core, the CAP-8B classifier and CSP, the CAP-9 runtime, every CAP-13 file, the three platform adapters, and every dependency pin.
  evidence: `docs/sdk-contract.md`; `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/cap10-closure-artifact.md` (the CAP-11 handoff section); the three flake entries this phase's ledger carries at CAP-10B1, CAP-10D1 and CAP-10D2.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: MEASURED BY THE CLEAN-MACHINE LEG AND FIXED: THE SDK ROOT COULD NOT BE RESOLVED FROM A NON-ASCII PATH ON WINDOWS - the brief asks for the archive to be extracted "to an unrelated path with a space and a non-ASCII character", and the first run of that leg found a real defect rather than a harness problem. `PWebCliExeDir` took the running image from `Executable.ProgramFilePath`, which mORMot fills from the RTL's `ExpandFileName(ParamStr(0))`, and FPC hands `ParamStr` to a Windows process through an ANSI conversion - so an SDK extracted into `C:\...\<e-acute>tude sdk\` resolved to a path with `?` where the accent was, and EVERY command refused with `sdk_share_missing` while the extracted tree sat there complete and correct. The class is the one this unit already avoids everywhere else: every filesystem call in `pweb.cli.platform` is a W call precisely because a UTF-16 answer cannot lose a character. The fix is one new platform primitive, `PWebCliImageDir`, in both bodies - `GetModuleFileNameW` on Windows, `Executable.ProgramFilePath` on POSIX where a path is bytes and the RTL changes nothing - with the shared `PWebCliExeDir` canonicalizing whatever it returns. It adds no conditional REGION: each body already exists and each gained one function. Measured before and after on the same extracted archive: `sdk_share_missing` on every command, then `sdk.manifest`/`sdk.integrity`/`sdk.version` all `pass`.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.platform.pas` `PWebCliImageDir` (both bodies) and `PWebCliExeDir`; the CAP-10D2 gate rows `clean_machine_path_non_ascii` = true and `clean_machine_doctor`; measured locally on Windows before the fix as `pweb: create failed: sdk_share_missing` from an extracted SDK whose `share/pweb/sdk-manifest.json` was present.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: MEASURED AND NOT FIXED, WITH ITS OWNER NAMED: `pwebbundle` CANNOT PACK A PROJECT AT A NON-ASCII PATH ON WINDOWS - the same ANSI-argv class as the entry above, one layer out and in a program CAP-10D2 may not touch. The frozen CAP-6 bundler reads its two arguments through the RTL, so `pwebbundle "C:\...\<e-acute>tude apps\demo\frontend\dist" ...` reaches its own code as `?tude apps` and it exits 1 with `FAIL: Exception: dist directory not found`. The CLI's half is correct: `PWebCliChildSpawn` builds a UTF-16 command line and CreateProcessW delivers it intact, and every stage before `pack` - `open`, `toolchain`, `stage_sdk`, `install`, `typecheck`, `build` - succeeds on exactly that path. So a `pweb build` of a project whose root carries a non-ASCII character fails at `pack` with `stage_exited 1` and a message about a directory that is plainly there. THE CLEAN-MACHINE LEG PUTS THE NON-ASCII WHERE THE BRIEF ASKS FOR IT - the extracted SDK root - and keeps the PROJECT path spaced, which is what CAP-10D0 and CAP-10D1 ratified for a project; a gate that demanded both would be measuring CAP-6 rather than the SDK distribution. TWO ways to close it, and the choice belongs to whichever shard owns it: make the bundler read a UTF-16 argv (CAP-6 surface, and the honest fix), or refuse a non-ASCII project root at the `open` stage with a typed cause the way CAP-10D1 refuses `project_root_too_long` (a new exit-3 cause, and therefore a supersession of the CAP-10A taxonomy). Doing neither in this shard is a scope decision, not an oversight.
  evidence: measured on Windows with the shipped `bin/pwebbundle.exe` from an extracted SDK: a `dist` tree at `<temp>\<e-acute>tude bundle\dist` exits 1 with `dist directory not found: C:\...\?tude bundle\dist`, and the byte-identical tree at `<temp>\ascii bundle\dist` packs successfully; `test/cap10d2/run_cap10d2_gates.ps1` (the comment beside `$projRoot` that records why the project path is spaced rather than accented). SUPERSEDED - this entry's summary is FROZEN by the closure table's digest `c3483e49` and therefore still reads as the measurement it was, but the thing it describes is FIXED: see the entry sourced from `spec-phase-6-cap6-bundler-utf16-argv.md` below, and the D2-13 row in `cap10-closure-artifact.md`, which now reads RESOLVED. A reader of this entry alone would otherwise carry away a stale claim.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: MEASURED ON A HOSTED macOS RUNNER AND FIXED: AN SDK INSTALLED UNDER A DIRECTORY WHOSE NAME HAS A SPACE COULD NOT LINK - the clean-machine leg extracts the distribution to a path with a space and a non-ASCII character, which is the first time in this repository an SDK ROOT has carried either, and macOS refused the link while Windows and Linux were green. The cause is neither the accent nor macOS: FPC accumulates every `-k` pass-through value into ONE options string and re-splits it on whitespace when it composes the linker command, and `pweb.cli.native` puts TWO SDK paths through `-k` on macOS and only on macOS - `-k-L<lib dir>` and `-k<pweb_cocoa_bridge.o>`. `ld` therefore received `-L/Users/runner/work/_temp/<e-acute>tude` and `sdk/pweb-sdk-.../lib/macos-arm64` as two arguments and reported `search path '...' not found` followed by three `file cannot be open()ed`. The fix applies FPC'S OWN RULE to the paths FPC does not own: `maybequoted` is what the compiler uses for every `-Fl` directory it writes onto the linker line, and `PWebCliLinkPath` does the same - quote iff the value carries a space or a tab. It is CONDITIONAL on purpose, so a path without a space comes back byte-for-byte unchanged and not one argument vector CAP-10C1 pinned moves; the CAP-10C1 suite's four-target command matrix was re-run and is unchanged. THREE LESSONS worth keeping: a defect can be invisible for four shards because no gate had ever put the SDK anywhere but an unspaced build directory; the gate that found it found it only because it printed the CHILD's lines - the pipeline's own `stage_exited 1` says nothing, and the first hosted run of this leg wasted itself proving that; and "installation is extraction, put it anywhere" is a claim a distribution has to EARN on a runner rather than state in a contract.
  evidence: `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.stage.pas` `PWebCliLinkPath`; `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.native.pas` (the two macOS `-k` arguments); `test/cap10d2/pweb.test.sdk.pas` `TTestPWebSdkLinkPath`, which asserts the identity half, the quoted half and the REAL macOS vector from any target; hosted run 33917202306, the macos-arm64 job, step "CAP-10D2 SDK distribution gates", whose log carries `ld: warning: search path '/Users/runner/work/_temp/<e-acute>tude' not found`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-10-cap10d2-sdk-distribution.md`
  summary: A CLEAN-MACHINE DOCTOR ASSERTION MUST BE THE PIPELINE'S RULE, NOT THE DOCTOR'S OVERALL STATUS - the first draft of CM1 required `pweb doctor` on the extracted SDK to report `pass` or `warning`, and both macOS jobs failed it with `platform.webview=fail/framework_absent`. That is not a defect and not a surprise: CAP-10C1 (ledger C1-15) MEASURED hosted macOS runners answering `framework_absent` on machines where the same projects build and run, and closed it by excluding that ONE row from the build verdict by name - `platform.webview` asks whether a machine can DISPLAY a WebView, which is what RUNNING needs and not what compiling needs. A gate that required the overall status to be green was therefore re-imposing exactly the requirement CAP-10C1 removed, one layer out. CM1 now asserts what the PIPELINE asserts: no REQUIRED row other than `platform.webview` may fail. The row itself is still recorded per target in the evidence, so nothing is hidden - and the two macOS jobs go on to build and run the application to 42 with it failing, which is the whole point of C1-15.
  evidence: `test/cap10d2/run_cap10d2_gates.ps1` (the CM1 assertion and the comment naming C1-15); `tools/pweb/pweb.cli.toolset.pas` `FirstRequiredFailure` and its `PWEB_CLI_ROW_WEBVIEW` exclusion; hosted run 33914729625, both macOS jobs, `clean_machine_doctor_react_rows` carrying `platform.webview=fail/framework_absent` beside fifteen passing rows.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6-cap6-bundler-utf16-argv.md`
  summary: RESOLVES D2-13, ON THE ROUTE THAT ENTRY NAMED AS THE HONEST ONE - the frozen CAP-6 bundler now reads its argv from the KERNEL on Windows (GetCommandLineW through CommandLineToArgvW, the same shell32 parse the CLI's own PWebCliRawArgs uses) instead of through the RTL, whose Windows argv is an Ansi conversion of the command line; POSIX keeps ParamStr, because argv there is bytes the RTL hands over unchanged. THE MECHANISM, measured on the dev host at ACP 1252: the accented byte survives the Ansi conversion as $E9 and is then read back as UTF-8 - mORMot's mormot.core.os sets DefaultSystemCodePage to CP_UTF8 for the whole process - so the RTL's own UTF-8 to UTF-16 step yields U+FFFD and the bundler refuses a directory that is plainly there. NOTHING ELSE MOVED: no option, no exit code, no format, and the archive is byte-identical, which the new CAP-6 leg asserts rather than assumes (accented input, accented output, SHA256 equal to the ASCII-path build; the options still parse after an accented positional; --verify reads the same argv; and an absent accented directory is refused with its name spelled back, because a `?` in that message is the defect returning). TWO SUPERSESSIONS the fix forces, both in the same commit: the CAP-7F divergence allowlist row for `tools/bundler/pwebbundle.pas` moves from 6 directives to 8 with a new fingerprint - one new region, two whole platform bodies, deciding nothing - and it therefore stops sharing pwebqjspack.pas's value; and the CAP-10D2 clean-machine REACT PROJECT root carries the accent on WINDOWS, retiring the comment that recorded why it could not. THE ACCENT STOPS WHERE THE EVIDENCE STOPS, and the boundary is itself a measurement: the react chain - node, npm, vite, tsc, the bundler and fpc - was driven end to end at exactly that shape before the gate was widened, and the pas2js chain was not. There is a concrete reason to be careful rather than optimistic about pas2js: the compiler is itself an FPC program and may carry the very RTL argv layer this fix routes around, which nothing has measured; the `--profile` builds run on the pas2js project and add `iscc` to that list. So those keep the spaced ASCII root CAP-10D0 and CAP-10D1 ratified. POSIX keeps the ASCII name for both, deliberately: the defect was the Windows RTL's argv, and macOS normalises the Unicode in a filename, so an accented POSIX root would add an OS-normalisation variable this correction does not own. THE SHAPE IS ASSERTED RATHER THAN IMPLIED - `clean_machine_project_path`, `_has_space` and `_non_ascii` are recorded, the Windows gate REQUIRES the accent by name, and the aggregator carries the three rows - because a proof that lives in an unasserted string literal can be reverted with every gate staying green.
  evidence: `tools/bundler/pwebbundle.pas` (the `{$ifdef OSWINDOWS}` ReadArgs region and the ArgCount/ArgStr pair that keep ParamCount/ParamStr semantics exactly); `test/cap6/run_cap6_gates.ps1` leg 9; `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` (the re-ratified row and the note on the pwebqjspack entry); `test/cap10d2/run_cap10d2_gates.ps1` `$projRoot`; measured before and after on the dev host - the shipped pre-fix binary exits 1 with `dist directory not found: ...\?tude dist` and the fixed one packs the same tree to `08e04e1e…` from an accented path on BOTH Windows and Linux, and a full `pweb build --project "<temp>\<e-acute>tude apps\demoreact"` from an extracted SDK reaches `pack: ok` and `verify: ok`.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-phase-6-cap6-bundler-utf16-argv.md`
  summary: NAMED, NOT FIXED, WITH ITS OWNER AND ITS REACH STATED - `tools/quickjs/pwebqjspack.pas` is the bundler's twin and keeps the argv the bundler just replaced: `ParamStr(1)` is its list file and `ParamStr(2)` its staging root, so on Windows it carries the identical RTL Ansi-conversion class D2-13 measured. IT DOES NOT SHIP IN THE SDK - `pwebsdk.pas`'s ship table names `bin/pwebbundle` and not this - so no USER path reaches it and no `pweb` command spawns it. WHAT DOES REACH IT is the CAP-9C1 gate, and the first reading of this was WRONG in a way worth recording: the gate does not pass repository-relative names, it passes `Join-Path $repoRoot 'build\quickjs-release'`, an ABSOLUTE path - so a CHECKOUT under a directory whose name carries a non-ASCII character would meet the defect on Windows, which is a developer's machine rather than a user's. Closing it is CAP-9C1 surface: the same kernel-argv region, a supersession of that file's own divergence row (still 6 directives, fingerprint `7e329077...`), and a leg that stages to a `[char]0x00E9`-composed directory the way CAP-6 leg 9 does. It was left alone here because the brief scoped this correction to the bundler and because widening it silently would have been the second deliverable in a one-deliverable change - not because it is untrue.
  evidence: `tools/quickjs/pwebqjspack.pas:475-488`; `test/cap9c1/run_quickjsrelease.ps1:157-166` (`$stage1 = Join-Path $repoRoot 'build\quickjs-release'`, then `& $packer examples/07-quickjs/plugins.trusted $stage`); `tools/pweb/pwebsdk.pas:151` for the ship table that does not carry it; `test/cap7f/check_divergence.ps1` (the note on the pwebqjspack row that now says this rather than the weaker version).
